  - Read in `render()` each frame, never in `isReady()`. A drawn frame, a throw, no camera, an invalidation, disposal, a latched preparation failure or unsupported scene (`status.reason`), and a skipped draw that is not a readiness wait (the graph refusing a paused redraw) all end it. `createEngine`'s `dispose` releases a hold it still reports (`onCompileHold(false)`). A loading first frame never raises it: `drawOwner` calls the world draw only once `isReady()`, and the load already pauses.
  - No cap on a link that is still pending: strict readiness has no synchronous way to present, and resuming the game under the picture of a compile that is still running is the outcome the pause exists to prevent. The report ends, with the image still held, when nothing is left to wait for: an effect of the run reports a compilation error (immediately), none of the view's effects was pending and no preparation ran for `COMPILE_HOLD_STALL_MS` (10 s), or `SCENE_DRIVER_COMPILE_TIMEOUT_MS` (60 s) passed since it began (a hung link). That wait then starts no second hold until a frame draws; a failing or hung link would otherwise pause scripts for good while the picture never recovers. A preparation that times out latches a failure, which also ends it.
- Loading budgets ([`stall-deadline.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/stall-deadline.ts)): shader warm stall 4 s, first frame 20 s, driver compile 60 s, and `SCENE_PREPARATION_CEILING_MS` (300 s). They measure stalls, not duration: when one expires, `DriverCompileWatch` ([`driver-compile.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/driver-compile.ts)) renews it for compile progress since its last renewal, which is an Engine effect key that became ready (once per key: a released variant compiled again is none) or a parallel-compile driver's pending set that changed or has been pending for under 60 s. Finished keys are the only sign where links are synchronous (SwiftShader has no `KHR_parallel_shader_compile`, so Babylon's completion check in the Effect constructor already finds each program linked). iPad WebKit WebGL2 renews through the pending set like D3D11: its new effects stay pending until the link completes. Progress is per Engine: anything compiling on the shared Engine renews every wait on it, which is why the editor's thumbnail queue pauses while a blocking scene load (not a rendering update) is open ([asset-registry.md](asset-registry.md#thumbnails)). It is checked only at expiry, so a stuck wait fails between one and two bounds after its last progress; a preparation stuck in an invalidation loop whose first build compiled anything fails up to 60 s later than without progress (one renewal of the total bound). A watch shared by sequential waits (one per thumbnail capture or model instance) starts each wait from fresh progress (`restart`, through `renewOnCompileProgress`). No watch renews from its ceiling, 300 s after its wait began whatever was credited, so a wait that keeps compiling and never becomes ready still ends. The watch renews the shader-warm, material-texture (an RGBD decode, such as the BRDF texture's, waits for its post-process effect to compile), first-frame and model material waits; graph preparation: `SceneRenderCoordinator.prepare` (10 s per generation and 60 s across invalidation restarts, both renewed; the ceiling is checked at every expiry check) and the forward graph's readiness loop (10 s, with its own ceiling), each creating its watch after its first presentation-fence wait, because a watch asks every Engine effect for readiness; and, through `createHeldThreadBound`'s `renew`, the preview, thumbnail, `prewarmMaterial` and Custom GLSL waits below. Their timeout messages end with the watch's counts (variants compiled while waiting, still compiling and on the Engine, and whether the extension is exposed), which the Scene Load Report prints. A validated first draw's GPU completion has no progress signal (the driver compiles the draw's programs inside its submission): its budget is `SCENE_DRIVER_COMPILE_TIMEOUT_MS`, the longest compile the loading budgets tolerate, and compile progress does not renew it.
- Who prepares a view: its loading owner calls `SceneRenderCoordinator.prepare`, and a frame or readiness question that finds the view unprepared asks for a preparation itself (`requestPreparation`). A preparation, once running, restarts for every invalidation until its owner cancels, rebuilding the graph each time. A game's Scene load therefore ends the one a frame had asked for (`supersedeRequestedPreparation`, where create-engine starts a world load; an owner's is never ended by it): a frame of the still-empty world asks for one just before the runtime names its first Scene, and left running it built and released a graph for the load's first commands (the Scene, an Area Light, the active camera's post-process stack) and again for the load's quality application. The load's own `prepare`, after those, builds it once. A game's world driven without a Scene load keeps its frames' own preparations.
- Held threads: a synchronous link also advances the wall clock, and so does a draw's first use of a new program on iPad WebKit WebGL2, where probes return before their links complete. The coordinator credits back to both its bounds any gap of at least `SCENE_THREAD_HELD_MS` between its checkpoints (`heldThreadMs`), and the graph loop credits the time inside each readiness probe; shorter gaps are polling and count, and shorter links renew through progress instead. Each credit has a `SCENE_THREAD_HELD_BUDGET_MS` budget, one per `prepare` call for the coordinator and one per readiness loop (`graph.prepare` call) for the graph. A gap is credited whatever caused it: in a hidden page, whose timers browsers align to a second or more, the coordinator's bound is credited, while the graph loop's, which counts the wall time outside its probes, still ends a never-ready preparation. Nothing above `prepare` is timer-bounded except a SceneLayer preparation's ceiling across renderer replacements (below). The readiness waits that probe the same way outside preparation credit through `createHeldThreadBound` (monotonic `performance.now()`) from a `HeldThreadBudget` (`createHeldThreadBudget`, `SCENE_THREAD_HELD_BUDGET_MS`): `waitUntilReady` ([`preview-readiness.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/preview-readiness.ts); the shader warm 4 s for a preview's meshes and its first presentable frame), the asset-thumbnail capture's loading and readback bound (30 s), `prewarmMaterial`'s compile poll (4 s) and Custom GLSL's probe-effect compile (15 s, with a separate 120 s for blocking textures, each with its own watch so one's renewal never spends the other's progress); all of them also renew on compile progress. On a parallel-compile driver (D3D11 through ANGLE) their own effect pending renews them too, until the same set has been pending `SCENE_DRIVER_COMPILE_TIMEOUT_MS`: a hung compile ends the 4 s waits after about 64 s and Custom GLSL's 15 s after about 75 s. A thumbnail capture's watch has the shorter `SCENE_CAPTURE_CEILING_MS` (90 s), which also ends the capture's own preview preparation (its `current` check), so unrelated compiles on the shared Engine cannot hold the serialized queue for 300 s. A capture ends `expired` (distinct from `failed` and `cancelled`, see [asset-registry.md](asset-registry.md#thumbnails)) when it reaches that ceiling (`reason: "ceiling"`) or one of its waits' bounds, or the coordinator's `PreparationTimedOut`, ends it (`reason: "bound"`) while it is still wanted; the editor's queue retries only a `ceiling` expiry, once, with `SCENE_CAPTURE_RETRY_CEILING_MS` (300 s), passed as the capture's `ceilingMs`. The per-wait bounds are the same on the retry. A one-shot capture (`captureAssetThumbnailPng`, `captureModelThumbnailPng`) shares one budget across all its sequential waits, so a capture that never becomes ready cannot hold the serialized thumbnail queue for a full budget at each step; every other wait has its own. `createStallDeadline` takes the same kind of budget (`prewarmMeshMaterials`, its own; `prepareInstanceMaterials`, one per model instance): a timer that fires a held gap late starts a fresh window and spends only the lateness, at least `SCENE_THREAD_HELD_MS`, so a stuck unit gets a fresh window per credited late fire (up to the budget over `SCENE_THREAD_HELD_MS`, plus one); its `renew` is asked at every fire, held or not, so progress a held window saw renews no later one. A `createHeldThreadBound` wait that never becomes ready fails after its bound plus its budget once nothing compiles, and the capture then ends `expired` and keeps the type icon. A loading owner's first-frame budget, then its validated draw's completion budget, is a `createHeldThreadTimer` (one budget per presentation): such a bound checked every `HELD_THREAD_CHECKPOINT_MS` (250 ms), so a held gap is credited on the first tick after it, before the bound is judged. A single timer due inside the gap would fire once the thread frees and judge the hold as waiting; a view that reuses a closed view's linked programs (the effect bridge) shows no compile progress to renew it, and on software GL their first use held the thread past the whole 20 s. A capture's shared budget does not reach the `renderer.prepare` inside `renderPreviewFrame`, which keeps the coordinator's and the graph loop's own credit and renews on compile progress up to its ceiling: held credit across one capture can reach three budgets (about 180 s). Hidden pages: a gap is credited whatever caused it, so a stuck wait there ends after its bound plus the budget instead of at the bound.
- Opaque world visuals, models, the grid and debug draws share `RENDERING_GROUP.world` with no group depth clear (`configureEditorRenderingGroups`, [`sorting.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/sorting.ts)); helpers/billboards use `foreground`.
- Blended draws in a group sort by `alphaIndex`, then back to front: water first (`WATER_ALPHA_INDEX`, -1, set by `createWaterMesh`), then the grid (0), sorting-layer keys (from 0) and unsorted surfaces (`Number.MAX_VALUE`). Water drawn with the scene copy covers everything drawn before it, so whatever draws after it composites over it where nearer and is hidden by its depth pre-pass where farther; the grid below a water surface is hidden, and a crest above the grid plane cuts it.
- A Scene's post-process passes load from `SceneSettings.postProcessStack` with the Scene. Register / Unregister Scene Post-Processing ([`scene-post-process.ts`](../../../packages/runtime/src/scene-post-process.ts)) edit the runtime `Scene.postProcessStack` and send the whole stack as `scenePostProcess` (the Scene Layers' shared stack: `sceneLayerPostProcess`, [scene-layers.md](scene-layers.md#post-processing-runtime)), which `create-engine.ts` reattaches. Entries that stay keep their id, so Get Post Process Entry references and parameter writes survive; a removed entry's reference stops working. Both Register nodes return the new entry's Material Object (`services.getPostProcessEntry` on the owner and new id, so it is the same object Get Post Process Entry returns). A scene change starts again from the next document's stack.
- SceneLayers are extra unlit orthographic Scenes on the same Engine, composited after the world output ([`scene-layer-compositor.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/scene-layer-compositor.ts)); world post-processing never touches them. A layer's steady frame draws without validating its presentation (`SceneRenderCoordinator.render(false)`, no second strict round after the draw), as the world view's steady frames do; it validates while the layer presents for the first time (its loading owner's `presentingLayers`), while the ended stack's last composite stands in (`held`, released once every layer presents), and in a captured frame report. Their own post-processing is one stack for all layers combined: one shared target every layer draws into, the passes once, one blit; none at all while it has no enabled pass ([scene-layers.md](scene-layers.md#runtime-and-render)). Their camera preparation updates matrices only; never upload scene uniforms while another Scene's floating-origin context is current. `SceneLayerCompositor.prepare` follows the layer's renderer, then the passes' renderer while there are passes, through any replacement a post-process rebuild, resize or re-create swaps in while it waits, and resolves for a removed layer (a layer load's scope rejects once the layer is removed), so a script's post-process change does not fail a runtime-settings transaction or a layer load. The caller's `assertCurrent` cancels it, and an error from a renderer that is still current (a timeout, a missing camera, a disposed Scene) rejects. Each replacement's coordinator starts fresh bounds, so `prepare` stops following with `PreparationTimedOut` once `SCENE_PREPARATION_CEILING_MS` has passed since its first pass began (wall time, not credited for held threads): replacement faster than the graph prepares (a script toggling post-processes, a long resize drag) still ends, at most one coordinator wait after the ceiling.
- Auxiliary views (`ForwardSceneFrameGraphOptions.auxiliary`, through `SceneRenderCoordinator`'s `auxiliary` option; High and Full Quality Render Target Captures, and Scene Layer Post Processing Panels ([scene-layers.md](scene-layers.md#post-processing-panels))) are extra graphs on the same Scene for a non-active camera with an output target (colour only: it never needs depth). The scene view's graph draws them (`drawSceneAuxiliaryViews`, [`scene-auxiliary-views.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/scene-auxiliary-views.ts)) from its `Forward admitted shadows` task's `onAfterTaskExecute`: after Babylon's evaluation (world matrices, `_activeSkeletons` reset, particle steps) and the view's shadow maps, before the view's own objects. They execute their graph without `Scene.render` (no second animation, particle step or render-observer run); never admit lights or shadows (scene-wide, camera-ranked: only the scene view admits, or every material's light defines would churn per frame); never probe the scene (`isSceneFrameReady` binds shadow maps, refitting cascades); draw the owner's candidate list; and draw into own scene targets (or the chain's), writing the camera output only in the final copy so a monitor in view samples the previous image. They never take a Scene Layer's retained draw list (`attachLayerDrawList` is the scene view's alone) and clear their own targets with the Scene's clear settings at the time they draw. Nothing that dirties lighting (a new shadow map, a light toggle) may happen inside their draw: it runs mid-graph for the scene view.
- Every-frame captures draw only while something shows their output (`RenderTargetCaptures.findShown`): each scene render asks every mesh which Render Targets its Material samples (a Material once a frame; an unlit Material by its mesh's binding), and an output is shown when such a mesh is enabled, visible and inside the active camera's frustum (occlusion is not asked). A reflection's list (`collectPlanarMirrorMeshes`) and a capture's own list report through [`pass-list-signal.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/pass-list-signal.ts): an output one of those meshes samples is shown in the next frame too, up to `SHOWN_THROUGH_CAPTURES` captures away from a view, so captures that only show each other stop. An output keeps drawing while its consumers cannot be placed: no mesh's Material samples it, a Material no mesh draws with samples it (a post-process's, a particle system's), or another Scene holds its texture (a Scene Layer). Nothing is cached between frames. A first image, a manual capture and a requested capture always draw.
- Update Rate (Mirrors and every-frame captures; core `UPDATE_RATES`, `updateRateInterval`: 1, 2 or 4): honoured only on a Scene `setSceneHonoursUpdateRates` marked ([`update-rate-gate.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/update-rate-gate.ts)), which `createEngine` does for `playMode` (editor Play, Preview Build, the player); the editor viewport, previews and thumbnails draw every surface every render, so an edit and the editor's first frame never show a stale image. Both owners count by the Engine's `frameId` (`updateRateTick`) and take each surface's phase at registration from one allocator per Scene (`takeUpdateRatePhase`: per interval, in registration order across Mirrors and captures, so surfaces on one rate take turns whatever their kind); a surface is a candidate on frames where (frame + phase) is a multiple of its interval, so a frame the Scene does not render can move a surface's next draw one interval on. On the others it sits out before any work of its own: a capture skips placement, `ensureTarget`, its list (`collectCandidates`), readiness and draw (`RenderTargetCaptureDiagnostics.rateSkips`; `draws` counts published images), and `findShown` runs only on renders where some every-frame capture may draw, keeping the previous answers otherwise so reflections still mark outputs (`noteListed`). A capture draws whatever its phase until its output has published (first image, a target made again for a new asset, quality or colour type) and while it is owed (`due`: set on its phase's render and by any settings change in `configure`, not by a transform; cleared only when it publishes, so one nothing shows or whose view is not ready keeps asking every render). Mirrors: below.
- Per-view sun maps: `SceneShadowController.attachView(camera)` (claimed by a High or Full Quality capture at target creation, released with its view; and by a planar reflection's mirror camera under Reflection Shadows, below) mirrors every admitted sun map for that camera: same admitted size, cascades and settings (a capture target no wider or taller than `SMALL_CAPTURE_EDGE` takes one cascade, `captureSunCascades` in `render-target-capture.ts`: each cascade is a shadow pass every captured frame; the count is a uniform, so materials compile nothing for it), created by the same `createShadowGenerator` (hooks, defines, bias), keyed to the camera (`light._shadowGenerators`; Babylon binds `getShadowGenerator(activeCamera) ?? getShadowGenerator()`, so receivers drawn for the capture camera bind its own map), fitted to it as a scene view there would fit one, and redrawn by a pass the view draws only when its refresh policy finds it changed (`ShadowMapRefresh.apply` through `SceneShadowController.applyViewRefresh`, applied in the view's shadows task once the output is bound and the transform updated, so the fit it snapshots is the capture's; render-once, as the scene view's maps), so a still capture of still casters draws its depth tiles once. A mirror is cascade tiles of the same shadow atlas, so it charges nothing (the atlas is charged once): it takes the largest tile size the atlas has room for beside the scene view's admitted and standby maps, halving at once, samples neutrally until its first draw (its tiles hold what their last map drew), and gives way when a scene view's map finds no room in the atlas (`ShadowViewMaps.yieldAtlasTiles`, before standby maps are released or the map halves), mirroring again at the sync's end from what is left; mirrors of released or replaced maps go before replacements are built (`ShadowViewMaps.prune`), so the atlas goes with its last tile. Refused at 256 (warned once per scene map), the view's receivers bind the scene map (`viewBoundGenerator` falls back to it) and the view asks again after a second or once the scene map changes: toggling the sun's `shadowEnabled` for the view would rebuild every unfrozen material's light defines twice a frame. `metrics()` counts their passes. A view's `ManagedShadowsTask` (`view`) draws only its own maps and receives the scene view's point/spot maps as drawn, never calling `refreshShadowMaps` (it would refit local depth and reset render-once for the capture camera). Its object passes report a camera-keyed map with the cascaded task name and its camera: Babylon's `_setLightsForShadow` writes every other map under the null key, which would hand the scene view the capture's map. `unsupportedManagedShadows` accepts camera keys only for `viewBoundGenerator` maps. A planar reflection's (Mirror or planar water view; `ShadowSettings.reflections`, Reflection Shadows, on at Ultra; [`reflection-sun-shadows.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/reflection-sun-shadows.ts), one `ReflectionSunShadows` per mirror camera, owned by its `PlanarMirrorTargetState`): claimed by its first draw while the setting and shadows are on (from `onBeforeRender`, before the scene view's graph, as a capture claims), given back when the setting turns off (at once, for every reflection), with the target's storage or the target (water's idle release, a disposed surface or view, a colour-type change), when a Mirror fails, and after `REFLECTION_SHADOW_IDLE_RENDERS` (120) of its owner's renders without a draw. No graph draws them: `drawViewMaps(camera)` draws them right before the reflection's pass, inside the owner's `mirrors` or `water` draw pass, with the scene view's camera active, each through `drawBorrowedTarget` and `withSceneReadinessState` as a view's shadows task draws one (`renderUnmanaged`: render id, intermediate rendering), its first draw waiting for its casters' depth variants (neutral meanwhile). As a capture's, they follow the scene view's refresh policy (`ShadowMapRefresh.apply`: render-once, redrawn when the fit, light, settings or a caster they reach changes, the change described in the journal), so a reflection kept because nothing changed draws no maps and their passes do not dirty every other reflection; `viewMapsDue` (also run by the owner's reuse check) says whether one would draw differently or has not drawn, which makes the reflection due; the owner then takes the maps' own journal entries as seen (`ReflectedContent.acknowledgeShadowPasses`, or by drawing them before it reads the journal). The mirror camera's frozen projection is oblique (near plane on the mirror plane), and Babylon's cascade fit slices the frustum between its near and far corners by the camera's clip range: fitted to it, every cascade is several times coarser than the scene view's. So the maps apply their refresh and draw with the camera's projection frozen to the view's ordinary perspective it was made from (same side planes, so it holds everything the reflection shows, at the view depths the block chooses cascades by), the oblique one restored for the pass: each cascade then has the scene view's splits and detail. A refusal by the atlas leaves the reflection unshadowed by the sun, warned once per scene map for every reflection. Only the reflections that draw (at most `MIRROR_RENDERS_PER_FRAME` Mirrors, one water view per camera) claim or draw maps; a scene readiness probe that binds them refits them to the oblique projection, which only redraws them once.
- Point/spot maps stay shared: they do not depend on the view, and `fitLocalShadowDepth` writes their depth range explicitly from the scene camera, so every view binds the drawn range. Differences from a camera at the capture: the near plane is the scene camera's, and which local lights get maps is ranked from the scene camera, like light admission.
- Cluster bounds: a point light's sphere is its range about its position; a spot light's is the smallest sphere holding its lit sector (`spotClusterSphere`: through the apex and the rim, centred range / (2 cos) along the axis, while the half-angle is under 45°; on the rim's circle, centred range · cos with radius range · sin, up to 90°; the range about the apex beyond that or for an unbounded range), widened by `CONE_TOLERANCE` of the range; a rect light's plane test (`markRect`) evaluates each corner of the cluster grid, which neighbouring clusters share, once per light, its view-space positions and lengths computed once per build at its first rect light, and marks exactly the clusters a per-cluster corner test marks (`light-clusters.test.ts` compares the two).
- The light clusters follow the drawing view: each drawing camera keeps its own block (`bindHapticClusters`; released with the camera), built at a pass's first draw when that camera's view or the local lights differ from its own last build's, so views that take turns in a frame (the scene view, Mirror and capture cameras) build only when their own inputs change, and an auxiliary view's draws read its own. A build reads only the recorded inputs, so a kept block is what a build would write. `verifyLightClusterSlots` (`HAPTIC_PERF_VERIFY_MIRROR_WORK`) builds a block kept across another camera's turn afresh and compares it bit for bit (`rendererWork.lightClusters`, `mismatches` must stay 0); `forceClusterRebuilds` (`HAPTIC_PERF_MIRROR_UNSHARED`) builds at every turn, as one shared block did. A failed auxiliary preparation retries at most once a second; a throwing auxiliary draw is caught per capture (warned once until a draw succeeds), which keeps its last image.
- GPU particle systems (`OwnedGPUParticleSystem`) step once per scene frame on their first draw; later draws that frame show the stepped buffers, so captures never step them again.
- Graph camera writes are counted per scene (`graphCameraWrites`), not per graph: Babylon's graph render, `setActiveCamera` and auxiliary swaps are never authored camera changes for any graph of the Scene.
- Occlusion culling, `RenderingQuality.geometry.occlusionCulling` (on at every tier; live; console `quality geometry occlusion on|off`): a world view (`SceneRenderCoordinator` with `occlusion`, which create-engine passes for the editor's, Play's and the player's world view, never previews, Scene Layers or auxiliary captures) culls while `sceneOcclusionCulling` holds: its Scene's access (`allowSceneOcclusionCulling`) is "setting" (create-engine's Play and player Scenes) with the setting on, or "always" (the editor viewport's Engine Settings **Viewport > Occlusion Culling**, `viewportOcclusionCulling`, default on, `EditorTools.setOcclusionCulling`); "never" by default. It gives its `CameraOutputCullTask` a `ViewOcclusion` ([`view-occlusion.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/view-occlusion.ts)). After the frustum cull and `leaveOutHidden`, and before `worldObjectList`, it rasterises the occluders in the list into a 256×128 CPU view-depth buffer ([`occlusion-raster.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/occlusion-raster.ts): row spans from edge crossings that adjacent triangles share, per-occluder one-texel separable erosion, a max pyramid whose texels a test reduces on first read after `finish` (an Infinity child ends the reduction), a frame clear of only the rectangle the last frame's occluders wrote, a test of at most 8×8 texels; conservative, fuzzed against a ray-cast reference) and replaces the output with its own list without the meshes wholly behind them (copy-on-write: the input and Babylon's state are untouched). The colour pass, its blended depth pre-pass, the geometry tasks, the water split and Screen-Space Shadows draw that list; shadow casters, captures, mirrors, probes, picking and outline masks build their own lists, so a hidden mesh still casts. Readiness probes walk the cull's input, so a hidden mesh still compiles. Occluders: meshes with a registered convex inner shape (`registerOccluderShape`; `createPrimitiveMesh` registers box, sphere, cylinder and single-sided plane shapes; `registerModelOccluder` gives a Model instance's largest mesh its import-baked box, `ModelPayload.occluder` from `bakeGltfOccluder` in `packages/assets/src/occluder-bake.ts`, in the actor root's space, drawn only while the camera is outside the model's bounds and every mesh of it is opaque), with the MeshComponent `occluder` property (`applyOccluderProperty`: auto, always ignores the size gate, never) that are opaque, not deforming, not helpers and in the world rendering group, at least `MIN_OCCLUDER_TEXELS` in radius on screen, ranked by screen size up to `OCCLUDER_TRIANGLE_BUDGET`; a box draws only its front faces (its winding flips with a mirrored world matrix and a right-handed view). Exempt occludees: always-drawn meshes (`keepsDrawingOutsideView`, `alwaysSelectAsActiveMesh`), helpers, rendering groups after the world's, own depth functions, and a mesh whose colour pass Effect has not drawn (`effectDrawnOn`), so its first draw never waits for it to come into view. Perspective cameras only, never under `skipFrustumClipping`. A frame whose ordered occluders have the meshes, shapes and world matrices the buffer was drawn with, under the same view-projection, near plane, winding parity and eye, keeps the buffer and its reduced pyramid instead of drawing them again (the rasteriser is deterministic); `verifyKeptOcclusionBuffers` draws again on every kept frame and counts a list that differs (`RenderDiagnostics.occlusion.keptMismatches`). A view whose filter culls nothing backs off (`ViewOcclusion.noteRun`): after `QUIET_RUNS` (4) such runs in a row it skips the whole filter (candidate scan, raster, tests) and passes the frustum's list on unfiltered, as with occlusion off, for `FIRST_BACK_OFF` (2) frames, doubling after each probe that again culls nothing up to `MAX_BACK_OFF` (32); a run that culls returns it to every frame. The state is per `ViewOcclusion`, so per view. Occlusion only removes meshes opaque occluders hide, so backing off changes how many hidden meshes draw, never the picture; nothing else reads the filtered list (shadows, captures, mirrors, probes, picking, outline masks and readiness probes build their own lists or walk the cull's input). An idle frame keeps the last run's counts, reports 0 ms and `idle`, counts in `skippedFrames`, and `backOff` is the current skip; `stat draws`, the `showocclusion` text and the Check's notes say idle, while off shows no occlusion row. A frozen view (`freezeocclusion`) runs every frame; `forceOcclusionEveryFrame` (Play test host, route `HAPTIC_PERF_OCCLUSION_EVERY_FRAME`) turns backing off off, and the route's `HAPTIC_PERF_OCCLUSION_PARITY` compares paused frames drawn both ways. `occlusionDiagnostics()` and `occludedMeshes()` report the last frame; `sceneOcclusionDiagnostics` feeds `RenderDiagnostics.occlusion` and the `stat draws` row `occlusion culled N/M · T tris · X ms`. Debug tier: `showocclusion` (engine command `setOcclusionDebug`; hosts read `handle.occlusionOverlay()` and draw `sceneOcclusionBuffer` into a DOM 2D canvas with `drawOcclusionOverlay`, so it compiles nothing) and `freezeocclusion` (`setSceneOcclusionFrozen`: the view keeps its last buffer and view-projection and tests each frame's meshes against them). The lighting CI gate runs with it forced on and the receiver hidden, and `e2e/occlusion-culling.spec.ts` holds it to the same image with fewer draws.

## Loading and readiness

- Play and the exported player share `createSceneLoadReadiness` ([`scene-load-readiness.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/scene-load-readiness.ts)): `sceneLoading` (or `activeScene`, for a session's first Scene, which the runtime realizes without a loading paint) opens a transaction with a monotonic load ID, `sceneRealized` closes the worker's assignment batch, then models, sample-ready textures, shader warm and first-frame presentation complete before `sceneModelsReady` acknowledges that ID. Superseded loads never acknowledge or report failures. A load has at most one paint barrier: one that departs from a shown Scene or layer (`sceneLoading`, `sceneLayerLoading`) waits for the host's `paint` before acknowledging the loading paint, so its loading UI is on screen before the runtime retires what shows; a session's first Scene waits for none. The first frame then presents, and the load acknowledges once it has, without another paint (the loading UI is already shown, and the presented frame is coherent whether or not the UI over it is gone yet). Editor Play's `paint` is `waitForSceneLoadingPaint`: a posted-message task (a UI commit scheduled before it lands in the frame), one animation frame, then a task after it, which runs once that frame's paint has. Streamed instances use `createSceneStreamingReadiness`.
- A world or SceneLayer load that a lost graphics context ends rejects with `RenderingContextLostError` ([`context-lost-error.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/context-lost-error.ts); `isRenderingContextLostError` follows `cause`), which editor Play reports as `scene.loading.contextLost` instead of `scene.loading.failed`. `isEngineContextLost` reads Babylon's `_contextWasLost`, set until the context is restored.
- Editor Play shows the loading dialog; Preview Build and exported players show no host loading UI (games author loading with SceneLayers) and only mirror phase/progress on `data-scene-load-*` attributes. A scene load's phases (`SceneLoadPhase`, [`scene-load-readiness.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/scene-load-readiness.ts)) run Preparing Scene, Loading Models, Loading Textures, Compiling Shaders, then Presenting First Frame; Compiling Shaders carries a count (`SceneLoadProgress.count`, shown as `(done/total)` by the editor's loading dialog). Compiling Shaders counts the warm-up's units (`prewarmMeshMaterials`: a Material's plain or instanced variant for one mesh, listed before the first, so units sharing an Effect finish at once), then goes on counting the programs its later work compiles (spawn warm-up, the graph's preparation: shadow depth, geometry, reflection probe and outline variants and screen effects), which no unit counted: each Effect the Engine returns meanwhile that was not cached when the warm-up ended, compiled once linked (`countEffectCompiles` in [`effect-requests.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/effect-requests.ts)). Otherwise a finished count would show while that work, longer than the warm-up on software GL, still compiles. The editor viewport's own load (`runSceneViewportBlockingLoad`) counts Compiling Shaders the same way.
- Asset sources are scoped: Scene catalogs, `EngineHandle.acquireSceneSources` and script load handles acquire through one loader; unread deferred scenes stay unread. Source/CPU budgets and GPU budgets are separate.
- Native work (model decode/LOD, audio decode, cold texture/material/font preparation) shares one admission queue per Engine (`nativePreparationForEngine`, [`native-preparation.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/native-preparation.ts); defaults 2 concurrent, 64 MiB temporary). Priority order: gameplay (High), preload (Normal), background (Low). Later commands for a slot wait behind its cold assignment and apply in order. A prepared source scope (`acquireSceneSources` with `prepare`) schedules each group (fonts, then textures, Materials and models) at once through `prepareTogether`, so the queue, not the caller, bounds it; a job pushes its release before it first awaits, the first failure cancels the group's queued jobs, and the group settles before it rethrows, so the scope's cancel releases everything it acquired. Models start Auto LOD (when their payload allows it) on the held lease without waiting for it. A Scene source's cold texture preparation (`acquireSceneSources`) acquires a 2D Texture in the material sampling (`MATERIAL_TEXTURE_SAMPLING`) and an environment cube as `{ isCube: true }`, the samplings Materials and environment lighting bind, so it warms their upload rather than a second one (`create-engine.play.test.ts`); a Sprite's pixel-art sampling is still uploaded when it is first acquired.
- Editor blocking loads realize in cooperative chunks (`runSceneWork`; yield after about 8 ms or 256 steps, through a `MessageChannel` turn, since nested `setTimeout(0)` clamps to 4 ms; progress is reported once per chunk). Hidden Scene tabs defer construction until their canvas has a nonzero size. A canvas's first size applies without the 80 ms resize hold (`canvas-resize-guard.ts`), and the paint before the first frame is skipped once a load has run for two frame intervals since its dialog painted. An editor Engine seeds no default scene: every view loads its own.
- The shader warm-up runs units of different Materials concurrently (`SCENE_SHADER_WARM_CONCURRENCY`, 8), so a driver with `KHR_parallel_shader_compile` links several programs at once; one Material's units run in order, because Babylon's `forceCompilation` saves and restores the Material's `allowShaderHotSwapping` around each compile. A unit whose mesh no longer draws its Material finishes at once (Babylon's poll never settles for a disposed Material). The stall window renews on each completion and names the oldest unit still compiling.
- Effect bridge ([`effect-bridge.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/effect-bridge.ts)): a view closing on a shared Engine retains every compiled Effect (one reference each) until the Engine's next world view presents its first frame, or `EFFECT_BRIDGE_MS`; the editor's view before Play and Play's before the editor's reload draw mostly the same Materials, which then hit the Effect cache.
- Play keep (`keepPlayEffects`, same file): editor Play's view (`playMode` on the shared Engine) takes one reference on every Effect the Engine's `createEffect` returns while it lives (`watchEngineEffects`, [`effect-requests.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/effect-requests.ts)) and keeps it past Play's close, so the next Play finds its programs in the Effect cache after the editor's reload has ended the bridge, and a load that releases a program and asks for it again (each world graph build's screen-effect passes, the environment filter run again after the load) links it once. Only `createEffect` reaches the cache, so every `EffectWrapper` Haptic builds draws from the shader store, the spatial and screen-shadow stages too (`customWrapper` in [`spatial-effects.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/spatial-effects.ts) draws from the shader store, since Babylon constructs an inline source's Effect outside the cache, which linked each stage at every graph build and the two occlusion blurs apart). Particle programs are named by build id, so they also need `shareParticleProgram` ([shader-graph.md](shader-graph.md)); a post-process Material's pass, named the same way, still links again in each Play. Budget: at most `PLAY_EFFECT_KEEP_BUDGET` (256) Effects per Engine, the least recently returned released first (about two large levels' Play programs; an Effect holds its program, never a texture or target). Measure it as the Feature Test Check's resource samples' `effects` (every Engine's compiled Effects) after Stop and after the editor's reload, or `keptEffectCount`; `e2e/feature-test-perf-route.spec.ts` with `HAPTIC_PERF_PLAYS=2` counts the programs each Play links. Kept programs make cache hits common, and Babylon's cache hit calls none of the request's callbacks (an options-style `createEffect`'s `onCompiled` and `onError`), so an owner waits on the Effect itself (`executeWhenCompiled`, its `onErrorObservable`), as the environment base radiance read does. The keep takes its reference once a program has compiled: one its owners let go while it still compiles stays abandoned (Babylon then skips preparing it; held, it was finished for nobody, from shader sources its owner had removed, a retired post-process pass's), and one that fails to link is never kept (a later requester would get it from the cache without its error). The keep goes with the Engine and with a lost context (a WebGL2 restore relinks every cached Effect). At Play's close each kept Effect's `onCompiled` and `onError` are cleared once it has compiled: they belong to its first creator, and would hold the closed Play's Materials and Scene. It keeps only Effects: render targets, textures and the editor view's release under Play are unchanged.
- Post-processing switch hold (`holdCompiledEffects`, same file): Play's `postprocessing off` (`setPostProcessingDebug`) takes one reference on every compiled Effect, held until the view first presents ready (`worldRenderer.isReady()` and the layers') with post-processing on again, the view's disposal (after its bridge), the Engine's disposal or a lost context. Switching off stops drawing the screen-effect passes' programs and the Material variants they need, which the Play keep's budget may let go meanwhile (and a Play that owns its Engine keeps none); held, switching back links none of them again. It holds programs only, never a graph's targets or managed leases.
- A world load compiles each lit Material once, against its final lighting, in the load's warm-up (`prewarmSceneMaterials`) and graph probes. Model and foliage instances that publish during the load compile nothing (`holdSceneInstanceCompiles` in [`glb-anim.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/glb-anim.ts); `prepareInstanceMaterials` still admits their textures): realization starts model loads before it creates the lights, fog and shadow maps, a new Material's light capacity is set at the warm-up's `syncSceneLighting`, and an unpublished instance is not yet a shadow receiver, so their compiles built variants the first frame never draws. The hold lasts from the editor's `loadSceneAsync` (while its generation is current) or Play's `sceneLoading` (while that load is) until the world warm-up starts or the load fails (the editor load rejecting, `sceneLoadFailed` reaching the Play Engine); instances published outside a load (drops, spawns, streams, foliage edits) compile before they publish, in the variants Babylon draws them with (next bullet).
- The warm-up (`prewarmMeshMaterials`) and `prepareInstanceMaterials` compile each mesh only in the variants Babylon draws it with (`drawnMaterialVariants`, [`drawn-variants.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/drawn-variants.ts)): plain, plus instanced for a mesh with instances, only instanced for a thin-instanced mesh (foliage) and for a source marked as drawing only its instances (`markInstanceSource`, [`instance-source.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/instance-source.ts)), and nothing for a mesh without vertices (empty roots, model placeholders, geometry that arrives later, which the graph probes compile). `prepareInstanceMaterials` compiles the source of each instance under the model once, wherever the source stands.
- A regular instance (a glTF node instance) draws in its source's batch, with the source's submeshes and Effects, so the source is its readiness unit (`drawingMesh`): the hidden-until-ready check reads the source's submeshes (an instance spawned into a drawing batch waits for a swap as the batch does, shown), and `hasDrawn` counts an instance drawn once its source has. The caster variant maps ask a batch's source for its instanced depth variant (`ShadowCasterVariants`): the map's own readiness check asks for the frame's visible instances, which outside a frame are none.
- Particle preparation follows the held-thread and compile-progress rules of the Loading budgets and Held threads bullets above. `boundParticlePreparation` ([`particle-system-factory.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/particle-system-factory.ts)) bounds `ParticleService`'s wait for a bundle's slots (one per preparation; expiry fails the bundle) and `bindParticleMaterial`'s wait for the Material's build and effect (one per bind; expiry rejects and the service skips the slot). A Material whose build has completed (`prewarmParticleMaterial`, which a Spawn Emitter runs for its Material when it is made) binds synchronously, with no readiness check or readiness mark. Each is a `createHeldThreadBound` of the 4 s shader-warm bound, checked every 16 ms (well under `SCENE_THREAD_HELD_MS`, so a longer gap between checks is a held thread), with its own `HeldThreadBudget` and `DriverCompileWatch`: a synchronous link that outlasts the bound is not charged, a wait that keeps finishing shaders renews up to `SCENE_PREPARATION_CEILING_MS`, and one that never readies fails after its bound plus the credited time. The Material build can outlast the bound (a Custom GLSL particle Material links a probe effect first). Progress is per Engine, so unrelated compiles on the shared Engine renew a preview's preparation too. Timeout messages end with the watch's counts and keep the words "timed out" (the particle proofs in `e2e/` retry on them).

## Rendering settings

- One lighting path; projects and Scenes carry no render path (`normalizeRenderProjectSettings` drops unknown keys on load).
- `settings.render.mode` is PBR or CEL. CEL is display-space with hard steps for bands, shadow visibility and highlights, each edge anti-aliased to one pixel; there is no softness or Smooth option. Derivatives are undefined in non-uniform control flow, so CEL's light loop sums continuous values (each light's brightness, its highlight signal) and every `fwidth` threshold runs after it (`haptic-lighting-shader.test.ts`). A cast shadow is the four-tap kernel (CEL sets `HAPTIC_SHADOW_NARROW`, as water does) sharpened by the pixel's footprint in shadow-map texels to a one-pixel ramp on the shadow side of a lit share of one half (a share of a half or more is full light, as the former hard step had it), so no derivative of the shadow is taken. The shadow does not go through the ramp: the loop sums the lights with the partially shadowed light on and off, both are banded, and they mix by its shadow; the ramp's edge width is the smaller of the summed and the unshadowed light's change across the pixel, so a shadow's step never widens a band edge into a line along it. Only graph Materials follow the mode: each is one graph whose `HapticLightingBlock` draws CEL as its `HAPTIC_CEL` define, re-prepared on a mode change, and nothing replaces a Scene's materials.
- A geometry pass without a colour output compiles no lighting: `HapticLightingBlock` leaves its light loop, shadow lookups and environment out under `GEOMETRY_PASS_WITHOUT_COLOR` ([`geometry-surface-output-block.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/geometry-surface-output-block.ts): `PREPASS` without `PREPASS_COLOR`, and the first attachment one of the outputs `GeometrySurfaceOutputBlock` overwrites), since every output such a pass keeps comes from the surface's inputs. Its draws bind only the light and cluster blocks (`HapticLightingBlock.readsBindings`, from `isGeometryPassWithoutColor` on the Effect's defines, decided once per Effect; the frozen Material rebind of `bindReceiver` too): a `std140` block stays active in a program that never reads it, and a draw with a smaller buffer left at its binding point fails, while its textures and uniforms are inactive, so skipping them saves CPU work and no GL call. A pass that requests the colour, or whose first attachment is another output (the object id picker's), compiles it as before. `e2e/geometry-pass-lighting.spec.ts` renders one frame with every spatial effect on, in PBR and CEL, over lit, mirror, alpha-clipped, translucent and 3D-lit unlit surfaces, and holds the geometry attachments and the frame byte-identical to a build that keeps the lighting in that pass (`HAPTIC_RENDER_NATIVE_GPU=1` with `playwright.perf.config.ts`'s `perf-gpu` project runs it on the machine's GPU).
- Real-Time Reflections (SSR) read the geometry pass's reflectivity attachment (rgb F0, a glossiness; `PREPASS_REFLECTIVITY`, requested only by `spatial-effects-graph.ts`). Writers: PBR (Babylon; unlit writes zero), lit graph Materials (`GeometrySurfaceOutputBlock`: base color, metallic, roughness; unlit writes zero, as the built-in Mirror surface does), and the unlit Material (zero). The engine creates no Standard surface. CEL runs SSR whenever the project's Real-Time Reflections effect runs (one setting for both modes; there is no CEL-only switch, and a saved `cel.reflectionsEnabled` is dropped on load); AO stays PBR-only.
- One compose (`Scene Effects Compose`, [`spatial-compose-shader.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/spatial-compose-shader.ts)) writes the spatial chain's colour: the last stage, full resolution, reading the chain's input colour and each effect's reduced-resolution result (`SpatialStage.reads`: AO Blur Y, SSR Blur Y, the fog march), applying occlusion, then Babylon's reflection combine (ported with its Fresnel, threshold and orthographic view direction), then fog, in linear colour. The SSR trace reads the chain's input (`SpatialStage.source`), so reflections carry no occlusion (decided: one full-resolution pass instead of three). Each effect alone is byte-identical to its former compose; together only the half-float (and, in Display Space, the 2.2-power) round trips between the former composes are gone. An idle fog march leaves the fog term out through `fogActive` (set from the march's `idle`, which `syncIdle` evaluated); fog alone shares the march's `idle`, so the compose skips and copies its source. `e2e/effects-compose.spec.ts` (`perf-gpu` with `HAPTIC_RENDER_NATIVE_GPU=1`; `HAPTIC_EFFECTS_PROOF_OUT` keeps the frames) renders one fixed 944 × 630 frame per combination for comparing two builds.
- Ambient Occlusion ([`ambient-occlusion-shader.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/ambient-occlusion-shader.ts)) samples a hemisphere around the geometry pass's view normal. The depth is read nearest, so the pixel and each sample are reconstructed on the ray through the centre of the depth texel they read; a point off that ray would sit in front of or behind its own surface and occlude it. A sample occludes only when the scene point it lands on is in front of it by the depth bias, rises above the pixel's tangent planes (the cosine to the normal past 0.15, full at 0.35) and lies within twice the radius in view space (full weight within the radius). The planes are the shaded normal's and the face's, reconstructed from the depth texels beside the pixel (each axis takes the neighbour nearer in depth); the rise is the lesser of the two. A smooth-shaded low-poly mesh's normal tilts by up to half the angle between its faces, so its plane alone cuts through the faces beside a convex edge, and the face plane alone is noisy with a half-float depth. A face seen edge-on, or past a convex edge, therefore never occludes itself, and a nearer object occludes no surface it does not reach: none of them darkens edges or silhouettes as the camera moves. `e2e/spatial-effects.spec.ts` holds all three to zero darkened pixels: a grazing floor, a wall behind a plate and a coarse smooth-shaded ball. The march and both blurs write visibility to every channel and every reader (the blurs, the compose) takes red alone, so their targets are R16F (`SpatialStage.format`): a quarter of the write and tap bandwidth. They do not share the half-scale RGBA16F targets the reflection and volumetric stages alias at the same scale, so with those on the pair is two more targets; with the opt-in merged marches a fog march lives from before the objects to the compose, so it aliases no reflection target either (one more half-scale RGBA16F target than its own pass needs).
- Merged marches ([`merged-marches.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/merged-marches.ts)), an opt-in awaiting a native-GPU proof (Direct3D 11, ANGLE on Metal): off by default, so the default graph draws each march in its own pass and the task order above is unchanged; `worlddebug mergedmarches on` (off by default unlike every other part, `world.debug` lists it as `mergedmarches on`; part of `liveSceneEffectsKey`, so the graph builds again) enables it. With it on and Screen-Space Shadows planned, the ambient occlusion and volumetric marches whose own target has the screen shadows' size (`round(w × scale)` equals `ceil(w / 2)` for every integer w at the High scale 0.5; checked per axis, `createSpatialStages`' `mergedMarchSize`) are `march` stages with no Effect, and the screen shadows' task becomes `Half-resolution marches`: one full-screen draw whose program writes the screen shadows (attachment 0, RGBA16F), the occlusion (R16F) and the fog (RGBA16F) into their own targets, each march in a uniform branch of `hapticMarchRuns`, before `Forward objects`. Two passes, two clears and two target switches fewer per frame; their readers (the blurs, the compose) follow as before. Moving them is exact: they read only the geometry pass (complete before the screen shadows), the shadow atlas and `HapticLights` (written by the shadow task and the frame's light write, before the geometry pass, and by nothing in the graph after), the Fog Volumes and the camera, never the scene colour (the fog stage's `source: "scene"` only orders it) or an earlier frame. Each march's text is its own pass's, moved: the generators return declarations, functions and main's body (`MarchSource`) and take how the body ends a pixel (`MarchEnds`); a merged early end assigns its output and runs the rest of its body in an `else`, so the merge adds no `return` and no function, and no march discards, writes `gl_FragColor` or takes a derivative (their implicit-LOD reads sample single-level, nearest buffers). `syncIdle` sets each merged march's `running` from its `idle`; the pass runs while anything in it marches (an idle one binds only `bindIdle`: the fog's light buffer and atlas, which the program declares) and idles when nothing does; an idle march's attachment holds the pass's zero clear, which no reader samples (the objects bind the placeholder, the compose leaves the fog out). Other sizes, no Screen-Space Shadows, or the part off (the default) keep each march's own pass. `e2e/merged-marches.spec.ts` (`effects-compose-proof.ts` with `mergedMarches`; software GL in Verify, and `perf-gpu` with `HAPTIC_RENDER_NATIVE_GPU=1` on a GPU for the native proof) compares the frame, the compose and every target a reader samples by SHA-256 between the default and the merged pass, with every march running and with the fog or the screen shadows idle.
- Volumetric fog ([`volumetric-shader.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/volumetric-shader.ts)) marches single scattering per pixel. Scene-wide fog's density (`volumetricLighting.density`) holds at and below `height` and thins above it as `exp(-max(0, y - height) * heightFalloff)` at each march point's world height (`volumeHeight`), so it pools low without a shader variant; Fog Volumes add their own density at every height. Extinction is per step for that reason. Each medium has its own samples and every step takes the nearest next one (front to back): scene-wide fog evenly over `[0, min(maxDistance, surface)]`, each crossed Fog Volume over its own chord up to the surface, with samples in proportion to the chord's length and at least one while the budget allows, so a short volume inside or beside a long one is never starved and no sample is anchored to a distance from the eye (eye-anchored samples made the fog swim as the camera moved). Optical depth adds per volume, so overlaps stay exact. In the Fog Volume variant with scene density the steps split evenly on every pixel (scene fog `floor(steps / 2)`), never per pixel, which would draw each volume's outline in lit scene fog; without scene density the volumes take all of them. Volumetric Distance never limits Fog Volumes: `selectFogVolumes` keeps the eight nearest in the frustum, whose near and far planes are the march's depth range, and point and spot lights take part out to the farthest bound volume (`bindFogVolumes` returns its reach).
- Anti-Aliasing (`PostProcessingQuality.antiAliasing`: `msaa4` | `msaa2` | `fxaa` | `off`; Low `fxaa`, Medium `msaa2`, High and above `msaa4`) applies to world views only (`allowSceneScreenShadows` marks them; not a Scene Layer editor view, `excludeSceneWorldPostEffects`): `sceneMultisampleCount` ([`render-settings.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/render-settings.ts)) gives the scene pass's samples, clamped to `caps.maxMSAASamples` (Babylon sets it to 1 where WebKit's MSAA is broken); it is the only anti-aliasing control (no project FXAA switch): `fxaa` alone puts FXAA in the plan (`SceneEffectsInputs.fxaa`), so MSAA never stacks with FXAA and Off is off; scripts set it with Set Anti-Aliasing. The count is in `effectsKey`, so a change rebuilds the view's graph and holds as any quality change does. In `ForwardSceneFrameGraph` the scene colour and depth the objects draw into carry the samples, whichever owns them (`SceneEffectsGraph`, `ScenePostProcessGraph`, or the view's own pair, which a multisampled view without a chain declares and copies to the single-sample output: the canvas is created with `antialias: false`, so direct presentation keeps working). Where that pair is the output's size, its colour is RGBA8, the output is the backbuffer and nothing after the objects pass reads the scene colour (no water scene copy), `MultisamplePresentTask` ([`multisample-present.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/multisample-present.ts)) blits the multisampled colour straight to the backbuffer and the objects pass skips its colour resolve; elsewhere the resolve and the output copy stay. Shared outlines then compose onto the backbuffer after that blit: their composition only blends onto its target and samples nothing beneath it. Everything later samples the resolve: colour averaged when each objects pass ends; depth (`resolveMSAADepth`, on the objects pass when the water scene copy reads it) by sample, never blended (`blitFramebuffer` NEAREST), so edge pixels hold one real surface's depth. The spatial geometry pass (AO, SSR, volumetrics, Screen-Space Shadows) and the outline masks keep their own single-sample depth. Bloom over the multisampled scene colour (no spatial stage before it) samples its resolve; its merge is the first stage of the single-sample Display Color pass (or `Scene Effects Bloom Merge` without image processing), which writes the output or a single-sample target of the scene colour's size and type. Alpha-to-coverage ([`alpha-to-coverage.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/alpha-to-coverage.ts)): masked graph Materials (`CoverageClipBlock`) and the unlit Material (alpha-tested meshes) register; every Material's `_afterBind` (patched once) sets the engine state for its own draw (on only for a registered Material's alpha-tested mesh into a multisampled target; the unlit Material leaves out a mesh whose texture samples nearest both ways, `samplesSteppedAlpha`, such as sprites and tiles: stepped alpha has no edge to smooth, and a coverage clip would draw its transparent texels at about 0.1 coverage wherever a 2 × 2 neighbour samples an opaque texel, including an atlas's next cell past a quad's edge), a blending `setAlphaMode` and the end of each objects pass turn it off, and the colour variant writes coverage alpha `(value − cutoff) / fwidth + 0.5` instead of discarding while the uniform `hapticAlphaCoverage` is 1, so toggling MSAA compiles nothing (pass variants and the geometry pass only discard). The unlit surface reads its UV at the centroid of a pixel's covered samples (`interpolateAtCentroid` in `unlit-material-blocks.ts`): a multisampled edge pixel is shaded once, at its centre, which can lie outside the triangle, and a sprite's, tile's or grid's UV extrapolated there sampled the texel beyond its atlas frame, a faint line along its bounding box. Surface Scene Depth nodes read Babylon's own single-sample depth renderer, unaffected. `e2e/anti-aliasing.spec.ts` holds it to: identical consecutive frames of a static camera, smoothed geometric and cut-out edges, and no pixel changed by MSAA farther than one pixel from an edge with AO, SSR and water in view (no halo). The samples are stored after every pass (Babylon resolves at each unbind) unless the `discardsamples` debug switch is on: then the render pass that writes each multisampled texture last in the graph (`lastSampleWriters` in [`sample-discard.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/sample-discard.ts), disabled passes counted) invalidates it after its own resolve, and `MultisamplePresentTask` its colour after the blit; a measurement for tile-based GPUs, not yet the behaviour. Auxiliary views stay single-sample. Accounting charges each sample beside the resolved texture (`renderTargetAllocationBytes` with `samples`; `estimateTextureMemory` reads `InternalTexture.samples`).
- Color pipeline (`render.effects.colorPipeline.mode`): `displaySpace` when unset; new 3D projects start `sceneLinear` with `neutral` tone mapping (`createEmptyProject`), 2D and Pixel Art Optimized projects `displaySpace` so sprite colours stay exact; `sceneLinear` only for PBR. `toneMapping` defaults to `neutral` (Scene Linear only), and the editor picks it when a project with `none` switches to Scene Linear. Babylon's image-processing pass is the only display encoding; each Display Color task owns its own `ImageProcessingConfiguration` (sharing the Scene's corrupts per-material processing). ACES hue shifts are expected; `neutral` holds hues.
- Post Processing (`render.effects`) resolves project → Scene → session, leaf by leaf (`resolveRenderEffectsSettings`, [`render-effects.ts`](../../../packages/core/src/render-effects.ts)): `SceneSettings.effectsOverrides` holds only the fields a Scene sets (`normalizeRenderEffectsOverrides` keeps a leaf only if the project block would accept it, clamped as there). The renderer reads it into `SceneRendering.effectsOverrides` with the Scene's other overrides (`applySceneDocumentRenderSettings` in `create-engine.ts`), session overrides (`runtimeOverrides.effects`) apply above it, and `ScalabilitySession.requested` reports the same order. The editor viewport bakes the resolved block into its render-settings key, so a Scene override changes the plan as a project edit does. A Scene's LUT override is a required Scene dependency unless that Scene turns Color Grading off.
- `resolveSceneRenderingQuality` ([`render-settings.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/render-settings.ts)) is cached per Scene by the identity of `SceneRendering.project`, `shadowOverrides`, `localQualityOverrides` and `qualityOverrides`: writers replace those inputs, never mutate them, and every caller shares the result (and `SceneRendering.shadows`, which aliases its `shadows`) read-only.
- Scalability categories, presets and the ownership registry are in core; `ScalabilitySession` holds non-persistent session overrides. Readback separates requested from effective; effective changes only after a renderer-confirmed ready frame. `RuntimeScalability` ([`runtime-scalability.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/runtime-scalability.ts)) applies a transaction that draws what the applied one draws (equal render values but for `preset` labels, `qualityValuesKey`; the frame cap is pacing) without preparing, retaining or holding the view, and acknowledges it at the next presented frame; level labels alone never rebuild shadow maps, standby maps or texture budgets, nor reapply the Resolution Scale. Any other transaction retains the view's graph (`retainResources`) until its replacement presents: the replaced graph's render targets are freed as it is replaced, so the replacement allocates into their room, and only its tasks and their Effects stay, so identical programs come from the Effect cache. Artistic settings (CEL, IBL, outlines, output size) never mark a category Custom. Tiers are authored targets (Low budget Android, Medium A16, High desktop, Ultra high-end), not qualifications.

## Lighting and shadows

- Lights are data (decided): no light change compiles a shader. A light switching, spawning, moving, starting or stopping its shadows, or entering or leaving the light budget only writes the `HapticLights` block and the shadow atlas. Reason: Babylon bakes each light's type, shadow and filter into a receiver's defines (`LIGHT{i}`, `SHADOW{i}`, `SHADOWPCF{i}`), so each such change was a new program, which iPad WebKit links in seconds and then blocks on at its first draw. The lighting CI gate (`the lighting CI gate` in [`compile-hold-acceptance.test.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/compile-hold-acceptance.test.ts), a merge gate in CI's unit job) holds it: after the first presented frame, lights are switched, spawned, recoloured, moved, removed and pushed past the block's maximum, shadows of each atlas kind are started, moved, stopped and restarted, and a held actor is spawned. Under the synchronous and parallel-compile driver models no step compiles an Effect, and no compiled Effect carries a light define. It runs again with Screen-Space Shadows at High: no compile or graph build, and the same screen-shadow programs throughout.
- One lighting path. There is no render-path setting and no light path chosen per material, Scene or device, and lighting enters a shader only in the lighting function. Every lit surface is a NodeMaterial built from a Material document, except a Sprite or Tilemap with Shading Mode 3D (the unlit Material's 3D-lit variant, below), and engine code creates no lit `PBRMaterial`, `StandardMaterial` or `CelMaterial`. Built-in water draws the water starter. A constant Environment Influence of 0 (a literal or a Float Constant node, never a Parameter; built-in water's) sets `HapticLightingBlock.noEnvironment`, which compiles the environment's light out (no `HAPTIC_ENVIRONMENT`, no cube bound or awaited) instead of multiplying it by zero. Every other lit program contains the environment's light whatever environment lights its mesh: its presence (a scale of zero and a 1 × 1 placeholder cube where there is none: the Engine's empty cube, made from raw data, as a render target created by a draw's bind would unbind its pass's framebuffer on WebGL2), a reflection probe's box projection, the cube's handedness and its encoding (linear, gamma or RGBD) are bound values (`hapticEnvironment`, `hapticEnvironmentForm`) read through uniform branches, so an environment that appears after a Material compiled, a probe's volume a mesh enters or leaves and a cube of another encoding compile nothing. A level's programs (its Materials with the mesh features they draw with, in each pass) are a bounded set, compiled behind its loading screen. Content arriving later compiles while it loads and stays hidden until ready (`reveal-pending.ts`). A settings change (quality level, render mode) may compile.
- The unlit Material ([`unlit-material.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/unlit-material.ts), blocks in [`unlit-material-blocks.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/unlit-material-blocks.ts)) draws the engine's own unlit surfaces in place of Standard materials: Painter 2D, sprites, tilemaps, Text2D bitmap, MSDF and solid glyphs, the overlay textures, panels, buttons, joysticks and UI controls, 3D Text (both sides; its back faces flip the geometry pass's normal, as Standard's `twoSidedLighting` did), the Scene Layer blit (the layer's target over the world, blended, without fog), the skeleton preview, and the editor helpers: billboards, the camera model, collider dashes, volume fills, the nav mesh overlay, the Play console's collision view, transform and shape handles, pivots, pick meshes and the transform gizmos. Editor helpers draw without fog (`role: "helper"`, `fog: false`), deliberately: a fogged handle would fade with distance. The engine creates no `StandardMaterial`; ESLint's `boundary/render-construction` ban keeps it so. It is an engine-assembled NodeMaterial graph, not a Material document, because a document binds one texture per Material.
  - Lit variants (`UnlitMaterialKey.lighting`), for Sprites and Tilemaps only (`drawnAlbedoKey` in `mesh-assets.ts`, from the binding's Shading Mode): `"2d"` multiplies the display-space colour by the 2D light at the pixel's XY (`Light2DBlock`, `unlit-lighting-blocks.ts`); `"3d"` decodes it to linear and lights it as a rough non-metal through `HapticLightingBlock` with the mesh's normal (so it receives shadows, follows CEL and the Unlit viewport), then the linear image processing lit Materials use (a tilemap's 3D variant takes the Scene's, not the atlas's). Variants of the same texturing share their pass variants; each lighting is its own program.
  - One instance per Scene and key (`UnlitMaterialKey`: textured, back-face culling, fog, role (world, helper or preview) and lighting), shared by every mesh with that key. Each mesh binds its texture, colour, alpha, cutoff and blending (`setUnlitMaterial`), read at every draw, and frozen materials rebind them per mesh. Fixed block names make every instance's code identical, so instances with the same texturing share one program (`shareCompiledProgram`), and a binding change compiles nothing.
  - A Text2D MSDF glyph (`UnlitMaterialKey.msdf`) samples its atlas as a distance field: the median distance, antialiased over a screen pixel, fills in the binding's colour and strokes its outline (`UnlitBinding.msdf`: colour and width) around it, blended by that coverage and discarded below 0.01. It keeps the look of the shader it replaced: exact display colours (`imageProcessing: "atlas"`) and no fog. Two things changed with the move: under a Scene Linear stage its colour decodes with the Engine's exact sRGB conversion, as every other surface's does (the shader used a 2.2 power), and it is in the geometry pass by Standard's 0.4 rule, as every blended surface is (the shader drew nothing there). Glyphs of one style share a binding and a batch; without its atlas a glyph draws its solid colour.
  - A sprite or Text2D bitmap glyph is cut out at 0.4 (blended instead while its text fades in or its animation always blends), and a fractional visibility blends the cutout, decided per draw: a crossfade or a texture switch (a PNG for a KTX2, whose texture matrix flips it) compiles nothing. Tilemap chunks draw from both sides without fog, under the atlas's image processing (`UnlitMaterialKey.imageProcessing`: exact display colours, still decoded for a Scene Linear stage). An overlay surface's construction look is an unlit binding its owner reapplies (`applyOverlayUnlitMaterial`), as is a Text Material glyph's fallback (`materialRestore`).
  - Its meshes never join an instance pool group (`perMeshBinding`): a group's instances draw with their source's binding. A pooled visual given an albedo texture leaves the pool for a mesh of its own under the visual, which draws the visual's binding.
  - An instance is disposed only with its Scene: a mesh disposed with its materials (`dispose(false, true)`) leaves it to the other meshes.
  - Babylon's gizmos swap whole materials on their meshes (idle, hover, disabled) and keep them in their axis caches, so each slot gets an instance of its own (`ownedUnlitMaterial`), whose binding every mesh drawing it shares and whose owner disposes it. `styleEditorGizmos` (`gizmo-host.ts`) replaces the slots, the caches and the handle meshes' materials, and gives each instance the `diffuseColor` Babylon copies to line meshes.
  - Shape handles (spline, water and deformer points; [`shape-handles.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shape-handles.ts)) are an invisible hit sphere with the drawn dot as its child. The dot keeps a size relative to the view; the hit sphere is never smaller on screen than `HANDLE_MIN_HIT_PX` (44 CSS pixels, from `ShapeHandlesOptions.viewportCssHeight`), so a short viewport or a narrow field of view cannot shrink a point below a touch target.
  - Alpha follows Standard's rules per mesh (`needAlphaBlendingForMesh`, `needAlphaTestingForMesh`). The mesh's visibility and overlay tint (`overlayVisualStyle`) apply after image processing, as on Standard.
  - It reproduces Standard's unlit pixels: display-space colour × texture × vertex colour, Scene Fog in display space with its distance per vertex, the stock image-processing block, and Standard's geometry-pass encodings (view depth, derivative face normal, no reflectivity, linear velocity, object ID, each scaled by Standard's `writeGeometryInfo`). `e2e/unlit-parity.spec.ts` draws each case with the Standard setup it replaced (with the engine's former Standard fog and reflectivity patches, now test-only in `apps/editor/src/testing/standard-reference.ts`) or, for MSDF glyphs, the former shader (`msdf-reference.ts`), and with the unlit binding.
  - Its outline, capture and shadow-depth variants and its lattice shadow adapter come from `registerAuthoredVariants`, as compiled Surface Materials' do. Its active textures include every bound mesh's texture.
- Babylon `Light` objects stay as inert data sources: gizmos, picking, billboards, admission and the shadow controller read them, and no Material does. A light joining or leaving a mesh's `lightSources` (switched, or entering or leaving the light budget) therefore marks no define of a mesh that only such Materials draw (`installLightFreeMeshes`, a Babylon patch hook); a mesh drawn by any other Material (the skybox's and the Fog Volume guide's PBR Materials, a NodeMaterial with a Babylon light block) is marked as upstream.
- 2D lights ([`lights-2d.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/lights-2d.ts), [`light-2d-group.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/light-2d-group.ts)) are not Babylon lights: each is a record in a per-Scene `Haptic2DLights` block (header: Point count and the Global lights' summed ambient; three vec4s per Point light), rewritten before each frame from the active camera (`pack2DLights`: at most `LIGHT_2D_MAXIMUM` (16) Point lights, nearest first by distance less radius; the rest are limited, the billboard's yellow). No 2D light change compiles, and they take no part in the 3D light block, its budget, clusters or shadows. With the Scene's lights off (the Unlit viewport) the block writes a white ambient. Reason for a separate component over reusing Light: 3D shadow maps cannot cast along the XY plane, and every 3D shadow, budget and culling path would need 2D exceptions.
  - A Scene's Shading Mode 2D surfaces draw the 2D-lit variant only while it is 2D-lit (`setScene2DLighting`, from `applySceneEnvironment`): it holds a Light2DComponent, or in Play its Classes carry or name one in Add Component (`spawnsLights2D`; never assumed for an unseen or pin-fed class, which would darken a Scene that spawns none). Play decides it at load, so the variant compiles behind the loading screen; an editor edit adding the first or removing the last 2D Light rebinds those surfaces (`onScene2DLightingChanged`).
  - Shadows ([`shadows-2d.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shadows-2d.ts)): each of the first `SHADOWED_2D_MAXIMUM` (8) packed Point lights with Cast Shadows takes a row of one R16F map (`SHADOW_2D_BINS` (512) directions, wrapping in U), holding where shadow starts along each direction as a share of its radius; the light function reads it at `atan(offset)` and fades the light over 2% of the radius past it. Rows are CPU rays (`polarShadow2D`) against world outlines of solid 2D colliders: closed outlines union (a ray is shadowed from where it leaves all solid, so a caster's surface is lit), open chains shadow where crossed, and an outline containing the light is skipped. A redraw sorts each in-reach caster segment into the directions whose ray can cross it (`segmentBins`: the bins between its two ends as seen from the light, one bin of margin either side, and the bins round the segment's own direction; every bin for a segment whose ends are opposite across the light, non-finite or beyond `BUCKETED_REACH`), so a direction tests only those segments, in caster then segment order: the ring is the one testing every segment for every direction gives, which `shadows-2d.test.ts` checks against that reference. A row redraws only when its light changes or moves or a caster whose old or new bounds reach it moves (`changedBounds`); casters pose lazily (`Occluder2DRecord`), only while a shadowed light needs them, from the Scene's set of changed casters (`dirtyOccluders`, filled by `markOccluderDirty`, the only writer of a record's `dirty`), not by looking at every caster each frame. The map is always bound (every 2D-lit program declares its sampler), allocated through the ledger when it has room. Casters: Play's runtime sends `setOccluders2D` per body slot (`emitActorOccluders2D`, from `RuntimePhysicsWorlds.occluders2D` over the main 2D world's published colliders, `occluder2DShapes` in `@haptic/physics`), on a collider set change (`PhysicsColliderSets` `onPublished`) and with the actor's visual; poses use the slot root's translation and Z rotation, never scale, which the outlines carry. The editor builds them from the document (`authored-occluders-2d.ts`, mirroring the 2D world's Collider, Sprite box and Tilemap chains) only in a 2D-lit Scene with a 2D physics world.
  - Editor: `syncAuthoredIlluminationSteps` keeps one `Light2DGroup` per actor posed from its authored world, and gizmo drags pose it from the live mesh (`syncAuthoredAreaLightsFromMeshes`). Play: the runtime sends `setLights2D` per slot (`emitActorLights2D`, none for Scene Layer actors), and `snapshot-apply.ts` poses each group from its slot root or bone attachment.
- Falloff is physical for every surface. Not supported, because no Material document or the glTF loader can turn them on (the loader registers no `KHR_materials_*` extension but unlit):
  - specular colour, IES profiles, projection textures, lightmap modes and falloff types;
  - clear coat, sheen, iridescence, subsurface, anisotropy and refraction;
  - shadowed rect area lights.
- Shadow atlas decisions:
  - It is one atlas of tiles of different sizes, not a layer of equal size per map, to keep memory within the mobile limits; filtering stays inside each tile.
  - Its texture is a depth texture array of four pages, each half the edge ([`shadow-atlas.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shadow-atlas.ts), `shadowAtlasPageEdge`; one page of the whole edge for an atlas made for a tile that wide). A tile lies in one page and a pass binds only its page, so on a tile-based GPU a pass loads and stores a quarter of the atlas, not all of it. A page holding only the tiles a render draws is cleared whole by its first pass (a clear load action: nothing loaded) and its other passes clear nothing; any other tile clears under a scissor. Consecutive tile passes on one page are one render pass: a tile pass ends without Babylon's default framebuffer bind while its render has a tile to come, and in a shadow stage (`holdShadowAtlasPages`, opened by `ManagedShadowsTask` around its maps) after its render's last tile too, so a sun's later cascades and a spot light's tile on the same page share it; the next pass on that page binds and attaches nothing (`AtlasTileTarget._bindFrameBuffer`), and one on another page only attaches that page. ANGLE's Metal backend ends its render encoder at any change of the draw framebuffer binding or its attachments, each a load and store of the whole page. Babylon's caches stay true (`_currentFramebuffer` is the atlas's while a page is held), so any other bind through the Engine binds for real and ends the hold; only tile passes bind the atlas framebuffer, so the held page is what it has attached. Between two maps of a stage nothing binds or draws (readiness probes do not bind; a camera's `outputRenderTarget`, which a stage binds before each map, ends the hold). The stage's `release` binds the default framebuffer where a page is still held; `ShadowAtlasDrawCounts.passes` counts the page binds against `tiles`. The pages hold the tiles one quadtree over the whole edge would, in the same places (`ShadowAtlasAllocator`), so admission and tile sizes do not depend on the paging.
  - The light block and the volumetric march sample it through the same units as one atlas (`sampler2DArrayShadow`, and `sampler2DArray` for Contact Hardening), a view's record holding its tile in page coordinates and its page: a texture per map or per tile size would take two units each and push lit Materials with several textures past WebGL2's 16. ESSL 3.00 has no `textureLod` for an array compare sampler, so a tap is `textureGrad` with zero gradients: an explicit level 0, as before. An implicit level (`texture`) would depend on derivatives, which the light loop's non-uniform control flow leaves undefined.
