ANGLE's D3D11 backend emits `SampleCmpLevelZero` for the array tap; for the flat atlas's `textureLod` it emitted `SampleCmp`, an implicit level, so on that backend PCF taps on lit surfaces differ from the flat atlas by up to 20/255 (the flat atlas's partial self-shadowing grain is gone).
  - A point light is six compare tiles filtered with PCF, so there are no cube maps and no Poisson fallback.
  - Contact Hardening (PCSS) reads the same depth texture without compare: through a WebGL2 sampler object (a Babylon patch hunk). No colour copy of depth exists, and no colour attachment.
  - Not supported, as nothing uses them: transparency shadows, frustum edge falloff, darkness as a setting, and the cascade debug define.
- Light culling: the light block's point, spot and rect loops visit only the lights of the surface's light cluster, in record order, so culled and unculled images are the same; a range test still skips those that cannot reach the pixel. Per-mesh lists fail on large surfaces and instanced batches. The clusters ([`light-clusters.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/light-clusters.ts)) cut the rendering camera's view into 16×8 tiles and 15 exponential depth slices, each with two mask words (point lights; spot lights in bits 0-15, rect lights in 16-19). A rect light's record is its plane: the shader's rect light is exactly zero behind its emitting side (`hapticLtcEvaluate`), so a cluster wholly behind it (every corner of its frustum piece, or box under an orthographic view, and every edge direction of the unbounded last slice, by a tolerance far wider than the shader's float32 rounding) drops it; a projection of another form keeps it everywhere. CEL's rect loop visits every rect light: a zero light still moves its highlight signal. They live in a separate `HapticClusters` uniform block of 15456 bytes, inside WebGL2's 16 KiB minimum, with no sampler, which every lit graph Material binds. The header holds the view-projection they were built with, so a surface projects its own world position to find its cluster, and a position outside them (another camera's or a mirror's view) reads every light. The masks are built from the drawing view's current matrices, never by computing a camera's: each pass's first lit draw compares the view and projection, the active camera, its depth range, the floating origin and the local lights as last written (`writeHapticLights` counts a change only when a light's cluster record differs) with the last build's, and builds and uploads only when one differs, since the buffer already holds what a build from the same inputs writes; so a view's several lit passes build once, and a still view with still lights none. The masks are conservative: each cluster is tested against a point or spot light's sphere, a rect light's plane (`light-clusters.test.ts` checks random rects against the shader's float32 plane test); a build computes the slices' depths and the tiles' edges once, not per cluster tested. `e2e/light-loop.spec.ts` checks per pixel on the Feature Test's lights grid, through a proof-only define (`HAPTIC_LIGHT_LOOP_PROOF`, set for a Scene by `setHapticLightLoopProof`: the lit graph Materials draw the visited, in-range and in-cluster counts instead of their lighting), that the loops visit their cluster's lights and every light in range among them, and that the lit grid matches its unculled draw (`setHapticLightCulling`).
- Device profile ([`device-profile.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/device-profile.ts)): the one place render code tells device classes apart (`isMobileGpuDevice`: an iPhone, iPad, iPod or Android user agent, or a Mac one with touch, which is how iPadOS reports, in every host). `deviceProfile()` (classed once per navigator) gives the class's caps: the shadow atlas edge ceiling and the texture cache ceiling. Phones and tablets get the desktop's shadow map sizes and light limits (`HAPTIC_LIGHT_MAXIMUM`). Consumers read the profile, never the user agent.
- The texture cache ceiling (768 MiB on phones and tablets, the Medium level's budget; `TEXTURE_BYTE_CEILING` elsewhere) bounds whatever budget a quality level, the Engine Settings or a view sets (`ResourceCache.effectiveCeiling`), and `deviceTextureLimits` reports `textures: at most … on this device class` in `qualityLimits` when a level asks for more. It only trims unreferenced textures sooner; nothing a Scene holds is evicted.
- Light admission ([`light-policy.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/light-policy.ts), [`scene-lighting.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/scene-lighting.ts)) is the light block's selection (`selectHapticLights`), within the scalability local-light budget: sun/fill first, then priority, camera distance with incumbent bonus, stable identity. Exclusion never changes authored Enabled. Graph Materials hold no light defines, so no light count or capacity is part of an Effect key. A Scene without lights (a Scene Layer) writes its empty block once; its later syncs skip admission and the write until a light or Material joins or `lightsEnabled` or `shadowsEnabled` changes.
- `HapticLightingBlock` ([`haptic-lighting-block.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/haptic-lighting-block.ts), [`haptic-lighting-shader.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/haptic-lighting-shader.ts)) is the one lighting path (every shipped Engine has uniform buffers, and the Babylon unit tests give every NullEngine them, `test-support/light-block-engine.ts`). `material-compiler.ts` builds every lit surface with it: Haptic's own metallic-roughness function (GGX, height-correlated Smith, Schlick, Lambert, the split-sum environment BRDF table with multi-scatter compensation, horizon occlusion, Babylon's pre-scaled irradiance harmonics, box projection, the box relative to the floating origin as the surface positions are) over every light of one per-Scene `HapticLights` uniform block ([`haptic-lights.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/haptic-lights.ts), written each scene-lighting sync (a view's readiness syncs before shadow admission and again only when that admission changed a map's allocation or caster participation, which `SceneShadowController.sync` returns; the frame draws with its `onBeforeRender` sync's write) from the lights its admission left on (`selectHapticLights`, Forward's selection hook: ranked as Forward ranks them, then admitted per type up to `HAPTIC_LIGHT_MAXIMUM`, on every device, and within the scalability local lights; one fill; the rest are forward-excluded like budget-limited lights, yellow billboard included, and listed in `sceneLightingLimits`), and bound to each draw's Effect directly: a `UniformBuffer` bound through `bindToEffect` rebinds to its recorded Effect on every update, and the block updates between draws). It is no Babylon light block, so its Materials hold no light defines. A lit surface's light function (`hapticDirectLights`, CEL's `hapticCelLighting`) has one loop for everything that reads the shadow atlas: two slots for the sun's cascade and the next one it blends into (the directional lights are summed after the second), then the cluster's point lights, then its spot lights, each looking its view up through the loop's single `hapticShadow` call (`hapticSunCascade` and `hapticPointShadowView` choose the view; -1 for none). Direct3D's compiler inlines every call and each copy of the filter adds over a second to a lit program's link, so the function keeps one call site (`haptic-lighting-shader.test.ts`); contributions are summed in the order the separate loops summed them. Point and spot lights fall off with the inverse square, windowed to zero at their range; a spot's cone is glTF's (`KHR_lights_punctual`): full inside its Inner Angle, zero past its Outer Angle, the cosine's linear step squared between (the inner angle's cosine sits in the spot record beside its shadow's first view), as the volumetric march's cone is, so a spot lights nothing outside its shadow's view. Environment Influence and Specular (a non-metal's F0 = 0.08 × Specular) are block inputs. CEL is the block's `HAPTIC_CEL` define, set from the Scene's render mode, so a CEL Material is the same graph (`followRenderMode` marks it dirty on a mode change, frozen or not): `cel-shader.ts`'s ramp, light mixing and hard highlights over the same records and falloffs, its own polynomial irradiance (`bindCelSettings`), and the display colour converted to linear so emissive, fog and the display conversion are PBR's. A highlight shows where its light's own brightness reaches the first lit band and takes its light's hue, not its intensity; the Material's Roughness scales its strength after the anti-aliased step (full up to 0.5, none at 1), the one surface input CEL reads besides Base Color, Normal and Environment Influence (Metallic and Specular stay PBR's); the fill is ambient, added after the ramp. `bindCelSettings` derives the ramp's exponent, lit threshold and highlight edges on the CPU and binds once per Effect, Scene and Engine frame (again on a settings, lights or environment change). CEL programs compile none of the PBR lighting and declare neither the split-sum table nor the atlas's plain read. A Scene with its lights disabled (the Unlit viewport) writes no records. Rect area lights are records of their centre and half extents, shaded with Babylon's LTC tables (retained for the Scene while the block exists; the block samples them stacked in one 64 × 128 texture, `areaLightLtcTexture`, LTC1's rows then LTC2's, each half ending at its last texel's centre so bilinear taps never cross, while Babylon's native `_ltcTextures` slot keeps the 64 × 64 pair its RectAreaLight and native materials read) and the standard LTC Fresnel; the block's fixed fragment samplers are six: environment cube, split-sum table, the stacked LTC tables, the area emission array, and the atlas compared and read plainly, plus the screen shadows under `HAPTIC_SCREEN_SHADOWS` (a CEL program declares three: no cube, table or plain read); CEL evaluates them at roughness one half, reading the LTC table only for highlights, as Babylon's light block does, and the fill's presence is `hapticFillDirection.w`. Their prepared emissions are layers of one always-bound 2D texture array ([`area-emission-array.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/area-emission-array.ts)): the first four (`AREA_EMISSION_LAYERS`) distinct emissions among the admitted rect lights, rebuilt (an upload) when that set changes, the layer in each record; a light past the cap draws its flat colour and is listed in `sceneLightingLimits`. `AreaRectLightOwner` registers its pixels (`setRectLightEmission`) and keeps no texture of its own. Spot lights' shadows, point lights' cube faces and the sun's cascades are tiles of the Scene's shadow atlas ([`shadow-atlas.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shadow-atlas.ts)): one depth-only target (4 bytes per texel: its depth texture is its only depth attachment, `generateDepthBuffer: false`, as Babylon's WebGL depth texture otherwise also allocates an unused depth renderbuffer of its size), made with its first tile and released with its last (except as its Scene is disposed: an empty atlas then parks on its Engine, `ParkedAtlas`, and the next Scene to ask for an atlas of its edge and paging takes its framebuffer and depth texture over behind a texture of its own, allocating nothing and leaving no fresh texture to be used for the first time (on ANGLE D3D11 such a first use showed a transient of about the atlas's size), as at every overlay Play start and stop, where the editor's Scene and Play's follow each other; nothing samples the depth it brings, as every map clears its tiles before its first draw and is sampled neutrally until then; a Scene whose shadow sync asks for no shadow (a Scene without lights runs none, so after one only the timer frees it), `PARKED_SHADOW_ATLAS_MS` without a taker, a context loss or the Engine's disposal frees it, a Scene asking for another edge frees it a frame before it makes its own, and no ledger counts it), packed with square power-of-two tiles by a quadtree ([`shadow-atlas-allocator.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shadow-atlas-allocator.ts)). Its level edge (`shadowAtlasEdge`) is the largest power of two whose depth fits the shadow quality level's byte budget (Low and Medium 4096, 64 MiB; High and Ultra 8192, 256 MiB), within `shadowAtlasEdgeLimit`: `SHADOW_ATLAS_MAX_EDGE` (16384), the device's texture limit and `DeviceProfile.shadowAtlasCap` (8192 on phones and tablets, as WKWebView kills a page past its memory limit; that is every level's own edge, so High and Ultra are 8192 there too; null elsewhere). A planned sun grows it (`sunShadowAtlasEdge`, called by `planShadowAdmission`, whose `atlasEdge` the controller, allocation keys and views use) to the smallest power of two up to that limit that holds the sun's cascade tiles at its requested map size, or the smaller one a failed allocation left it (`ShadowEntry.recovery`); where none holds them, to the one that holds them at the largest smaller size, to which admission halves the map (an 8192 map falls back to 4096 where the device makes no 16384 atlas, reported as any reduction is: `shadow map reduced by shadow atlas space`, `allocationDownsized`). Local maps and a level's own sizes never grow it. Decided: a level's byte budget is admission policy sizing the atlas for its own maps, not a device limit; a Directional Map Size past what that atlas holds is the author's explicit choice, honoured where the device allows (an 8192 map: a 16384 atlas, 1 GiB). The shadow controller admits these maps by the atlas's area (`ShadowAdmissionPlan.atlasEdge`; a tile needs no material sampler, one past the area is `budget-limited` for `shadow atlas space`, and the sun, admitted first, leaves room for the local maps planned into the atlas at the level's size, six tiles for a point light, but at most half the atlas, so local maps past it halve or wait rather than the sun's shadow), and the atlas takes its bytes from the budget once it holds a tile, leaving the rest to the other maps; an allocation key carries the atlas's edge, so a quality level of another edge (or a sun's growth coming or going) rebuilds every atlas map: the sync that plans another edge than the live atlas's (`liveShadowAtlasEdge`) releases every map in it, standby maps too (no planned map can reuse them, and they would keep it alive), and the atlas with them, and the planned maps wait `budget-limited` for `shadow atlas resizing` until a sync in a later Engine frame (`engine.frameId`) builds the new atlas, so the old and the new never live in the same frame (a GPU process may free storage only once a frame's commands finish); the scene view draws that frame without shadows, but a manual colour Render Target capture (Fast or a capture view) keeps its request through it (`SceneShadowController.atlasResizing`), as it would keep an image drawn with no maps; a tile the packing cannot place fails its allocation, which frees standby maps or halves the tile as any failed allocation does. Casters' depth variants are asked for ahead instead (`ShadowCasterVariants`, [`shadow-caster-variants.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shadow-caster-variants.ts), from Play's world presentation loading frames through `SceneShadowController.warmCasterVariants`, after the starting state has posed the casters, since a variant reads a caster's scaling): a map of each kind of depth program (the sun's cascades; a local light's, which a spot light's tile and a point light's faces share: `AtlasShadowGenerator` asks a caster for the same defines for both, without Babylon's light-class define, which no shader reads, and without a point light's distance output, a colour the depth-only atlas has no attachment for), built as an admitted map is (`createDetachedShadowGenerator` in `shadow-map-factory.ts`) on a stand-in light outside the Scene (`dontAddToScene`; a spot light for the local kind) with tiles in a private 256-texel atlas of the atlas's format (`createPrivateShadowAtlas`, `placeInPrivateAtlas`; out of the Scene's texture list, leased in the ledger), calls its readiness check for every caster in its first pass, whatever the light's reach; the controller's syncs call it again only for the casters it found not ready (the map's shaders loading, an Effect compiling, a depth wrapper's variant Material not yet ready, which the first call compiles), so a caster that never becomes ready costs one check per sync. Never drawn, the maps keep those Effects (in the casters' draw wrappers, or their depth wrappers) while they live, so a first shadow start of a kind compiles none; new shadow settings build them again, a lost context or the Scene disposes them. A caster that arrives later with a variant no caster had compiles it at its first cast. A spot or point light's map is an `AtlasShadowGenerator` (a point light's six tiles one per cube face, in Babylon's order +X, -X, -Y, +Y, +Z, -Z, each a 90-degree view drawn and sampled as a spot light's is, filtered with PCF where Babylon's own cube maps fall back to Poisson sampling) and the sun's an `AtlasCascadedShadowGenerator` (Babylon's cascaded generator, built with the level's cascade count, one included, a tile per cascade; the level's last `SHADOW_HALF_TILE_CASCADES` cascades, never the first two, take tiles of half the map size's edge, down to 256, and its first cascade a tile of `ShadowSettings.firstCascadeScale` times the edge (First Cascade Detail: 1 at every level, 2 a Custom project or scene choice for sharper near shadows, whose texels are largest on screen; an authored setting, not a level constant, so it is in the allocation key, the allocation request key and the admission plan's kept-size check, and a change rebuilds the sun map as a map size change does), the scale halved while that tile would be wider than half the atlas's edge, never below the map size (rule: the first tile takes at most a quarter of the atlas, so 2 applies to maps up to 1024 in Low and Medium's 4096 atlas and up to 2048 in High and Ultra's 8192 one, and at 8192 every level's cascades leave three 4096 quadrants, room for the level's local lights as spot maps; point lights past that halve the sun by the local reserve), over the same split, `cascadeTileSizes`, and each cascade's fit snaps to its own tile's texels; the generator's `mapSize` stays the map size, which admission, recovery and diagnostics read, while its first tile may be larger; a pass's bias, normal offset and smallest far caster use its own tile, `shadowTileSize`, and admission, the allocation key and a capture's mirrored cascades count those tiles): Babylon's generator and caster drawing, with its own render target object sharing the atlas's framebuffer, a pass per tile, each pass's viewport set to its tile after the bind and its clear scissored to the tile. A spot record names its shadow view in the block, a point record its first view and face count (the block picks the face by the position's dominant axis from the light), and the sun's record marks the shadowed sun, whose cascades are the first four views (`hapticShadowViews`: per tile, the view-projection it was last drawn with, relative to the floating origin, the tile, the darkness, which stays neutral until the map has drawn, the filter and its quality, whether it is a cascade, whose depth clamps, receiver-plane bias, and Contact Hardening's terms: a perspective view's near and far planes or a cascade's corrections toward the first cascade (Babylon's `_lightSizeUVCorrection` and `_depthCorrection`, from the extents it was drawn with), and the light's size). The shadow functions are one source in two variants (`hapticShadowGlsl`): the surfaces', and one bilinear compare tap for a position on no surface, the volumetric march's. Each atlas map records its views as it draws and the block's views are written again once it has (`watchShadowDraws`), so a map drawn after the block's write is sampled with the views it was drawn with. A shadow pass (`ManagedShadowsTask`) draws its maps inside `coalesceShadowDraws`: nothing between its maps samples their views (each draws casters' depth only), so the block takes them once after its last map, before anything draws with them; a map drawn on its own (a reflection's `drawViewMaps`) still writes them as it draws. The block is a static `UniformBuffer`: Babylon compares each write with the values it holds and uploads only when one changed, so a still Scene's syncs and maps upload nothing (`haptic-lights.test.ts`); it must not become dynamic, which uploads on every `update`. The sun's views are those of the map the rendering camera samples: its own in the atlas (a High or Full Quality capture's, or a planar reflection's under Reflection Shadows, `attachView`), else the scene view's, except that a planar reflection's camera (`isReflectionCamera`, [`reflection-camera.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/reflection-camera.ts): `PlanarMirrorCamera` marks itself) without one gets no cascades (count 0, so `hapticSunShadowFactor` and the screen-shadow darkness are 1: the sun lights its receivers unshadowed, the sun record keeping its shadowed mark, so no define changes): the scene view's cascades are fitted to the scene camera and chosen by its view depth, so they would miss or misplace what only the reflection shows (behind or beside the camera), in a pattern that moves with the camera. They are written again when the active camera changes and the map it samples differs (`onActiveCameraChanged`, which the capture's view fires as it starts and ends, and `drawPlanarMirrorPass` as a reflection pass does: Babylon's object renderer swaps the camera without notice), so the capture or reflection draws with its cascades, or none, and the scene view after it with its own. Spot and point maps are view-independent and every view samples the scene view's. The block's buffer is not dynamic: a static uniform buffer, which a view drawing with its own sun map writes again between draws. The block samples a view with Castaño's exact bilinear compare kernels ([`shadow-filter.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shadow-filter.ts)): 3 × 3 texels (four taps) at Low, 5 × 5 (nine) at Medium, 7 × 7 (sixteen) at High, each tap kept a texel inside the tile. Softness holds in world units: each view's record carries its texel ratio (`shadowViewTexelRatios`: the first cascade's world texel over a cascade's, from its extents and tile; a local light's requested map size, `filterReferenceSize`, over its admitted tile), and the block takes the exact kernel nearest the quality's width times that ratio (`shadowKernelTexels`: a coarser far cascade or downsized tile filters with 5 × 5 or 3 × 3); Low always takes 3 × 3, since a single bilinear tap steps with the texels wherever a texel spans several pixels (up close). Each shadow quality level sets its filter quality (`SHADOW_PROFILES`): Low the 3 × 3; Medium, High and Ultra the 7 × 7. Decided: discrete kernels, not one kernel stretched to a fractional width, which loses Castaño's exact weights (the edge dips and steps). No noise and no temporal accumulation. `e2e/shadow-filter.spec.ts` holds a diagonal edge across texels about five pixels wide to a monotonic ramp with a straight contour at Low, Medium and High. Applied for a mesh that receives shadows, a light whose `shadowEnabled` is on and a Scene whose `shadowsEnabled` is. A view whose map uses Contact Hardening runs Babylon's PCSS on its tile (`hapticContactLit`): a blocker search of the atlas's depth over the light's size, then bilinear compare taps over a radius that grows with the blockers' distance, on Babylon's Poisson discs turned per position; a perspective view compares Babylon's depth metric, (clip z + near) / (near + far), recovering the blockers' from their stored depth through the view's planes. With receiver-plane bias (a cascade's under automatic bias) the search reads and taps compare against the receiver's plane, as PCF does, where Babylon's PCSS lets a flat receiver shadow itself. CEL draws Contact Hardening as PCF. The search reads the depth without compare, through a second uniform on the same depth texture (`hapticShadowAtlasDepth`, bound with `setDepthStencilTexture`), whose unit the patched WebGL engine gives a nearest sampler object without compare (`_samplerObjectFor`, installed per Engine by the block and made again after a lost context), every other unit none. `e2e/haptic-contact-shadows.spec.ts` checks a spot light's, the sun's and a point light's against ray-cast masks of their blockers, and CEL's against its PCF. For the sun it chooses the cascade as Babylon does (the first whose far end lies beyond the position's view depth from the camera the cascades were drawn for), blends into the next over the cascade's last `cascadeBlendPercentage`, and fades out over the shadow distance's last `fadeFraction`; with automatic bias each tap compares against the receiver's plane, from the position's screen derivatives taken before any branch. The block holds 52 views (four cascades, then 48 local tiles); a light whose views do not fit draws unshadowed. `e2e/render-target-quality.spec.ts` checks a Full Quality capture far from the scene camera against the scene view at its pose. Native materials keep Babylon's path.
- Only the first enabled directional light in scene order illuminates.
- `SceneShadowController` ([`shadow-controller.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shadow-controller.ts)) owns all shadow maps in editor and Play, including the per-view sun maps of High and Full Quality captures (`attachView`); the managed bridge renders them unmanaged before `Forward objects`. Local maps render once and refresh on caster revision ([`shadow-map-refresh.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shadow-map-refresh.ts)); a caster whose content can change without a tracked revision (skinning, morphs, updatable geometry, render hooks, alpha, unknown shaders) keeps every map it reaches drawing each frame. Not unknown: a mesh with neither geometry nor parts (it draws nothing; the geometry it gets later is tracked), a landscape chunk drawing its own geometry (`hasRevisionTrackedLandscapeGeometry`: sculpting rewrites its updatable vertex buffers through notified updates, never its indices), a mesh whose owner writes its updatable buffers only through notified updates (`hasOwnerNotifiedGeometry`: a sprite quad's resizes and frames, an animated tilemap chunk's UVs), static thin instances (`hasStaticThinInstances`: non-updatable matrix and attribute buffers with automatic recreation off, so placements change only by a new buffer or count, which the caster snapshot tracks), a shared material's lattice-cage shadow wrapper for a mesh without an active cage (`isCageOnlyShadowWrapper`: the wrapper compiles that mesh with `SLATE_LATTICE` off, its own geometry; it counts once the compiler keeps the wrapper for every mesh, a non-identity WPO or masked graph), a mesh whose only deformation is a lattice cage that widened its bounds to the cage's hull (`LatticeBinding.widens`, `canCacheShadowMesh`; an instance only under its source's cage: what it draws follows the cage's `revision()` (every `refresh`: controls, parts, materials, activity; and its root's world matrix taking another value), the mesh's world matrix and its geometry, all tracked, and its bounds hold it, so it counts as deforming within its bounds (`deformsWithinBounds`) and is culled and tracked per cascade by them; the walk (`refreshShadowMaps`) flushes the cages first (`flushSceneLatticeDeformers`), so a root moved after the cages' own before-render observer still has its bounds current; a part the cage has not gathered yet, or a cage on a skinned, morphed or padded mesh, stays unknown), and a compiled surface the compiler rebuilt from the same plan for a loaded texture (`rebuildWhenReady` re-registers its new build id; any other rebuild still revokes it). An `InstancedMesh` caches as its source would: its own pose and visibility, its source's geometry, sub-meshes, deformers, material and hooks (`canCacheShadowMaterial` in [`shadow-material-policy.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shadow-material-policy.ts)), and an instance of a source with levels of detail keeps refreshing; a source with instances caches too. Being an instance, or having instances, does not by itself count as unknown content (an instance is posed by its own world matrix, drawn with its source's parts, and a map culls those parts whole for a mesh with instances): otherwise every map they reach would draw each frame. Point/spot maps (`AtlasShadowGenerator`) move to another light of their kind (`moveTo`) or stand by instead of reallocating: a move or a standby return spares the atlas map a new tile allocation, render target, pass ids and caster draw wrappers on each admission change. A move keeps receivers' light order (`exchangeShadowReceivers` swaps the two lights in `scene.lights` and receivers' `lightSources`), which the light block's records follow. A map stands by whether the camera rejected its light (outside the relevant area, budget-limited) or the light block's selection left it out (`isForwardLightExcluded`): releasing it disposes the generator, and readmission would rebuild every caster's shadow draw wrappers (recompiling depth Effects that left the cache) and change the managed shadow task's generator set. Standby maps stay bound, so that set does not change. A new map starts neutral like a moved or resumed one (`neutralUntilRendered`), and its darkness returns to 0 when its last face first draws. Shadow maps keep anisotropy 1. Helpers (`isEditorHelperMesh`), skyboxes, debug overlays and water never cast. The controller keeps the caster registry, the public API and the apply half of `sync` (releases, map moves, standby, construction, the live reservation); beside it, `shadow-entry.ts` holds the per-light record and its allocation-key and cost helpers, `shadow-admission-plan.ts` the pure admission planner (`planShadowAdmission`: ranking, residency, the sun first, count, pass and memory budgets; `applyShadowAdmission` writes its decisions back), `shadow-map-factory.ts` map construction and instrumentation (`createShadowGenerator`: defines, bias, culled caster lists, draw counters; `createDetachedShadowGenerator`: a map no admission counts), and `shadow-view-maps.ts` the per-view sun maps (`ShadowViewMaps`). A sync keeps the last sync's effective settings object (`effectiveShadowSettings`) while the requested settings (replaced on a change, never changed in place), the Engine's cascade support and the render mode are the same, and compares settings with a standby map's, a moved map's and the caster variants' maps by a key made once per settings object (`shadowSettingsKey` in `shadow-entry.ts`), so a steady sync serialises none; its plan reuses the last plan's decision objects. Decided: no early-out for an admission sync whose inputs match the last one's: a Play frame's readiness sync and its `onBeforeRender` sync have animation and the camera update between them, so their inputs differ, and a key covering every input the plan reads (each light's state, request, priority, range, failed allocation and position, the camera, the budget, residency expiry) costs what the plan does. The controller reserves the scene's shadow bytes itself. `shadow-controller.ts` re-exports their public names.
- A component's Cast Shadows resolves through `componentCastsShadows` (`@haptic/core` `shadows.ts`): its authored value, else its class default, off for the unlit Sprite, Tilemap and 3D Text. The editor applies it with `applyComponentShadowParticipation`; Play carries it on each part, or on `assignMesh` itself for a one-visual actor's optimized mesh, which has no parts.
- Custom-depth casters (authored WPO, masked and lattice materials use a `ShadowDepthWrapper`) share depth Effects: the patched wrapper keys each depth Effect by its source and defines, not a per-(subMesh, generator) random token, so every caster of one graph, including its private instances, shares one Effect per depth-define set.
- The caster walk (`ShadowMapRefresh.syncCasters`) reads a caster (`walkCaster`, the one reading of every value its shadow depends on) only when something may have changed it since its last walk. What casters share is read once per walk and gives each caster one revision to compare: a Material's depth and culling state, a source mesh's geometry, parts, deformers and thin instances (its own caster's and each instance's), and which of a geometry's buffers are updatable. A caster itself reads its world matrix, enabled state, visibility, shadow distance cull and Material identity. Each frame the walk:
  - asks every caster for its world matrix and compares the matrix object and its `updateFlag`: Babylon recomputes a child of a moved ancestor only when asked, so this keeps a moved subtree's shadows in the same frame, and it is the only write point of a frozen matrix an owner rewrites in place;
  - reads the Scene's render change journal ([`render-change-journal.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/render-change-journal.ts)) for enabled, visibility and Material writes, a caster's own or an ancestor's, and Haptic-owned changes (`RenderChange.Other`, such as `setShadowDistanceCulled`);
  - reads per caster billboard mode, infinite distance, an instance's lattice cage, a caged caster's cage revision and whether it is an automatic-LOD master (one field or lookup each, none with a write point), and the drawn level of each visible automatic-LOD caster (it follows the camera); a source's poll reads its own cage and its revision;
  - polls each distinct Material, then each distinct source mesh, in use once (`materialState`, `sourceState`): parts, thin-instance buffers, levels of detail, render observers, vertex alpha and a Material's depth, culling and transparency state have no write point. A source's poll compares typed fields with its last read, allocating nothing. Two of its inputs are read only when they can differ: which of a geometry's buffers are updatable, after `setVerticesBuffer`, `removeVerticesData`, `setIndices` or `setIndexBuffer` (`geometryBufferLayout`; a buffer's updatability is fixed when it is made, and `removeVerticesData` notifies nothing), and a Material's part of `canCacheShadowMaterial` (`canCacheShadowMaterialFor`), once per Material, walk and vertex-alpha value: only exact PBR, Standard and Node Materials pass it, whose alpha tests read nothing else of a fully visible mesh. A change walks every caster using it; a source found with another geometry is left to those casters' walks, which watch the geometry first;
  - walks every caster when it is new, when the Scene's default Material was replaced, or when this reader fell behind the journal.
  A caster whose only observed change is its world matrix reads that alone (`moveCaster`): the journal and this walk's polls vouch for everything else its full read would find, so a move changes only its transform revision, the first value of its snapshot, and the revision and flags are the full read's. A caster whose class redefines a hooked accessor (`journalFollows` false: a Gaussian splat part proxy's `isVisible`, a line mesh's `material`) is walked every frame. Every 32nd walk the full walk follows and heals what the journal walk missed (`rendererWork.shadowJournal.heals`, `missedKinds`); `verifyRenderChangeJournal` (`__hapticPlayTest.verifyRenderChangeJournal`, `HAPTIC_PERF_VERIFY_JOURNAL`) runs it after every walk and counts `mismatches`. `visited` and `moved` against `casters` measure the walk's gain; `sourceChanges`, `cacheChecks` and `bufferChecks` count the polls that did real work. The full walk reads every kept answer again (which buffers are updatable, whatever the layout says; the Material part is kept for one walk only) and counts a stale one as missed (`bufferLayout`). A new value goes in the record of whatever owns it; a value with no write point is polled at the coarsest owner, never trusted to a hand-placed mark.
- The walk logs each caster it found changed, moved, new or removed, once per walk (`ShadowMapRefresh.forEachChangeSince`, `changeCursor`; a stage further behind than the log's 16 384 entries reads every caster), and the later stages of the frame read that set instead of every caster. What they keep is read again in full in a frame whose walk ran the full path (`stageCheck`: every 32nd, or each while verifying), which counts each kept answer that read disagrees with under `heals` or `mismatches` and heals it.
  - `ShadowCasterList.prepare` keeps each caster's shown state and world box from frame to frame and reads again only the casters added since and those the walk listed: a box and the shown state change only with a move or a walked change, and whether bounds can be trusted only with a change the walk reads. Its check compares every kept entry (`preparedCasters`). Counters: `prepared`, `prepareScans`.
  - A local map keeps the casters in its range (a spot light's: also within its shadow cone) with the revision and drawn level it saw (`applyLocal`), updated from the listed casters and from those it reads every frame (unsafe ones, and automatic levels of detail, which follow the camera); the light's snapshot holds the light alone, and a change of it, a log it fell behind, or a refresh that was not a local one reads every caster. Its check compares the kept casters and the boxes it holds of those it did not read (`localCasters`); a heal redraws everywhere. Counters: `localVisited`, `localScans`.
  - A cascaded map whose snapshot already changed before its caster loop (the view moved, a refit, a setting) redraws everywhere that frame whatever its casters did, so `trackCascades` leaves its caster bookkeeping out and forgets what the map saw (`cascadeSkips`). The next frame without such a change reads the casters again: when the change log holds nothing since the last skipped frame (that frame drew every caster, with this frame's camera and fit) it notes them without adding where and keeps the map's depth; otherwise the incomplete snapshot redraws it once, with every caster as they are. Frames with the full check never skip. So the frame the view stops is exact, and at most one extra redraw of the same depth follows a skip when a caster outside the cascades changed.
  - Per cascade, `trackCascades` also snapshots the casters that cascade draws (its rectangle and texel rule; thin-instance batches and deforming casters count in every cascade, as the caster lists draw them) and reports the cascades whose casters changed, entered or left or change on their own, or all of them for a change before the casters (`cascadeChanges`). An atlas sun map takes that mask (`AtlasCascadedShadowGenerator.refreshCascades`); any other reset of its object renderer's refresh counter (a pass that skipped a caster not ready, a new refresh rate set elsewhere, a debug switch) marks every cascade. A render in the same Engine frame as the refresh keeps each unmarked cascade that was drawn whole (`keepsCascade`): its pass binds and clears nothing and lists no caster (`takesKeptPass`), and its tile keeps depth drawn with the same fit, camera, light, settings and casters, which a redraw would reproduce. A render with no refresh in its frame draws every cascade.
  - Still on the full path: each map layer's caster list (`getCustomRenderList`, whose cascade planes move with the view) and the retained casters' `take`, whose first layer of a frame describes each caster.
- A caster that draws nothing (`castsNoShadowDepth` in [`shadow-mesh-policy.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shadow-mesh-policy.ts): `drawsNoShadow`, a plain Babylon `Mesh`, no subclass, with no geometry and no levels of detail, such as an actor's root; or `drawsOnlyBlended`, a caster every part of which needs alpha blending as the map's dispatch asks it (each part's Material from the mesh that draws it, asked for the caster and, for an instance, its source; not with levels of detail or a Material for a render pass), since Babylon's shadow pass draws blended parts only under `transparencyShadow`, which no Haptic map sets; the controller reads every map's flag before each classification and lets blended casters draw while one has it) stays a caster (its participation, the maps' `renderList`, `ShadowCasterList.bounds` and so the cascades' caster bounds) but is in no map layer's list, Babylon's caster draws included, and out of the caster walk and every later stage (`SceneShadowController.drawing`; `ShadowCasterList.setDraws`). Decided: its instances, thin instances, sub-meshes and children draw nothing through it, so none of them makes it draw; geometry applied or released, a level of detail added, or a part coming to blend or to stop blending (a Material's alpha or transparency, the mesh's visibility or vertex alpha, an unlit binding's blend or alpha) does, and `classifyCasters` reads every caster again before each walk (a few property reads each; a drawing caster's first part ends its blend read), so a caster joins or leaves the frame it changes, with no write point needed. A translucent caster therefore redraws no map while it is still or moves, though a WPO or padded Material would make it unsafe if it drew. A caster that leaves the walk is a removal to the stages and one that joins is new: a local map it reaches redraws once. Its moves redraw no map: what it changes there is only the caster bounds, which a cascade follows at its next redraw, as for any caster out of a cascade's reach. In a frame whose walk ran the full path, each layer's list is also built the full way (every caster in the planes read from the mesh, `queryPlanes(…, everyCaster)`, less those `castsNoShadowDepth` names now) and must hold the same casters in the same order; a disagreement is counted (`casterLists` under `heals` or `mismatches`), the full list is drawn, and each caster's list flag is set again from the frame's classification.
- The render change journal records, per Scene and only while it has a reader, each node whose world matrix was recomputed or replaced (`_afterComputeWorldMatrix` on TransformNode and AbstractMesh), whose enabled state or parent changed (`_syncParentEnabledState`, which recurses into descendants), whose `isVisible` or `inheritVisibility` changed (with the descendants that inherit it), whose `visibility`, Material or Material for a render pass (`setMaterialForRenderPass`) changed, or whose rendering group, layer mask, vertex alpha or LOD levels changed (`Dispatch`; each with a Mesh's instances, whose getters read their source), or whose geometry was replaced or notified an update; `noteRenderChange` adds Haptic-owned state (`Other`) and an overlay visual's opacity or tint (`Style`, from `applyOverlayVisualStyle`: values it draws with, never its program). It wraps those Babylon 9.29 prototypes at runtime, installed with the first reader, rather than patching them: no package rebuild, and one place to review when the pin moves. Each reader (`openRenderChangeReader`) names the kinds it reads (the shadow walk: visibility, enabled, Material, Other and Style) and drains from its own cursor; a kind no reader reads is not recorded; a node is listed once until every reader has read it; a reader that falls 65 536 entries behind starts over with a full read. The shadow walk closes its reader while no map exists, so nothing records then.
- Between a frame's caster walk (`SceneShadowController.refreshShadowMaps`, which calls `RetainedShadowCasters.beginFrame`) and the end of that Scene render, no caster moves, shows, hides or changes. The frame's map layers therefore describe a retained caster once: the first layer that lists it does, with the world matrix the walk computed (`ShadowMapRefresh.casterState`), and the others list what it found (a layer drawn against another floating origin writes the caster's row again). The casters themselves are a flat list (`ShadowCasterList`, [`shadow-caster-list.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shadow-caster-list.ts)), not a hierarchy, which had to be refitted for every caster that moved: after the walk `prepare` updates arrays of each caster (whether the walk found it shown, whether its bounds can be trusted, its world box) from the walk's changes, each layer's query tests those numbers against its planes, and `release` ends it with the Scene's render. A pass outside such a frame (a readiness probe, a warm-up draw) asks every caster itself.
- A floating render origin shift alone redraws no shadow map (`ShadowMapRefresh` leaves the origin out of its scene state): a shadow pass offsets the casters and the light's view alike, so the depth it draws is the same, and the light block writes the views it samples with (world-space `drawnViews`) relative to the current origin each frame. `e2e/haptic-contact-shadows.spec.ts` checks that a spot and a point map kept across an origin move draw what a fresh draw at that pose would.
- A sun cascade after the first leaves out casters too small for its texels: a caster whose bounding sphere's diameter in that cascade's texels is below `SHADOW_FAR_CASTER_MIN_TEXELS` of the shadow quality level (`AtlasCascadedShadowGenerator.farCasterMinTexels`, set by `applyShadowSettings`; `castsIntoCascade` in [`shadow-caster-size.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shadow-caster-size.ts)) is not in that cascade's caster list (`getCustomRenderList` in `shadow-map-factory.ts`). The whole mesh's bounds decide, so a large mesh with small parts stays and a thin-instance batch counts as its batch; deforming bounds are not tested, unless they hold the deformation (`deformsWithinBounds`: a skinned caster's that follow the pose, [pose bounds](#play-presentation-and-editor-views), or a lattice cage's widened to its hull). The first cascade lists every caster that reaches it. The sun's refresh tracks a caster only where a cascade draws it (`drawnIntoCascade`, which tests each caster against each cascade by index, allocating nothing per caster or cascade), so a small far caster moving redraws nothing, and the threshold is part of the map's state.
- Retained shadow casters ([`shadow-retained-casters.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shadow-retained-casters.ts), `RetainedShadowCasters`): a map layer draws its rigid, opaque casters from one shared buffer, a draw per geometry part with its casters as instances, instead of one Babylon draw each (defines, buffer bind, five uniforms, the mesh's uniform buffer and four observers per caster, per cascade and face, per redraw). Retained: a caster the refresh scheduler calls `safe` (`ShadowMapRefresh.casterState`: static geometry, no deformers or render hooks, and a Material that passes `canCacheShadowMaterial`), that is a `Mesh` or an `InstancedMesh` with non-updatable positions and normals held on the CPU, triangle fill, no levels of detail (authored or automatic), no thin instances, no lattice cage on it or its source (a tracked cage is safe, but the shared buffer draws undeformed geometry), the map's own normal bias (not a smooth-normal caster such as a landscape chunk) and parts that are whole triangles of its geometry. Everything else keeps Babylon's path: skinned, morphed and cut-out casters, a Material with its own depth variant, thin instances, levels of detail, dynamic geometry, and every caster while the Scene clips or forces a fill mode. Data: each geometry's positions and normals are appended once to one pair of vertex buffers and its indices, rebased, to one 32-bit index buffer; the buffers double when outgrown and are packed once freed ranges exceed 65 536 elements and half of what they hold, each range written again from its geometry's own arrays (referenced, not copied). Each draw (a part of a mesh) owns a slot: five texels of one RGBA32F data texture holding its world matrix less the floating origin (subtracted before narrowing to 32 bits, as Babylon's upload does) and two flags (has normals, uneven scaling); a row is written again only when its matrix, the origin or the Scene's handedness changes. Upload: before a walked frame's first map binds a page (the maps' `onBeforeBindObservable`, `prepareFrame`, once per frame), every caster is described as its first layer would describe it and the changed rows are uploaded, outside any render pass and before any draw sampled the texture (ANGLE's Metal backend blits an upload to a texture with pending render work, ending the pass); the frame's layers then list without writing a row. A layer that finds rows written since (a caster first met inside the stage, a floating origin that moved between maps, a pass outside a walked frame) uploads them before it draws (`lateUploads`). Listing: a map's `getCustomRenderList` (`shadow-map-factory.ts`) passes each caster that survives its culling to `take`, and leaves the ones taken out of Babylon's list; a hidden, disabled or blocked mesh is not taken, and Babylon's list draws nothing of it. Drawing: an observer of the map's `onBeforeRenderingManagerRenderObservable` (after the layer's clear, before Babylon's casters) draws the list with one program per set of generator defines, built from Babylon's own `shadowMap` fragment shader and vertex chunks (normal bias, depth metric), so a retained caster writes the depth Babylon's draw of it would. Per cull state the layer's draws are sorted by part (a part is an index range of the shared buffer: every caster of one geometry and submesh draws the same one, `partFor`), up to `RETAINED_CALL_DRAWS` (256) slots go to a uniform `ivec4` array, and each run of one part is one `drawElementsInstanced`, an instance finding its slot at `hapticRetainedBase + gl_InstanceID`. No multi-draw: ANGLE runs `multiDrawElements` as a loop of single draws, each setting the draw-ID uniform, so its sub-draws cost the GPU process as many draws. The target is depth only, so the order of a layer's casters changes no texel. Fewer draws than parts would need a copy of a geometry per caster or world-space positions (not the same rounding); neither is built. The cull state (culling, side, back or front faces) and the normal matrix are chosen as `ShadowGenerator._renderSubMeshForShadowMap` chooses them, including a mirrored matrix turning the side and an instance drawn in its source's batch taking its source's `nonUniformScaling`. If the program fails to compile, Babylon's path draws every caster. The draws and their indices are counted as Babylon counts draws (`shadowDrawCalls`, `shadowTriangles`). Budget: buffers and texture reserve through `beginManagedRenderAllocation` (`geometry`); a caster the budget has no room for stays with Babylon. Lifetime: the controller releases a caster that leaves the Scene's shadows; a caster the scheduler stops vouching for (a fade, a new Material) is parked without draws and keeps its geometry's place; a restored context rebuilds everything from the casters' geometry. `setRetainedShadowCasters(scene, false)` turns the path off for tests and proofs. `RenderDiagnostics.shadowRetained` and the Check report's `Shadow casters` line say how many casters are retained, the parts they draw, the caster draws a draw stood for and the late uploads. `e2e/shadow-retained-casters.spec.ts` draws one scene of rigid casters, kilometres from the origin, under a shadowed sun, spot light and point light with the path on and off: the images, and the atlas pages' depth bits (`apps/editor/src/testing/shadow-atlas-readback.ts`), are equal through five stages (settled; a caster moved; casters added, removed, hidden, mirrored and stretched; one faded; that one back).  - Each wrapper entry holds one Effect reference and disposes it once (`AuthoredShadowDepthWrapper.retireEntry`), so a shared Effect is released when its last entry retires: mesh, material or wrapper disposal, a define or base-effect change, or its generator's disposal. The vertex array object is released only when the Effect itself dies.
  - `ShadowGenerator.dispose` never reaches a material's wrapper. A wrapper observes the shadow map's `onDisposeObservable` from the first entry it prepares for a generator and retires that generator's entries on every sub-mesh. That covers every disposal path (controller, `Light.dispose`, scene teardown) and a recreated map; a re-created generator acquires new references.
  - `NativeLatticeShadowDepthWrapper` keeps no entries: it delegates to one `AuthoredShadowDepthWrapper` per generation. Stock `ShadowDepthWrapper`, whose define change overwrites `mainDrawWrapper.effect` without disposing it, therefore runs only in tests.
- The sun's cascade split ([`cascade-split.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/cascade-split.ts), applied as each sun map binds in `shadow-map-factory.ts`): from Medium up, a pure log split anchored at 4 m (`setMinMaxDistance` minimum, lambda 1), so cascade 0's slice starts at the anchor and its stabilized sphere must contain the eye (k² · q ≥ 1.2, k = hypot(tanX, tanY), q = end 0 / anchor); Low, orthographic cameras, narrower views and short ranges where the anchored first cascade would end later than the mix keep Babylon's lambda 0.7 mix from the near plane. Reason: lambda 0.7 from a 0.1 m near plane is nearly uniform. The far end is unchanged; Contact Hardening's sun penumbrae narrow with the finer near texels (accepted). Per-cascade ends and texels per metre: `AtlasCascadedShadowGenerator.cascadeDensity`, the Lights debug text and `captureShadowDiagnostics`; `e2e/cascade-shimmer.spec.ts` holds outlines to a ray-cast mask while the camera slides.
- Screen-Space Shadows ([`screen-shadows.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/screen-shadows.ts)), `ShadowSettings.screenShadows` (Low and Medium off, High medium, Ultra high; off in CEL): `SceneEffectsPlan.screenShadows` alone makes a plan, only for a Scene `allowSceneScreenShadows` marked (create-engine's world Scene; previews, thumbnails, Prefab Preview and Scene Layers never) and only while its support check (`spatialEffectsUnsupported`, read at each replan) passes, so an unsupported device plans no offscreen chain for them and reports `screen shadows: device capability` (`sceneEffectsQualityLimits`). Enabled at any level, they need the spatial geometry pass, which they add when no other effect does; it draws while the march runs or another running stage reads it (`syncIdle`). The spatial geometry pass draws the cull task's `worldObjectList`, its output without editor and Play helper visuals (`isEditorHelperMesh`), which the cull builds only while a spatial geometry pass reads it (`publishesWorld`), so collider outlines and grids neither block the march nor reach ambient occlusion or reflections. `ScreenShadowsGraph` declares an RGBA16F target at half the render size (`SCREEN_SHADOW_SCALE`, rounded up) and a fullscreen task between `Spatial Geometry` and `Forward objects` (with `worlddebug mergedmarches on`, also the merged marches, [Rendering settings](#rendering-settings)) that marches once per 2 × 2 block, from its farthest surface pixel (a receiver where the block straddles a caster's edge; the plane from the depth two pixels to each side), toward `hapticShadowedSun` in view space (the camera's projection; steps, pixel and world caps and eye-distance fades per level, `SCREEN_SHADOW_LEVELS`), writing R the lit share, G that pixel's eye distance, B the block's pixels on its plane (bits 0–3) + 16 × its index + 64 × the index of the first pixel off it, A that pixel's eye distance. Every level is the same program and target, so `sceneEffectsKey` keys only their presence (`:screen`) and the march reads the plan's current level when it binds: High↔Ultra or a Custom level edit rebuilds no graph. With no shadowed sun (`hapticShadowedSun`, the light block written before the frame) or an orthographic camera the march would write lit everywhere, so it idles that frame (`ScreenShadowsGraph.sync`, from `syncIdle`): the task runs its empty disabled pass, the objects passes bind the placeholder with the read off (`screenShadowsForPass` null, `hapticScreenShadowTerms.x` 0), which the light block takes as lit as it would the march's 1, and the geometry pass idles too unless another running stage reads it. No other pass samples the geometry buffers (water, Mirrors and captures draw their own views; an authored stack has its own geometry pass). A sun that starts casting re-enables the task the next frame and creates no pipeline (a mesh the idle geometry pass never drew may compile its geometry variant then, which the view's readiness holds as for any graph change, as after a Fog Volume idle); a projection change rebuilds the view anyway (`liveSceneEffectsKey`). The objects pass registers its render pass id and declares the target as a dependency (the water split's own opaque pass draws through the same renderer); any other pass binds a scene-owned lit placeholder with the read off. `HAPTIC_SCREEN_SHADOWS` says a surface can read them, not that the plan has them: a world view's PBR Scene whose device supports the pass, surface opaque or masked by `transparencyMode`, not water, not unlit. A tier or setting that turns them on or off binds the target or the placeholder with the read off and links no lit program again (a lit colour program takes seconds to link on Direct3D); `followLightingDefines` marks frozen Materials dirty when `replanSceneEffects`'s lighting-define key (mode, device support) changes. The block reads its own pixel's surface distance from its block's texel (G or A, by its bit), as the full-resolution march gave it, and keeps 1 unless its own distance carried along its screen slope (`dFdx`, `dFdy` of the distance) to that pixel is within 0.004 d + 0.01: floor seen through glass never takes a neighbour's shadow. It then blends the lit share of the 3 × 3 texels around its block whose marched pixel lies within two pixels (a tent) and whose carried distance is within twice that (decided: half the march cost for slightly softer contact edges and thin streaks); the march's dither is a fixed per-pixel interleaved gradient, filtered by that blend (no temporal accumulation exists), then takes the sun term's min of the cascades and mix(first cascade's darkness, 1, lit); the light block reads the screen shadows only inside the sun's term and only where the cascades leave the sun lighter than that darkness (`hapticSunScreenShadow`: at the darkness the min is the cascades' value whatever the read says), with the eye and the distance slope taken before the lights where control flow is uniform. Only casters occlude the march: with screen shadows planned the geometry pass's view depth is RG, and `GeometrySurfaceOutputBlock` writes G 1 where the drawn mesh casts shadows (`meshCastsShadows`: a pool source as its group's key says, any other mesh as the shadow controller last set with `setMeshShadowFlags`, unseen meshes casting), 0 elsewhere and at the clear; the march skips a sample whose G is 0, so Cast Shadows holds for both shadow sources. Babylon's own geometry shaders write G 0. Translucent casters are in the geometry depth, so they occlude the march (glass casts screen-space contact shadows); the march assumes a surface it passes behind is at most min(0.15 m + 0.02 z, bias + max(the ray's depth change per step, the filter reach in metres of the sun cascade at that depth)) deep (`screenShadowThicknessCaps`, bound per frame), so it adds only contacts finer than the cascades resolve and never darkens a face the cascades show lit because a nearer object's silhouette sits beside it; with coarse cascades it can still darken a little floor beside slim objects. `e2e/haptic-screen-shadows.spec.ts` holds it to a ray-cast contact mask.
- Directional Auto Bias adapts to the current projection and admitted map size; derived values are never serialized. It adds no normal inset (split normals would open contacts) except for casters marked with `markSmoothNormalShadowCaster` (landscape chunks), which draw with `SMOOTH_CASTER_NORMAL_BIAS_TEXELS` world texels so receiver-plane PCF does not self-shadow concave triangle edges (`shadow-bias.ts`). Point/spot automatic bias is unsupported (authored offsets only). The sun's cascades are built on its own Engine's support (every shipped Engine draws them), not Babylon's last-created Engine's (see the runtime member replacements below).
- Sun incidence sets directional shadow quality: a map texel and its PCF kernel stretch by 1 / cos along a face the sun meets at incidence cos, and an overhang's shadow lengthens likewise. The seeded sun (`DEFAULT_SCENE_SUN_POSITION` in [`skybox.ts`](../../../packages/core/src/skybox.ts)) therefore meets faces turned to the default camera at cos 0.6. The Basic 3D Mannequin shadow specs hold the drawn edge to the ray-cast one in metres (`mannequinShadowEdgeError`, [`mannequin-shadow-pixels.ts`](../../../e2e/mannequin-shadow-pixels.ts)); their sample classes scale with texel size and cos, so a grazing sun widens its own tolerance.
- Shadow admission lowers resolution to fit (256 px floor) and retries at half size on allocation failure; it never changes authored values. A grown atlas that fails as its first tile makes it is taken as one the device cannot hold: the sync builds nothing more (those maps wait `budget-limited` a sync) and the sun's recovery asks for half its admitted size, so the next sync plans a smaller atlas. Allocation validation (`shadow-allocation-validation.ts`: GL error drain, attachment and completeness probes, all synchronous GPU round trips) runs in full only for a render target not yet validated; a new map that only draws into the Scene's validated atlas target (`shadowAtlasRenderTarget`) makes no GL call. Quality presets and per-tier ceilings live in core [`shadows.ts`](../../../packages/core/src/shadows.ts).
- Shadow memory is the atlas: one depth-only texture array (its pages together the edge squared) of 4 bytes per texel (`SHADOW_ATLAS_BYTES_PER_TEXEL`, `shadowAtlasBytes`), charged to the shadow quality level's byte budget once it holds a tile; its tiles cost nothing more. Low and Medium's 4096 edge is 64 MiB, High and Ultra's 8192 is 256 MiB, a sun-grown 16384 is 1 GiB. `ENGINE_SHADOW_BUDGET` is 768 MiB and the managed ceiling (`MANAGED_RENDER_BYTE_LIMIT`) is 1 GiB. A grown atlas is reserved whole, its bytes past the level's edge exempt (`ShadowCost.exempt`, `reserveManagedShadowBytes`'s `exempt`): they raise the managed ceiling as much while reserved (`managedRenderReservations().limit`, `shadowExemptBytes`) and count in neither other Scenes' `ENGINE_SHADOW_BUDGET` room nor standby retention, so the explicit size takes no other allocation's room. Each Scene with a grown atlas holds its own 1 GiB.
- Editor light billboards are a status palette, not the light colour: white operating, yellow limited (past the light block's maximum or the Local Light Budget, or missing shadow allocation), red disabled/non-illuminating. Tint multiplies the diffuse texture (emissive would wash it white).
- Membership or shadow changes must dirty frozen materials' light/shadow defines.
- GPU allocations (shadow, cluster, water, area light, post-process, geometry) reserve in one Engine ledger before construction (`beginManagedRenderAllocation`, [`managed-render-resources.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/managed-render-resources.ts)); a lease releases at once after its resources are disposed (`releaseManagedRenderLeaseAfterDisposal`). Uncertain cleanup keeps the lease. Estimates, not measured VRAM. A Mirror surface's fixed 1×1 fallback texel (one per Scene, bound while its reflection has not drawn) stays outside the ledger, like held images.
- Rectangular area lights are unshadowed (no generator, light through walls). LTC tables are bundled ([`resources/`](../../../packages/render/src/resources/), CC BY 4.0, no CDN). Emission textures are prepared at authoring time into a versioned derived chunk; gameplay never runs the processor; missing data disables the emitter with a diagnostic.

## Resources

- Nearest Texture Filtering ([`nearest-textures.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/nearest-textures.ts); project `twoD.nearestTextures`, effective only with `pixelPerfect`: `nearestTextureFiltering`) is per Engine: `acquireMaterialTexture` tracks every texture a Material samples (`trackMaterialTexture`, once loaded), and `setNearestTextures` switches them all to NEAREST in place and back to the mode each loaded with, so the switch is live and reversible without recompiling Materials or reacquiring textures. A switch, or a texture loading while it is on, rebinds every draw of the Engine's Scenes once at the next frame (a frozen Material may skip binding a run of draws) and calls `observeNearestTextures` listeners, which redraw each view. The editor sets it on the shared Engine from the project settings (`play-context.tsx`), so editor views and overlay Play follow it; the player gets `nearestTextures` from the manifest through `CreateEngineOptions`. A Painter2D canvas is tracked too, and `resampleMaterialTexture` reapplies the switch after a resize recreates its GPU texture. Sprites, tiles, 2D images and Render Target captures sample NEAREST already; environments, text atlases (MSDF and non-pixel fonts) and engine data never pass through it. `e2e/nearest-textures.spec.ts` checks a frozen Material.
- One `ResourceCache` per Engine (`resourceCacheForEngine`) shared by world, SceneLayer and preview Scenes. Exact-generation leases with idempotent `release()`; reading a held resource never re-acquires. A texture lease also holds its sampling key (`invertY`, mips, filtering, alpha, anisotropy): when a sampling's last lease is released while other leases keep its entry, its wrapper is disposed, with its upload and extra object URL when no other sampling shares them, because entry eviction cannot reach it then. An unreferenced entry keeps every sampling until it is trimmed or flushed; a flush (`flushUnreferenced`, which every owner's `dispose` runs) also disposes a sampling left without leases in an entry that another lease holds again. Default 2 GiB accounted ceiling, trims unreferenced entries to 80% ([`perf-ceilings.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/perf-ceilings.ts)); live leases are never evicted. The cache has no budget of its own: each view sets one through its owner (`ResourceCacheOwner.setByteCeiling` and `setBudgetEnabled`), the largest live view cap wins, the default applies with none, and any live view that turns its budget off stops eviction. Geometry is accounted (`GEOMETRY_BYTE_CEILING`) but never evicted.
- Texture handover (`holdForHandover`, `claimHandover`): Play's start and stop release one world view and load another on the same Engine, and both sample mostly the same textures, so the next view takes them over instead of decoding and uploading them again. A view told `handOverTextures()` before `dispose()` (the editor's Scene viewports when Play suspends them, never for Preview Build or Simulate; Play's view when it stops) holds the entries its leases held, once its release confirms, instead of flushing them; other owners' flushes skip held entries, and an LRU trim may still evict them.
  - The Engine's next world view (an editor Scene view or Play's: not a preview, an RTT view or one owning its Engine) claims the hold when it first installs texture sources: at construction when its options carry `textureBytes`, else at its first asset install. Every held entry whose asset guid and content identity (the SHA-256 taken at installation) its `textureBytes` or `fontMsdfPng` do not name is flushed at once, as is a six-face cube with a blob face they do not name, before that view allocates anything. What only the released view sampled therefore never sits beside the next view's load: the viewport release still frees that, and its Scene's shadow maps and probe cubes, before Play allocates (iPad WebKit loses the context under the doubled memory).
  - The view leases the rest as its load asks for them. Its first presented world frame, a world load that fails (a presentation cancelled for anything but a newer load, `sceneLoadFailed`, a lost context) or its disposal flushes what it left unleased, and the samplings no lease holds. A hold nothing claims is flushed after `TEXTURE_HANDOVER_CLAIM_MS` (10 s), a claimed one after `TEXTURE_HANDOVER_MS` (120 s). A view handing over while a hold it claimed is still open (Play stopped during its load) adds its leases to that hold, so the editor's reload takes back what Play had not sampled yet.
  - Keys match across the transition only for the same bytes: the editor's viewport and Play read the same Texture chunk unless Editor Texture LOD downsamples the editor's, and then nothing of that Texture is handed over. `create-engine.texture-handover.test.ts` pins one upload of a texture both views sample across Play's start and stop, and that what only one side samples is released before the other view loads; `e2e/play-texture-handover.spec.ts` counts an Environment Texture's uploads through the editor's real Play and Stop.
- Editor Play compares the Engine's texture cache before and after a session; a texture that outlives it is reported with its name, size, native references and ResourceCache entry (`describeEngineTexture`), whose lease count is the leases on the samplings that share that native texture, not on the entry. A KTX2 decoded to RGBA reports its last mip's size (Babylon 9.29), so a 1×1 report can be a full-size texture. A texture a live Scene outside the session still references is not Play's to release, so it is not reported: an editor preview opened mid-session (a Class graph's viewport) creates its own BRDF table on the shared Engine. Nor is one the ResourceCache holds, with no lease on it, for the editor views that reload after Play (texture handover above, `texturesHeldForHandover`). Ownership decides it, by identity: `internalTexturesHeldByScene` collects what each other Scene in `engine.scenes` registers, its Materials sample, its environment sets and the per-Engine area-light LTC tables it retains (the native pair and the block's stacked copy) (textures built on the Engine, like the ResourceCache's, reach a Scene only through a Material or the environment), and for every render target among them its colour texture, multi render target attachments and depth/stencil texture, which are separate native textures. The session's own Scenes (`EngineHandle.ownedScenes()`: the world Scene and every Scene the SceneLayer compositor built, meaning layer Scenes, output-blit Scenes and retired generations still releasing, captured before disposal) never excuse what they hold, even one that outlived its disposal, and a texture no live Scene references stays a leak. `e2e/simulation-graph-debug.spec.ts` stops a Simulation after a Class graph first opened during it and expects no leak report. `create-engine.play-textures.test.ts` pins the default skybox, default material and sun shadow map to zero retention across cycles; textured materials, models and post-processing are not covered there. `resource-cache-texture.test.ts` pins that a handle's sampling of a texture is released while another handle still leases the texture in another sampling, and that a flush releases a sampling that went idle before another sampling leased the texture again.
- Content identity of an immutable installed Blob is a verified identity the caller passes when it holds one for exactly those bytes (`installTextureBytes`' `verifiedSha256`: the exported player passes a Texture's or MSDF atlas's catalog revision, its SHA-256), and otherwise `assetContentHash`: the byte length and a 128-bit MurmurHash3 x86 of the bytes, about a hundred times faster than a JavaScript SHA-256 and not a cryptographic digest (integrity is the catalog revision's job, checked with `crypto.subtle`). Raw arrays (fonts, sprite sources) are fingerprinted the same way. The two schemes never compare equal, so the same bytes installed both ways count as different content (an extra upload, never a wrong one). `installAssetBytes` builds the Blob straight from the caller's array (the Blob constructor copies it) and keeps only a 64 KiB header beside it (`installedAssetHeader`, `assetSourceHeader` for image-size and container sniffing). The exported player installs each Texture, Model source and MSDF atlas as `apps/player/src/artifact.ts` loads it, right after verifying its catalog revision, with that revision as the identity (a Model's GLB, a slice of its packed payload, takes `<revision>:source`), and keeps no array of those bytes (`payloads` holds an empty one for eviction): the Blob is their only copy. It hands the Blobs to `createGameEngine` (`textureBytes`, `modelSources`, `fontMsdfPng`) and every `acquireSceneSources`, which keep installed Blobs as they are; Complex Collision cooks read a Model's Blob asynchronously. A released asset drops its Blobs with its load, and `releaseInitialSources` drops every initial source from the view's options copy (`releaseInitialSourceOptions`), `modelSources` included, so neither keeps a released source's copy alive. Never fragment `blob:` URLs (Babylon escapes the fragment into the identity). Different upload keys (`invertY`, sampling, mips, cube) get separate wrappers from the same Blob. An entry mints its Blob (a copy of the bytes) and object URL only when something fetches them by URL (cubes, Blob inputs, images, `acquireBlobUrl`): Babylon decodes a 2D KTX2 from the buffer it is given and never fetches its URL, so each such upload key gets a unique never-fetched name (`haptic-buffer:`) instead, and only it gets a buffer copy (Babylon's image path loads the URL and ignores a buffer).
- KTX2 block-compressed uploads ignore `invertY`, so `prepareTexture` builds them unflipped and flips V on the wrapper (`vScale = -1`, `vOffset = 1`). Samplers must apply the texture matrix. A Babylon change that sets `_invertVScale` for KTX2 would double-flip.
- `configureKtx2DecoderRuntime` ([`ktx2-transcoder.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/ktx2-transcoder.ts)) sets the decoder default `forceRGBA` to true on a software GL renderer or a GPU with neither ASTC nor BC7 (`shouldForceKtx2Rgba`) and otherwise leaves it undefined (not false): decoder defaults override per-texture options. Compressed Textures encode at sizes rounded up to a multiple of 4 (the ASTC/BC7 4×4 block grid), except atlases.
- Six-face cubes are created on the Engine, not the Scene; skybox materials null `reflectionTexture` on dispose so Play teardown cannot drop a cached cube. The skybox never calls `createDefaultSkybox`, never sets `scene.environmentTexture`, is not `infiniteDistance`.
- Babylon's cube upload runs when the last face decodes and never checks disposal. A cube released or evicted mid-load (`disposeTexture` in `resource-cache.ts`) drops its wrapper at once but keeps the native texture until that upload settles, bounded by the preparation deadline; disposing it earlier uploads to an unbound target ("no texture bound" warnings, then GL error 1282 at the next presentation).
- Publication of materials, texture parameters and sprite/skybox/model replacements waits for full lease preparation (byte accounting and budget admission). `Texture.isReady()` is not enough: Babylon's error sampler reports ready.
- Replacements are transactional: prepare the successor, keep the working predecessor, publish only on success; generation/owner checks drop late completions; failed requests stay latched until their inputs change.
- Lowered plans are shared across every `MaterialLibrary` in the realm: `planFor` asks the shader graph's one lowering memo (`lowerMaterialDocumentCached`, [shader-graph.md](shader-graph.md)), by document identity first and then by content, so Play's library, previews and thumbnails reuse what the editor viewport lowered. Hosts replace Material and Material Function documents to change them and never edit one in place, since an unchanged document object is answered without being read again. Failed lowerings are shared with their diagnostics, which every sharing library reports. The compiler indexes each shared plan's operations and texture bindings once (`planIndex`), so lookups by operation id stay constant-time at the operation bound.
- `MaterialLibrary` warms a new Material generation for the meshes bound to it only when it replaces a published one: the predecessor stays bound until the successor has compiled for them. A first acquisition warms nothing; its host compiles what it draws (the load's `prewarmSceneMaterials`, readiness probes, or a preview's own prepare-then-bind step), because warming at acquisition runs before the Scene's lighting, fog and transforms are final and builds variants nothing draws. A host that binds at acquisition and captures right after (the asset thumbnail) sets `warmFirstAcquisition`, so a program that cannot link fails the capture through `ready`. So `ready`, `isReady` and `compiledMaterialGuids` mean built with textures admitted, not linked.
- A program the Engine cannot link is reported where its Effect is first created: the load's warm-up rejects naming the Material and mesh, and `material.onError` (compiler `onLinkError`) reports the shader log, without JS frames, as `material.compile.failed` through `onTextureError` / `onMaterialDiagnostic` (`console.warn` when the host takes none). The library then retires the generation: it is disposed, so its meshes fall back to the default Material and readiness can pass, but its entry stays in its map so holders' reference counts carry to the next acquisition of the key. Without that, an unlinkable bound Material never reads ready and the Scene draws nothing, silently. Babylon hands a cached Effect to a second requester without its `onError`, so each compiled material also listens to `onEffectErrorObservable` and matches the Effect's first creator by build id (`nodeMaterial<buildId>`): materials sharing a program (a root, its Instances and per-component copies adopt one build id) retire together. A surface variant (outline mask, depth or normal capture, shadow depth) is a program of its own that its pass cannot draw without, so its link failure reaches the owner the same way, with the log naming the variant, and retires the generation. The owner is found by the entry's current key, which `adoptPreparedInstance` moves from the staging token to the component's, so an adopted copy retires too. A reported failure also drops the program from the compiler's shared-program registry, so a Material compiled after it builds its own Effect (and hears of its failure) instead of adopting the dead build id. A sharer the registry cannot reach would adopt the dead program without hearing of it, so the library latches the failed plan per asset and shading variant, shared by its instance keys (hash and diagnostics): `acquire` and `resolve` of that hash fail at once with the same diagnostics, compile nothing and report nothing again, until the hash changes (an edited graph, function or texture identity), `markDirty`, `forgetFailures` or `releaseScene`; releasing the key does not end it, because the next holder would adopt the same dead program. `forgetFailures` runs by itself when the Scene's receivers bind other lights or shadow maps (`notifySceneReceiversChanged`, [`scene-readiness-signal.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/scene-readiness-signal.ts)): `syncSceneLighting` after a different enabled-light set, shadow layout or light capacity, and every `invalidateShadowReceiverHandoffs` caller in the shadow controller (a map created, released, moved or retuned). A varying or uniform limit can fail a link for that set and not for another, with the plan hash unchanged. A refresh that changes none of them (the retirement's own) keeps the latch. Any failure of the plan itself latches (a build failure, a link failure, a shader error from the replacement warm-up or Custom GLSL's probe).
