A texture that is not ready does not (its own preparation reports it, and its identity is part of the hash), and neither does a stall (`MaterialCompileStalled`, diagnostic `material.compile.stalled`: no shader finished for the warm-up's or the probe's bound, which says nothing about the program); those requests compile again.
- Models: canonical bytes are GLB; [`gltf-loader.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/gltf-loader.ts) registers the glTF 2.0 loader and its extensions only. Draco/meshopt decoders are self-hosted, never a CDN; Draco ships only its wasm build, and `configureGltfMeshDecoders` clears Babylon's `fallbackUrl` (an asm.js CDN default), since the engine requires WebAssembly. Play views decode Draco and transcode KTX2 on the main thread (the packed player and the Preview Build iframe cannot load the decoders' wasm from a blob Worker) unless created with `workerDecoders` (the editor's Play, whose page loads them in workers). OBJ/FBX/STL are converted to GLB at import (`convert-*-to-glb.ts`). Containers load without the file's materials (`skipMaterials`), so nothing draws a loader `PBRMaterial`: a mesh draws its slot's Material asset or the engine default. Per-Scene template containers instantiate per actor. Model slots map by the glTF material index each primitive names (`modelMaterialIndexOf`, read from the primitive pointer in `_internalMetadata`, which instances and clones share), never child visit order; a file without materials draws every primitive with slot 0, and points and lines are hidden (Babylon keeps a primitive's draw mode on its material). `loadModelContainer` gives every triangle mesh without `NORMAL` flat normals (`addMissingFlatNormals`; indexed meshes are unfolded, and unindexed meshes skip Automatic LOD): surface Materials read the `normal` attribute, and a missing buffer reads as zero, which draws black.
- MeshComponent geometry and Material are separate references: a primitive with a Material keeps a null `meshAssetGuid`. `materialSource: "override"` distinguishes an explicit None (engine checker) from inherited Model slots; the picker's "Model Materials" value is UI-only and never saved.
- Runtime parameters (`setMaterialParameter`) share the compiled default until a component's first write, then `MaterialLibrary` compiles a private instance for that component only; saved assets and siblings never change. A None texture binds a neutral white sampler.
- A script write of a mesh parameter value the renderer already has sends nothing (`RuntimeMaterialParameters.unchanged`: the stored value is equal channel by channel and the name was sent since the material's last `assignMaterial`, which `emitMaterialAssignments` clears with `resend`); the value is still recorded and the revision still advances. A seeded default was never sent, so its first write still sends. Inspector writes, texture parameters and post-process writes always send: the renderer drops a post-process write while its owner loads, and a texture until it has that texture.
- Environment cubes reach render only as ENV or DDS: `.hdr` panoramas become prefiltered RGBA16F DDS at import ([asset-registry.md](asset-registry.md#importers)), so render has no HDR, panorama or filtering path.
- The scene environment controller leases one cache source and clones only its CubeTexture view ([`environment-lighting.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/environment-lighting.ts)); intensity goes through `scene.iblIntensity`. Missing irradiance is derived once per cube, from 32 × 32 faces drawn on the GPU and read back through a fenced pixel buffer (no synchronous `readPixels`), and remembered per Engine by the source's content key and decoding (16 entries), so a reload of the same environment reads nothing back. Pending or failed uploads join readiness and are never thrown from a frame. A Skybox's cube reports only coefficients its upload carries (`sphericalPolynomial` overridden on that cube): its unlit PBR material would otherwise start Babylon's lazy CPU irradiance from the full-size faces.
- Skybox source: the controller takes `acquireExisting` on the first enabled, visible skybox mesh's cube (no second upload), draws it in `CUBIC_MODE` with the reflection matrix `RotationY(Environment Rotation) × inverse(sky rotation)` so lighting matches the drawn sky, and follows sky changes from its before-render hook (the mesh list is rescanned only when it changed). A turned sky only rewrites the view's matrix in place: no define reads it and unfrozen materials rebind it every frame, so the hook dirties no material and signals no readiness change, and forces a rebind only on frozen materials' own draw wrappers, whose uniform buffers skip it otherwise. A sky whose cube is still uploading holds readiness and keeps the previous view; a failed sky disables its mesh, which drops the lighting.
- Preview sky ([`preview-environment.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/preview-environment.ts)): `installPreviewEnvironment` draws the engine default sky in Model, Animation, Skeleton, Material, Camera Shake and opt-in Particle previews and lights the preview from it through the same controller (`applyEnvironmentLighting` in skybox mode), so a preview frame waits for the sky's upload and filtering like a Scene's. Without it a metal reflects nothing and draws black apart from direct highlights. Thumbnail hosts and Prefab Preview do not call it.
- Reflection probes ([`reflection-probes.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/reflection-probes.ts)): each `ReflectionProbeComponent` captures static meshes (`reflectionProbeStaticActors` in core: plain Actors whose components never move, under static parents; Water and helpers excluded) outside the view's graph (`drawOutsideSceneView`), one probe per frame, once edits and mesh changes settle (`REFLECTION_PROBE_RECAPTURE_DELAY_MS`) and only when the static mesh set changed (a forced recapture after any authored edit, a Play load or the Recapture Reflection Probes node). A capture draws each face with the view's own programs into one scene colour target (half-float, with depth): only the static meshes that face's frustum holds (`listFace`; Babylon culls no explicit list, so the capture tests each with `drawsInsidePlanes`, as a Mirror's pass and a Fast capture do), while the target's readiness check still asks every one of them. That check announces a draw of the target too, so it reads the first face. Then one pass ([`display-conversion.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/display-conversion.ts), `DisplayConversion`: Babylon's image-processing pass with a default configuration of its own) writes it into that face of the raw cube display-referred, as a Material that applied the Scene's image processing itself would have (the Scene's own configuration stays at its defaults: encoded for display and clamped to 0..1), so a capture compiles no Material a second time. A view that is not Scene Linear already draws display colour, which the same pass copies. The cube is filtered by the shared prefilter path (which decodes it) with its own irradiance, then box-projected onto the probe's world AABB. Per-mesh selection ([`mesh-environment.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/mesh-environment.ts)): a pinned patch of PBR and Node `isReadyForSubMesh`/`bindForSubMesh` swaps the scene's `_environmentTexture` backing field to the mesh's cube (the smallest published probe box holding its bounding-sphere centre), so each submesh compiles for its own cube's defines and binds it; a cube switch between consecutive draws of one material, or a change to the cube's box (`markMeshEnvironmentChanged` bumps its revision), forces a rebind (`_forceRebindOnNextCall`, which also refreshes frozen UBOs). A mesh whose probe changes dirties its material; the resolver appearing (first publish) or going (last published probe removed) dirties every material, since selection only dirties meshes it assigned before (probe cubes are engine-bound, so their box setters dirty nothing). Instanced batches use their source mesh's probe. Probes never hold readiness; until a capture publishes, meshes reflect the scene's environment. Lighting reads only the filtered cube, so a runtime scene's probe (Play, Preview Build, the exported player: no authored document) frees its capture's targets (the raw half-float cube, and the face's scene colour target and depth) once the filtered cube publishes, and its next capture (a changed static set) makes a new one; an editor probe keeps it, as every settled document edit recaptures. A published probe whose new capture cube the ledger refuses keeps its last filtered cube and stays dirty, retrying each time the recapture delay passes again until the ledger has room; a refused first capture marks the probe failed, so it captures again only on a forced recapture.
- Sky filtering ([`environment-prefilter.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/environment-prefilter.ts)): the controller lights from a GGX-prefiltered copy of the sky, at most 256 per face in half-float, one per Engine and sky upload. Babylon's `hdrFiltering` shader writes every mip, mip 0 included, with the lobe PBR samples it for (`lodGenerationScale` 0.8), and reads the source mip matching each sample, so 64 samples suffice and the face downsample needs no extra pass. The target reserves under the `environment` managed category. While filtering, readiness holds and the previous view stays; a device without prefiltering or float targets, an exhausted budget or a failed filter lights from the unfiltered sky.
- Automatic LOD ([`model-lod.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/model-lod.ts)): index-only levels sharing the master's vertex buffers, generated off-thread by meshoptimizer, never persisted or exported; selection in `customLODSelector`. Models generate one at a time in one shared worker; a model copies its vertex streams and takes its native-preparation admission (`background`, since levels never gate publishing) only in its own turn, so models waiting in line hold no slot or copies, and one whose bytes another Scene is already generating waits for that generation without a turn. No texture streaming (Babylon has no residency API).
- Lifetime: everything a Scene, preview or Play session allocates must return to the context when it closes; the project Engine lives for the whole session, so any retention grows until allocation fails or the context is lost. Babylon disposes a draw wrapper's Effect one end frame after that wrapper, so an Engine with no rendering client would keep a closed Scene allocated until another view draws. A non-owning client's `dispose` therefore calls `drainEngineAfterRelease` ([`idle-engine-drain.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/idle-engine-drain.ts)): the shared Engine runs empty frames until that client's release settles, then three more. It gives up if the release has not settled after 5 s, or no frame follows within a second of settling, leaving the rest to the next client's frames; it removes only its own loop, so a client that starts mid-drain keeps rendering.

## Play presentation and editor views

- The Play Scene starts with camera and light only; actors come from `assignMesh` plus snapshots. Play always draws the latest published snapshot, no interpolation (render and tick share the ~60 Hz cap). `SnapshotSampler` ([`snapshot-sync.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/snapshot-sync.ts)) ignores unpublished buffers and is cleared on scene change; snapshot TRS is world space; new geometry stays hidden until a snapshot poses it. One slot is one visual; when an actor's last Play renderable stops rendering (Render In Game off, Editor Only on) and no capture, audio, particle or rigid body fallback assignment takes its place, the runtime replaces it with an empty `assignMesh` (`meshKind: null`), sent only for a slot whose last `assignMesh` carried a visual (fog-only and probe-only actors get their own empty view-owner assignment).
- The runtime re-sends an actor's `assignMesh` with every component property write, unchanged values included (a script writing a Text3D's text every tick). `applyAssignMesh` ([`snapshot-assign-mesh.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/snapshot-assign-mesh.ts)) keeps the slot's visual for a command equal to the assignment that visual was built for (`showsAssignment`: equal as later style, control, painter and text reveal commands left it), while the visual is still the one the slot shows or prepares in that host Scene, the binding's install revision is the one it was built at (`meshAssetInstallRevision`: the inputs `meshAssetFingerprint` hashes, compared by identity through a weakly keyed number per map, since an install replaces them; the record keeps the number, never a superseded map or its bytes, and holds its visual weakly) and neither its staged construction nor its model load failed; anything else rebuilds, and a failed preparation is tried again. A rebuild would hand the world view a mesh that has not drawn, hidden until it is known ready, and restart a staged visual's preparation before it adopts. A real change still rebuilds. The one change that does not rebuild is what a 2D Text says (a script writing a score or a timer, a spawned label's Begin Play): a command equal to the slot's assignment but for the `text` of its 2D Text redraws those texts with the visual the slot shows (`redrawTextInPlace`). `text2DRedraw` ([`text2d-mesh.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/text2d-mesh.ts)) lays the new text out and rasterizes it as a build does, then replaces the vertices of the glyph batches and the pick plane, so no mesh or material joins or leaves the Scene: the view runs no readiness probe and the text never waits hidden. It draws the pixels a rebuild would (the same quads sampling the same texels). A text redraws this way only with what it already owns: plain 2D Text without a Text Material or underline, in the same font and renderer, with the same glyph batches in the same order (the same MSDF styles; bitmap glyphs on as many atlas pages, a batch sampling another page when its glyphs moved there). Rich Text, any other property change, a slot whose last assignment was refused or is still preparing, and a text the bitmap limits refuse all take the rebuild path. A text that cannot redraw for what it is is turned down once it is measured, before its glyphs are rasterized (`draftText2D`). A planned redraw holds its glyphs in the atlas until it is applied or discarded (`Text2DRedraw`). A redraw replaces vertices on a mesh whose world matrix Play froze, which Babylon does not recompute, so it refreshes those bounds itself (`replaceVertexData`). Building or redrawing a text measures and rasterizes each distinct glyph on a 2D canvas and reads it back, which is most of the work; [`text2d-bitmap.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/text2d-bitmap.ts) keeps measured and rasterized glyphs between builds (`rememberedGlyph`: up to 4096 glyphs and 8 MB of pixels, the least recently used leaving first), keyed by everything their drawing reads (character, exact size and outline, weight, slant, colours and font stack), so a rewritten text reads back only glyphs it has not drawn. What a glyph draws as depends on the font faces the document holds: the cache empties when `FontRegistry` loads or removes a face and on the document's `loadingdone` (faces a stylesheet or a canvas draw loaded), and it belongs to one document. Cached cells are shared and never written; allocation limits are still checked for every caller. A material command's restart of a staged preparation rebuilds whatever the slot shows. The decision is the renderer's, since only it knows what it built, with which installed bytes and in which Scene; script writes still call `refreshComponent` for unchanged values, which can have effects of their own (a written Layer or Mask makes the collider Custom).
- Snapshot apply runs once per applied snapshot identity (`{frameId, layoutGeneration}`); audio pose and listener sync live there, not in render admission. Every `pushSnapshot` resets the identity (a publish outside a tick keeps its frame id). A pump posts a pose only when the runtime wrote one since the last post; forced posts (realization markers, a mutating runtime Inspector reply, a returned buffer after a skipped pose, the first pump after a layout install) may post the current pose again ([bridge.md](bridge.md)). A running frame whose identity is already applied re-poses only what moves on the host's clock (`applyHostClockPoses`, when `hasHostClockPoses`): bone attachments (Babylon animates bones on wall time) and the lights and cameras they carry, spring-arm lag (stepped on wall time, `updatePlaySpringArms`) and its socket cameras, those actors' component lights and cameras, then pixel snap and tilemap parallax; spatial audio stays once per applied snapshot. A paused session's frames apply nothing, so spring-arm lag holds until the next pose, which steps it at most `SPRING_ARM_MAX_LAG_STEP_SECONDS`.
- An unchanged row writes nothing. Babylon's setters mark a node dirty whatever the value (`copyFromFloats`, the `rotationQuaternion` setter), so `writeActorTransform` returns without writing when the node already holds the row (`holdsActorTransform`): its local position, rotation quaternion and scale equal the row's ten floats (`===`: NaN is never equal, so it writes; ±0 compare equal and compose the same matrix), its world matrix was computed from that TRS (`isSynchronized`: no TRS, parent or pivot write since), and it is frozen exactly when `shouldFreezeStaticWorldMatrix` says the write would freeze it. Comparing the node itself, not a remembered row, needs no invalidation: another writer of the TRS (a gizmo, a fresh visual at the origin with no quaternion) makes the row differ; a visual that copied its predecessor's TRS (layer migration, staged replacement) is unsynchronized and unfrozen, so it still takes its first write and its world matrix is ready in the same frame; a freeze rule that changes (the engine marks the node always drawn) rewrites. A writer that replaces the frozen world matrix without the TRS restores it itself (bone attachments unfreeze before each pose, `restoreSnapshotPose`). Visibility skips the same way per node (`appliedPlayVisibility`, keyed by the mesh, so a new visual for the slot always applies), as do fog volume visibility and area light and 2D occluder poses.
- Between full rewrites the worker rewrites only changed rows, in place, in both buffers ([bridge.md](bridge.md)): an unchanged actor's row is bit-identical from snapshot to snapshot, which is what lets `holdsActorTransform` skip it, and row order changes only with a full rewrite. Readers still match rows by `slotId`, never by row index.
- A tick's commands and its snapshot are separate deliveries, so a frame can draw between them and apply the snapshot of the tick before, which knows nothing of those commands. Two rules keep that snapshot from undoing them. A visual a command built for a slot that no applied snapshot has listed (`seenSlots`) is not retired by a snapshot that omits the slot. The rows of a slot a `despawn` command retired are skipped until the view receives its next snapshot (`despawnPlaySlot`, `notePlaySnapshotReceived`; `SnapshotSampler.push` reports the buffers it ignores), so the row of the actor that left neither builds a visual for the freed slot nor marks the slot seen, and never poses or shows an actor that took the slot in the same tick. Slot ids are recycled ([bridge.md](bridge.md)): a slot left marked seen would have the next actor's visual retired by a snapshot that predates it, and that actor would stay invisible until it despawned.
- Main-thread apply: [`snapshot-apply.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/snapshot-apply.ts) keeps the per-snapshot apply and the slot lifecycle (migrate, retire, dispose) and re-exports by name the public API of the modules behind it, which importers and `index.ts` reach only through it. In dependency order, each importing only those before it and none importing `snapshot-apply.ts`: [`snapshot-binding.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/snapshot-binding.ts) (`SnapshotSceneBinding`, `SlotKeyedMap`, `SlotRootMap`: `binding.meshes`, which indexes its roots on every write so Play outlines exclude other slots' roots without copying every slot, component mesh names), [`snapshot-visibility.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/snapshot-visibility.ts) (snapshot visibility, helper metadata, world copies of overlay slots), [`snapshot-materials.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/snapshot-materials.ts) (Material assignment, parameters, retained owners), [`snapshot-illumination.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/snapshot-illumination.ts) (helper lights and cameras, active camera), [`snapshot-spring-arms.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/snapshot-spring-arms.ts) (spring-arm lag), [`snapshot-component-commands.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/snapshot-component-commands.ts) (component poses and per-component commands), [`snapshot-visual-factory.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/snapshot-visual-factory.ts) (`createPlayVisual`, `createPlayMesh`) and [`snapshot-assign-mesh.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/snapshot-assign-mesh.ts) (`applyAssignMesh` and its strategies). Each keeps its per-binding and per-mesh WeakMaps private behind accessors.
- Overlay Play: size from the overlay box with `engine.setSize`; `clearBeforeCopy`; disable other enabled Scene views until Play closes (restore exactly those); the paused editor view under it frees its prepared graph and render targets (`syncEditorPlayState` → `EngineHandle.releaseViewResources` → `SceneRenderCoordinator.releaseResources`; a view still preparing keeps them), so the shared Engine never holds both views' multisampled targets, and its first frame after Play prepares them again; `scene.autoClear = true`; `skipPointerMovePicking` on world scenes. Custom resolution with Black Bars locks the framebuffer and letterboxes with `fitContainedRect`, never CSS `object-fit: fill`; with Upscaling Pixelated (`pixelatedUpscaling`, Custom Resolution with or without Black Bars) it enlarges by the largest whole multiple in device pixels with CSS `image-rendering: pixelated` (`presentedFramebufferStyle`, overlay Play and the player alike): with Black Bars the locked design size (`fitWholeMultipleRect`); without them (`pixelatedExtend`) the host locks the framebuffer at its box's device pixels divided by that multiple (`playFramebufferSize` with the container; the Engine cannot measure the box, so a runtime output change leaves the size to the host), and `setSceneCameraLensExtent` widens every authored lens by the extra height over the design height (`pixelatedLensExtent`: orthographic size and the field of view's tangent; `lensPropertiesFromCamera` reads the authored lens back, unless something changed the camera since), so the design size keeps its scale and the extra rows show more scene. Pixelated draws the world at the full design size: Play's `effectiveResolution` pins scale 1, and the Scalability readback reports the clamp. Nothing softens it either: `updateSceneRenderingSettings` resolves Anti-Aliasing to Off (no MSAA, no FXAA) and drops FSR from the resolved effects after the project, Scene and session layers, for the editor viewport and Play alike (`RenderShadingSettings` carries `customResolution` and `upscaling`; a session output change in Play passes its own); the settings rows say so instead of hiding the stored values. Scene Layers draw into the same framebuffer, so UI enlarges with the game. Play restores the hardware scale captured before it started.
- Play output with Scene Layers (`composited` in `create-engine.ts`, from the first layer to the last): the backbuffer is the full output, the view's device pixels (the Engine level is 1 / `devicePixelRatio`, which keeps `pickBufferSize` in CSS pixels) or the locked resolution, and the `HardwareScalingController` level (Resolution Scale or Engine Settings) becomes the world's output scale (`setSceneOutputScale`, [`scene-output-scale.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/scene-output-scale.ts)) instead of the Engine's. `ForwardSceneFrameGraph` renders a scaled view at that scale (then FSR's render scale) and its single output pass fills the backbuffer: the chain's last stage or its output copy. FSR upscales and sharpens to the view's size (the CSS size on a high-density screen) and that copy fills the backbuffer, so the two FSR passes never run at the device's resolution (`SceneRendering.fsrOutputAtView`; `fsrdebug output device` runs FSR's tail at the backbuffer's size to measure it); a view with no chain draws into own scene targets for that copy. A scale change rebuilds the graph. Without layers the Engine level is the world's, as in the editor, with no extra copy. Size consumers that read the backbuffer for the world (mirrors, planar water, the water scene copy, diagnostics `width`/`height`) go through `sceneViewRenderSize`; diagnostics `scalingLevel` is the world level.
- Pixel-matched orthographic cameras ([`pixel-matched-camera.ts`](../../../packages/core/src/pixel-matched-camera.ts); `pixelMatchedCameras`: Pixel-Perfect Mode with Nearest Texture Filtering, so a default Basic 2D project, which leaves Nearest off, is unchanged): `pixelMatchedView(requestedHeight, { design, output, pixelsPerUnit })` picks `wholePixelScale(designHeight / ppu / requestedHeight)` design pixels per texel and shows `output / (ppu × scale)` units, where `output` is `sceneLayerOutputDesignSize`, the same output Scene Layers fill (design size with Black Bars, the extended framebuffer under Pixelated, the design size widened to the window's aspect under Smooth), so the camera never stretches and the lens extent does not apply. The requested height is the authored Orthographic Size × 2, or the Layer Camera's bounds fitted to the design aspect over its zoom. Play sets it per Scene (`setScenePixelMatchedLens` from `syncPixelMatchedCameras` in `create-engine.ts`, on resize, locked size and Nearest switches; `WorldLayerCamera.pixelMatch`); the editor sets it without an output for camera previews and frustum guides (`EngineHandle.setPixelMatchedCameras`, the design size widened to the lens's aspect), and its orange outline is `pixelMatchedDesignFrame` (`sceneLayerCameraFrame` with `pixelMatched`). The runtime mirrors it with `pixelMatchedCameras` in its load options and `SceneLayerOverlay.pixelMatchContext`: cursor and touch rays use the whole output's view; Get Orthographic Size reports the design area's half-height (`pixelMatchedDesignFrame`), so Set with it keeps the ratio. `snapPlayCameraToPixelGrid` snaps a pixel-matched camera's left and bottom view edges to the design-pixel grid (the texel grid when zoomed out) instead of its centre, so odd output sizes stay pixel-true. New scenes in such a project (the Create Project starter scene and New Asset > Scene) take `projectSceneCameraFraming`: Orthographic Size = design height / Pixels Per Unit / 2 and Layer Camera bounds = design size / Pixels Per Unit; a Camera component added later keeps the engine default (5), and its Inspector status shows the ratio it picks.
- A Scene Layer's editor tab (`fullQuality` option) sizes its view at its canvas's device pixels with its own `customResize`, never writes the Engine-wide level, and its Scene is `pinFullRenderQuality`. It also plans none of the spatial effects (`excludeSceneSpatialEffects` in [`render-settings.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/render-settings.ts)): no ambient occlusion, reflections, volumetric lighting, Fog Volume pass or Screen-Space Shadows, whatever the project, tier or Scene enables, so its graph has no spatial geometry pass, effect passes or their targets, and its lit draws do not read Screen-Space Shadows. It draws as Play draws a layer (`excludeSceneWorldPostEffects`): the default effects Play's layer Scenes keep, which no project, Scene or session setting reaches (no bloom, Scene Linear display stage, tone mapping, white balance, vignette, LUT or upscale), and no Anti-Aliasing (Play's layers draw single-sampled, without FXAA, onto a backbuffer without antialiasing), so it plans no effect chain.
- A 2D editor view with Pixel-Perfect Mode (`EditorTools.setPixelPerfect` non-null, only while the viewport is 2D and the project has it on) draws at device pixels the same way (`devicePixels` in `create-engine.ts`: the view's level only, `customResize`, canvas `image-rendering: pixelated` for the sub-pixel floor), without pinning quality. Like a Scene Layer tab it never writes the Engine-wide level; turning it off restores the level its `HardwareScalingController` holds. Its camera (`editor-camera.ts`, [`pixel-perfect.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/pixel-perfect.ts)) keeps a continuous zoom and shows `pixelZoomDetent(zoom × pixelRatio)` device pixels per texel: the continuous scale, or the whole level (1, 2, 3… or 1/2, 1/3…) while within `PIXEL_ZOOM_DETENT` (6%) of it. Only the shown scale settles; gestures keep moving the zoom, so a slow wheel or pinch leaves a level once it passes the band (no hysteresis needed). Zoom 1 is one texel per CSS pixel; and `pixelPerfectOrthoBounds` puts the frustum's left and bottom edges on the world's device-pixel grid on every view-matrix change, so texel edges always fall between device pixels. Picking stays correct because `pickBufferSize` maps CSS to the backbuffer proportionally.
- `EngineHandle.samplePresentedImage` ([`presented-image.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/presented-image.ts)) answers at the end of the next presented frame (`framePresented`: not a loading or held frame) with a 64-pixel-wide downsample of what the player sees, the Engine canvas while the view presents directly and the view canvas otherwise, read in the task that presented it, so the WebGL drawing buffer still holds the frame without preserveDrawingBuffer. Waiting callers get null on a context loss or disposal. The Feature Test Check fails a scene whose samples are mostly black.
- Engine Settings `postProcessingEnabled` gates only editor viewports and in-editor Play, never exported games or the Material editor preview.
- `visibilitychange` (and Capacitor app state) pauses the render scheduler, game tick and texture encode queue. A blocking overlay counts as closed only with `data-closed` or `hidden`; Base UI marks open backdrops `aria-hidden`.
- Editor scenes use `ScenePerformancePriority.BackwardCompatible` plus `applyEditorMaterialFreeze`. Babylon binds a frozen NodeMaterial's blocks only when a draw's Material or Effect differs from the previous draw's, so per-mesh values on a shared Material leak from one mesh to the next; the compiler rebinds them from `onBindObservable` for every draw of a frozen Material: bone palettes, morph weights, text, lattice, overlay style, water waves, the geometry pass's caster mark, the light block's Receive Shadows terms (`HapticLightingBlock.bindReceiver`: `hapticShadowReceiver` and the screen shadows' read) and, there too, the Scene's light and cluster blocks (other programs reuse their binding points). `e2e/shadow-participation.spec.ts` checks each Cast/Receive combination of a cube over a plane, frozen and pooled as the editor draws, as Play draws and unpooled, with Screen-Space Shadows off and at High.
- Play uses `ScenePerformancePriority.Intermediate` and `playMode` skips the editor freeze so packed textures can rebuild. Intermediate marks every mesh `alwaysSelectAsActiveMesh`; Play's world Scene clears it before each cull on meshes added since (`play-view-culling.ts`, `settlePlayViewCulling` from `CameraOutputCullTask`), so the camera culls by view. It walks `scene.meshes` only when the list can hold a mesh added since its last walk: its length or last mesh changed, or the Scene announced a removal (`onMeshRemovedObservable`, synchronous), as Babylon only appends (`addMesh`) and removes in `removeMesh`; a steady frame reads three values. Kept always drawn: meshes the engine marks (`drawWhateverTheView`: particle emitters, spring-arm debug lines), geometry-less meshes, skyboxes and infinite-distance meshes, meshes a pose moves on the GPU with no bounds to follow it (morph targets, baked vertex animation, and a skinned mesh whose bounds cannot follow its skeleton, below) and thin instances whose bounds hold the source mesh alone; a regular instance is culled by its own bounds unless its source is kept. Culled like any mesh, as the editor's view culls them, are those whose bounds are made to hold what their vertex stage does: a Material's padded bounds, a lattice's cage, water's wave reach, and foliage cells, whose bounds hold every instance (`markBoundsHoldInstances`); occlusion culling may then leave them out too. A Play actor's root has its world matrix frozen by each snapshot that poses it (`shouldFreezeStaticWorldMatrix`), which reads only a mark made on purpose (`drawnWhateverTheViewOnPurpose`): Babylon's own is still on every mesh when its first snapshot arrives, and stays on one without geometry. SceneLayers and editor Scenes are unchanged. A texture-load rebuild must unfreeze → build → reapply authored blend → mark dirty with dirty-block off → refreeze.
- A view's cull tests a mesh by what of it can show ([`cull-accuracy.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/cull-accuracy.ts), `cullSceneByWhatShows`, once per Scene a `SceneRenderCoordinator` draws). By its box, not its sphere alone: Babylon's default strategy keeps any mesh whose bounding sphere touches the frustum, and a long or flat mesh's sphere reaches far past it. `CULLINGSTRATEGY_OPTIMISTIC_INCLUSION` accepts a mesh whose centre is in view at once and tests the rest by sphere, then box; mirror and capture lists test with the same strategy (`mesh.isInFrustum`). And never a mesh a pose moves on the GPU with no bounds to follow it (`posedBeyondBounds`: morph targets, baked vertex animation, a skinned mesh that is not followed; an instance as its source): its bounds hold its vertices as authored, which a pose, even a model's rest pose, puts elsewhere, so it is marked `alwaysSelectAsActiveMesh` in every Scene, the editor's and the previews' as in Play (below); mirror, capture and probe lists, occlusion culling and the outline masks then keep it too. Babylon announces a new mesh on a later task, until which it is culled by its sphere.
- A skinned mesh's bounds follow its skeleton ([`pose-bounds.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/pose-bounds.ts), [`skinned-bounds.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/skinned-bounds.ts)), so every cull treats it like any mesh: the view's, mirror, capture and probe lists, occlusion culling, and each shadow layer's caster list.
  - **The box.** A skinned vertex is a weighted mean of where each of its bones puts it, so it lies in the hull of those places. Per bone, the box of the vertices it moves is taken once per Geometry (`skinnedBoneBoxes`, rounded outwards to 32 bits; actors of one Model share it); per pose, each box moved by its bone's skinning matrix (`skeleton.getTransformMatrices(mesh)`, in the mesh's own space) and joined (`posedBox`, by centre and half extents, a ten-thousandth of its size wider for the GPU's 32-bit arithmetic) holds every posed vertex.
  - **Who is followed** (`followPose`, asked as a Scene's cull settles a mesh: `cullSceneByWhatShows`, `settlePlayViewCulling`): a mesh posed by its skeleton alone, on the GPU, whose positions, bone indices and weights (the extra set too, past four influences) the CPU holds in a buffer nothing rewrites. Not one with morph targets, a baked vertex animation or a lattice, nor one skinned on the CPU: it stays drawn whatever the view. A LOD level and an instance answer for their master and source, and a master's bounds hold its levels' geometry too. A mesh that later loses what it was followed for is let go: bounds as authored (`clearDynamicMaterialBounds`) and `alwaysSelectAsActiveMesh` again.
  - **When** (`updatePoseBounds`): once an Engine frame, first of the Scene's `onBeforeRenderObservable` work (after animations and the ragdoll's and attachments' writes, before mirror, capture and probe lists, the view's cull and the shadow lists), and at once when a mesh is followed. Babylon computes a skeleton's matrices only for a mesh a cull kept and only once a render id, so they are computed here, `prepare(true)`, once a call per skeleton for every followed mesh it poses: the glTF loader gives each primitive its own mesh on one skeleton, and a skeleton whose bones are linked to nodes recomputes every bone and re-uploads its matrices on every `prepare` (the node copy marks it dirty), and nothing in the call writes a bone or a node. Babylon's own render-id-checked `prepare` of a drawn skeleton, in the frame's first render list, still repeats that work once a frame for a linked skeleton: skipping it, or skipping a skeleton nothing wrote, needs a write signal for linked nodes that does not exist (writers set node TRS in place; the render change journal sees only world-matrix recomputes). A hidden mesh without instances is left for the frame that shows it, and a mesh whose skeleton did not move is not rewritten (`onBeforeComputeObservable` marks it).
  - **Where the bounds go**: through `updateDynamicMaterialBounds`, which keeps a Material's padding on them and reapplies them when a Material is assigned. Every submesh of a mesh of several gets the mesh's bounds (Babylon culls such a mesh by each one's), and every instance the box at its own world matrix.
  - **Shadows**: `hasDeformingShadowBounds` still says a skinned caster deforms, so the spatial index returns it whatever the planes (its leaves refit only on world-matrix changes) and the refresh policy still redraws the maps it reaches; a followed caster (`deformsWithinBounds`) is then culled per layer by its box in `getCustomRenderList`, takes the far-caster texel rule, and reaches a local light by its own radius.
  - `setScenePoseBounds(scene, false)` follows none of a Scene's meshes, for the browser proof's reference run (`e2e/pose-bounds.spec.ts`: two actors of a skinned glTF, exact images against always drawn, fewer draws out of view).
- Editor apply is incremental through `EditorSceneSync` ([`editor-scene-sync.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/editor-scene-sync.ts)); work stays proportional to the change. Material rebinding reports net changes only (`withNetMaterialChanges`); unchanged post-process stacks and in-place light uniform edits keep the prepared graph. A transform-only edit (`isTransformOnlySceneEdit`) re-realizes only the actors whose pose changed (`EditorSceneSync.applyPoses`; both compare poses number by number with `samePose`, never serialized), refreezing the actor roots under them and running the Scene-wide cable and illumination passes; it falls back to the full apply while a realization, visual replacement or model load is in flight, when the Scene has an overlay layout (a move can relayout other actors), and when a moved actor's visual fingerprint changes (a 2D Panel's scale).
- Full editor loads and actor visuals (mesh names, origin hierarchies, transforms, freeze, cable previews, `applySceneToBabylonScene`) live in [`scene-loader.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/scene-loader.ts). It delegates actor classification to `scene-visual-classify.ts`, rebuild fingerprints to `scene-visual-fingerprint.ts`, the per-component mesh factory to `scene-component-mesh.ts` and Mesh collision dashes to `mesh-collision-dashes.ts`, and re-exports their public names. A component's fingerprint kind must change whenever the factory would build a different mesh, because `EditorSceneSync` rebuilds only on a fingerprint change. `TimelineProxyShapeComponent` is an editor-only class the Timeline Clip asset viewport adds (never authored or in Play): a solid, opaque, pickable unit box (a square in 2D) in the proxy colour on the unlit helper material, the box's faces shaded by a fixed light baked into vertex colours (`createTimelineProxyShapeMesh`, [`editor-volume.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/editor-volume.ts)).
- Editor canvases render continuously at the viewport cap and freeze when hidden, off-screen, resizing, under a Dialog/AlertDialog/Sheet, while Play/Preview Build runs, or while Save All runs ([`render-scheduler.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/render-scheduler.ts), [`apps/editor/src/lib/viewport-render-gate.ts`](../../../apps/editor/src/lib/viewport-render-gate.ts)). Static previews idle after a ready frame.
- **Half Rate When Idle** (Engine Settings `viewportIdleHalfRate`, default on): an editor viewport idle for `IDLE_HALF_RATE_DELAY_MS` (1 s) draws at half its cap; it never stops, so animated materials, water and particles keep moving. Only `applyLiveEngineSettings` turns it on (`RenderScheduler.setIdleHalfRate`), and only for views that take the Viewport Frame Cap (Scene, Prefab and Timeline clip previews); Play, Simulation, Preview Build and the player never get it, and a held render lease never halves. Half rate keeps the full-rate cadence and skips every other slot, so the first activity draws at the next full-rate slot. Activity: every `invalidate` (camera, gizmo hover and drags, selection and hover outlines, edits, assets), `requestPausedFrame`, lease acquire and release, `setPaused`, `setResizing`, `setObstructed`, the view shown again, the setting turned on, and `noteActivity` from [`create-engine.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/create-engine.ts): pointer, wheel, touch and keyboard input over the canvas and view-matrix changes of the view's camera ([`view-activity.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/view-activity.ts)), world or layer loading and pending presentations, held frames (shader compiles), performance recordings, frame captures, presented-image readbacks, `resize` and runtime commands. Signals raised while a frame draws (editor cables stepping) are that frame's animation, not activity. `renderDiagnostics().frameAdmission` reports `idleHalfRate` and `halfRate`; `frameCap` stays the configured cap.
- Gates on an editor canvas compose; none ends another. `setPaused` (one flag per Engine handle, written by Play, scene loads and the app lifecycle pause through `EditorSchedulerRegistry.setPaused`) also pauses its audio and particles. Obstruction leases (`acquireObstruction`, reference-counted) only stop ready frames: loading frames still present under them, and while a Play scene or Scene Layer load is pending, owners already ready keep drawing under its blocker (`shouldRenderReadyOwners`), each frame then leaving the main thread free for as long as it took (`noteRendered`'s end time), so a world whose frames run long (software GL draws the Feature Test main scene at about 3 s a frame) cannot starve the load's source preparation, which awaits storage between frames; a frame that fits its cadence is not held back. Every gate also leaves the main thread free after a frame that held it past its own work (on software GL with software compositing the browser waits there for the GPU to finish the frame, about 3 s for the Feature Test main scene): from the second frame in a row whose next check comes at least `HELD_THREAD_MS` (500 ms) after its work ended, the next frame waits as long, up to a second, so input, timers, worker replies and a test driver's commands still run between such frames (one such gap alone may be a long task after the frame). The count is dropped when Play pauses or the view's visibility changes. Scene edits still reach Babylon under a lease, because the viewport's scene load applies them through `EditorSceneSync` (`loadScene`, or `loadSceneAsync` yielding on timers) when the document changes, not in a frame. Save All holds one lease on every scene and Prefab viewport registered with `EditorSchedulerRegistry` (`registerScheduler`; viewports mounted during the hold included) through [`editor-frame-hold.ts`](../../../apps/editor/src/lib/editor-frame-hold.ts), because each storage await in it resumes only after any frame drawn meanwhile. Play's game view, Preview Build, a Simulation's view, asset previews and thumbnails never register, so a save never holds them. The selected camera's or capture's lens preview (`EditorDebugOverlay`) takes no timed passes while its Engine is paused (`applyPause` calls `setPaused`), so a game never pays for it, nor while its viewport is off screen (`RenderScheduler.isShown`). It redraws at the **Camera Preview Frame Rate (FPS)** Engine Setting (default 30), read each tick. Its RTT carries the view's Display Color stage when the view has one: an `ImageProcessingPostProcess` with its own `sceneEffectsImageProcessingConfiguration` from the scene's effects plan (from linear HDR, into a half-float input, in Scene Linear), rebuilt when tone mapping, exposure, contrast, white balance, vignette or LUT change, and the preview waits while its shader compiles; materials draw into it as they do in the view, so a Scene Linear preview is not linear colour shown as display colour (dark and oversaturated). Without a display stage materials encode display colour themselves and the RTT has no post process. A camera lens draws no editor helpers (`isEditorHelperMesh`), and the preview RTT's `getCustomRenderList` applies the scene cull's `visibility > 0` and layer-mask checks, which an explicit render list skips.
- Picking: viewport taps use a 1×1 GPU object-ID pass ([`gpu-object-id-picker.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/gpu-object-id-picker.ts)) that follows skinning, morphs and WPO and pins the floating-origin context to its scene while it draws (outside `Scene.render` Babylon uses the last scene to render, the gizmo layer in the editor, whose full-view matrices would sample the viewport centre). Babylon writes an object id only where fragment alpha exceeds 0.4 but writes depth regardless, so the pass draws pickable meshes hidden through `visibility` (actor-origin pick spheres) at 0.999 visibility for the probe and draw: otherwise they would go unpicked and hide what lies behind them, such as their billboard; marquee, brushes and transform tools use the CPU ray ([`picking.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/picking.ts)). A batch of regular instances draws under its source's id, so the pass numbers each pickable instance's source too (a hidden source draws only its instances), and a hit on a source whose instances it drew is resolved by a ray through the tapped pixel's centre against the source (when pickable) and those instances; when the ray meets none of them (a deformed batch) the tap falls back to the CPU ray. Instances are pick candidates by their own bounds, as their source would be.
- Meshes without a Material draw `installEngineDefaultMaterial`: a NodeMaterial compiled from `engineDefaultMaterialDocument()` (a procedural grey checker, lit, with an Emission parameter that 2D sets to 1), so it lights, shades in CEL and Unlit mode and casts shadows as an authored Material does. The checker is world-aligned, not UV-mapped: quarter-unit cells (`ENGINE_DEFAULT_CHECKER_CELLS_PER_UNIT`) of absolute World Position, in the plane across the World Normal's dominant axis (ties to X, then Y), so scale never stretches a cell, neighbouring meshes' cells meet, and a face on a cell boundary never flickers; a moving mesh slides under its cells. Primitives keep `mesh.material === null` so the Inspector shows None.
- Editor selection, gizmos and debug overlays never change Scene membership (pre-created hidden guides, entity-free cameras/RTTs); a membership change costs a whole-scene readiness probe.
- Possess Camera With Blend: a `possessCamera` with `blend` first applies the possession, then, when the new game camera differs from the previous active camera and has its projection mode, views through `ViewBlend`'s own `playViewBlend` camera ([`view-blend.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/view-blend.ts)). It eases from the previous view's frozen world pose and lens to the live game camera (`resolvePlayGameCamera`) each rendered frame (`stepViewBlend`, real time between frames, `sampleEasingCurve`), then disposes itself and `refreshPlayActiveCamera` restores the game camera, which skips `playViewBlend` as it skips the free camera. Different projections cut. Another camera that takes the view (the free camera) ends the blend.
- Camera shake (`setCameraShake`): `CameraShakeView` ([`camera-shake-view.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/camera-shake-view.ts)) offsets a Scene's rendering camera in `onBeforeRenderObservable` and restores exactly what it changed in `onAfterRenderObservable`, so snapshot poses, blends and scripts never see the shake; position moves along the camera's local rotation, rotation applies in its own frame, Field Of View widens (an orthographic camera zooms its bounds about their centre as a 60° view would widen). The world view's camera is `scene.frameGraph.findMainCamera()` (Babylon clears `activeCamera` at the start of a graph render), never the free camera; each Scene Layer gets one from `SceneLayerCompositor.setShake`, disposed with the layer. The Camera Shake editor Preview (`createCameraShakePreviewScene`, [`camera-shake-preview.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/camera-shake-preview.ts)) draws a code-built set merged into one mesh under the preview sky through the shared preview presenter (`PreviewPresenterHost`).

## Feature owners

| Feature | Source | Rules |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Post-process stacks | `scene-post-process-*.ts`, `framegraph-post-process.ts`, `owned-post-process.ts` | Each entry owns its Material instance; stable entry IDs survive reorder. A retired pass removes the shader sources it registered (`OwnedPostProcess`), which every graph build's pass of the same Material build shares by name, only once no live pass holds them: Babylon reads a new Effect's sources after its shader imports load, a tick or more after the pass registered them, and a source removed meanwhile was fetched as a URL and failed to compile. `whenDisposed()` is a bounded report; only `whenReleased()` permits host Scene/library disposal. Missing buffer or failed pass skips only that pass. |
| Settings effects | `scene-effects-graph.ts`, `bloom-blur-task.ts`, `display-color-task.ts`, `spatial-effects*.ts`, `fsr1-tasks.ts`, `color-grading.ts` | AO/SSR/volumetrics before bloom and Display Color; FSR after; LUT is display-referred. Tail: `FrameGraphBloomBlurTask` (Babylon's bloom downscale and two blurs, their thin post processes and targets at `bloom.scale`, each blur compiled once at its final kernel; no merge) → `FrameGraphDisplayColorTask`, one full-size pass that adds the blurred highlights as Babylon's merge does, holds the sum as the merge's own target of the source's type stored it (RGBA8: clamped, rounded half to even in 1/255 steps; half-float: `packHalf2x16`), then runs Babylon's image processing (`Scene Effects Display Color`; without image processing it is bloom's merge alone, `Scene Effects Bloom Merge`), then, with shared outlines, composites their stroke (its `OUTLINE` stage, [Outlines](#feature-owners)) → FXAA → FSR. The pass writes the separate merge and image-processing passes' colour and alpha (neither changes alpha) without their full-size merge target and pass. Its image processing takes `ThinImageProcessingPostProcess`'s define set, uniforms and samplers from the same dedicated configuration (`prepareDefines`, `bind`), and it compiles once, when it records and the source's type is known, never a default variant first; a configuration change that changes its defines compiles again and retires the replaced Effect (`retireOwnedEffect`), which `SceneEffectsGraph.whenReleased` awaits. The bloom texture is sampled bilinear, set by the pass: a graph target starts nearest, and the blur target may alias another. A view's chain leaves out a stage that writes its input bit for bit (`withoutIdleStages`, proofs in its comment): bloom at weight 0 unless it is the chain's only stage, and Real-Time Reflections at strength 0 in Scene Linear unless an authored stack feeds the chain or its removal would leave volumetrics the geometry pass alone (a seeded pass's depth differs); ambient occlusion at strength 0 stays (its compose's `max(weights, 1e-6)` floor darkens a pixel whose taps are all depth-rejected). Strength is in `sceneEffectsKey`, so a change rebuilds the graph under the readiness hold. The one data-dependent difference: a non-finite input pixel turned the pixels within bloom's or the compose's reflection reach NaN through `blur · 0`; skipped, they keep their colour. Their geometry pass (`SpatialEffectsGraph`) draws the view's meshes before the objects, and clears its own depth as it starts (`LogicalGeometryTask.clearsDepth`): a clear task before it would be a render pass of its own that stores the cleared depth for the pass to load. In a game's world view (`seedsFromSceneDepth`: Play's and the player's, never the editor viewport, whose depth holds its helper visuals) a pass that only feeds the view depth (Fog Volumes or scene-wide volumetrics, with no ambient occlusion, reflections or Screen-Space Shadows planned, whose normals, reflectivity and caster marks only the meshes' own draws give) instead follows the objects and starts from their depth ([`geometry-depth-seed.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/geometry-depth-seed.ts)): one full-screen draw writes each pixel's view depth, computed from the scene's depth (resolved by sample where the object pass is multisampled), and that depth itself (so the pass clears none), and the pass then draws only the meshes that depth lacks (`writesSceneDepth`: blended surfaces without a depth pre-pass or a forced depth write), blended over it as in its own draws. Its readiness probe asks only for those meshes, so opaque Materials compile no geometry variant there, and whatever writes the view's depth bounds the fog. `e2e/spatial-effects.spec.ts` compares every Fog Volume capture of a seeded pass with the pass's own draws, also with a multisampled scene. SSR reflectivity writers: [Rendering settings](#rendering-settings). Unsupported devices keep base rendering. The volumetric march has a fixed shape (`volumetric-shader.ts`): one variant per settings (Max Lights slots, steps, Fog Volumes present), never per light, so a light coming within reach, leaving, or gaining, changing or losing its shadow map rebinds uniforms only, with no rebuild, hold or fog pop. Each slot reads its light's type and its shadow kind (`VOLUME_SHADOW_CODE`: none, a spot light's view, a point light's faces, the sun's cascades) and view count from uniforms, and the march skips slots past `volumeLightCount` with one uniform branch per step. Slots are ordered directional light first (only the first slot reads cascades), then creation. The march selects its lights and resolves each slot's shadow when it draws, after the frame's shadow task (`bindVolumetricLights` in `volumetric-lights.ts`); a light with no views drawn draws unshadowed. The march has no maps of its own: each slot's `volumeShadow` (kind, views, first view) names its light's views in the block (`hapticShadowViews`: a spot light's one, a point light's faces, the shadowed sun's cascades), which it reads from the bound `HapticLights` and samples in the shadow atlas with one bilinear compare tap, the block's projection without its surface filtering (`hapticShadowGlsl(false)`), choosing, blending and fading the sun's cascades as the block does; its samplers are the scene depth and the atlas. A stage whose fragment reads a uniform buffer is drawn from the shader store (`customWrapper`): Babylon builds an inline source's Effect with a constructor that takes no uniform buffers. `e2e/spatial-effects.spec.ts` checks that the occluder cuts this march's fog exactly where the view's rays cross its shadow (a ray-cast mask) and leaves the fog elsewhere as the unshadowed light's. |
| Render targets | `render-target-capture*.ts` | One producer per target per frame; a Fast colour capture that samples itself (a monitor in its own view) needs no second buffer, since its pass draws its own scene colour target and its Materials sample the output's last stored image; captures exclude helpers but keep particle emitter meshes (Babylon draws a system only with its emitter in the pass list); no recursive RTT draws. The scene view's `onBeforeRender` draws Fast captures (before evaluation: skinning and particles one frame behind) and queues High and Full Quality ones for its graph. A Depth Pass target is R16F: only red carries the depth, and it samples as the (d, 0, 0, 1) the capture writes and clears. Quality (`fast` default; absent loads as fast) applies to Scene Color only: Fast is a manual RTT pass (no scene copy, effects, post-process or outlines; depth pre-pass off) over the eligible meshes its camera sees (Babylon culls no explicit list: the capture tests each against its camera's frustum at its target's shape, `drawsInsidePlanes`, as a Mirror's pass does; an emitter stays for its particles); a Capture Only Actors capture of any quality tests only the meshes under its included actors' roots (every mesh its actors own descends from one; a mesh another actor owns is still left out), so its list matches the walk of every Scene mesh without making it: every 120th list of a capture, and every list under `verifyCaptureActorLists` (perf route `HAPTIC_PERF_VERIFY_CAPTURES=1`), is that walk instead, and a list the actors' meshes would have made differently is counted in `rendererWork.captureActorLists.mismatches`, which must stay 0 that draws with the view's own programs into the capture's scene colour target (with the depth; half-float where the view is Scene Linear, as the view's own, and made anew when that changes), which one pass (`DisplayConversion`, as a Reflection Probe's faces) then stores display-referred in the output, colour alone, so a capture compiles no Material a second time; Depth Pass and World Normal set a mesh's pass material (its depth or normal variant, or a per-mesh slot container) only when it or the container's children change, since Babylon's `setMaterialForRenderPass` drops the mesh's draw wrappers for that pass on every set (a MultiMaterial mesh still loses every pass's wrappers whenever the main and capture passes alternate, as Babylon's `SubMesh.getMaterial` resets them when the effective sub-material changes). A kept wrapper's cached readiness is cleared on every capture, so a frozen variant still prepares its defines there: dirty calls run in the main pass and reach only submeshes whose material there is theirs, never a variant's, so an in-place define input such as a lattice cage attaching or leaving would otherwise keep the old program; High and Full Quality are an auxiliary forward view with its own sun maps (`attachView`), its own water scene copy (its transparent pass id) and a mirror of the gameplay outline contributions (`setOutlines`, `SceneOutlineHost.attachCaptureView`; never the selection; with Capture Only Actors only the included actors' outlines and occluders, refreshed on include or actor-root changes). Full Quality also runs the scene's screen effects and the scene view's post-process stack (`setPostProcess`, parameter writes forwarded). High Quality (`auxiliary.screenEffects: false`) draws the plan without them (`planWithoutScreenEffects`: no bloom, vignette or LUT; in Scene Linear a Display Color stage that only encodes, `displayEncodingEffects`) and attaches no stack, so a monitor's main view applies them once; its output is display colour clipped at white, as Fast's. No planar mirror (`SLATE_WATER_PLANAR` still compiles; its lookup is null there). Its graph, output and lease are freed only after the graph's confirmed release (`whenReleased`), and its sun maps' tiles at once, on disable, removal, quality or asset change, scene change and handle teardown. |
| Outlines | `shared-outline*.ts`, `scene-outline-host.ts` | One bounded FrameGraph owner for selection, Outline components and CEL; at most three mask passes, one depth copy and one compose per view; IDs ≤ 65,535, never recycled. The stroke's maths exists once, the GLSL include `hapticSharedOutlineStroke` ([`shared-outline-stroke.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shared-outline-stroke.ts), with its uniform and sampler lists): the task's own composition (`hapticSharedOutlineCompose`, blended `ALPHA_COMBINE`) and the Display Color pass's `OUTLINE` stage both include it. Where a settings chain has a Display Color pass, that pass composites the stroke and the task runs composed externally (`externalCompose`, decided by `SceneEffectsGraph`, so a rebuild picks the mode): it records its mask passes (clears, depth copy, masks) and no composition pass or Effect, measures its size from the pass's target (the output it was given, else its source, whose size its own target takes; the pass rejects masks of another size), and exposes `strokeTextures` (the pass's dependencies, so the masks draw before it and live until it has drawn), `setStrokeSampling` (nearest), `bindStroke` (uniforms, and samplers with a group without targets bound to its style table, never a render target) and `strokesShow`; its counts leave out the composition. The stage runs after image processing's last step (strokes are not tone mapped, dithered, clamped or bloomed) and reproduces the blend into the target the pass writes: where a stroke lands it rounds its result as that target stored it (`OUTLINE_UNORM8`: RGBA8, 1/255 steps; `OUTLINE_HALF_FLOAT`), mixes the colour by coverage and adds the coverage to the alpha, which an RGBA8 target clamps to 1 and a half-float one keeps (above 1); elsewhere the result is untouched, so frames are byte-identical to the separate pass. A frame where no stroke can show (the task's passes skipped) binds every group inactive (`activeGroups` 0): the stage reads no mask (they hold an earlier frame's identities) and costs three uniform branches and its seven bound samplers; nothing rebuilds or recompiles. The pass then binds at most ten samplers (scene colour, bloom, LUT, three masks, three style tables, strict depth). A view whose outlines start or end (attached, detached, every contribution emptied) rebuilds and switches the pass between its two programs: the view keeps the last of each under one effects key (`ForwardSceneFrameGraph.displayColorPrograms`), so an editor selection that comes and goes links each once; both go on an effects-key change, with the view and with a lost context (as `effect-bridge.ts`'s kept Effects do). A view's task creates a group's mask and depth target and passes only for the groups active as its graph is built (the through and selection groups share one depth through the graph's allocator; the reservation counts both), so Play, which has no selection, never creates the selection group's. A group that activates later builds the graph again (`coversActiveGroups`, read by `outlineMatches`); one that deactivates keeps its targets, its passes disabled, until the next build, so a selection toggled beside an authored outline, or an outline a script toggles every tick, rebuilds the graph at most once per group. The compose binds a group without targets to its style table and reads it as inactive. Babylon culls no explicit list, so each mask's list is narrowed for the frame: its members to those that can show (`cannotShow` in [`shared-outline-occluders.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shared-outline-occluders.ts): not all behind the camera, nor beside the screen by more than the group's widest stroke; a mesh drawn whatever the view, `alwaysSelectAsActiveMesh` or `infiniteDistance`, always stays), and the strict mask's occluders to those that can hide such a member (`cullStrictOccluders`; `cullOutlineMembers` for the through and selection groups). The strict cull walks members and other candidates apart, from a split by membership it keeps while the candidate list, the view's revision and the owner's identity and identity-stream revisions hold (`strictCandidates`), made afresh every `STRICT_SPLIT_CHECK_INTERVAL` culls and replacing the kept one where they differ. Strict members include unstroked meshes that only carry an instance source's identity stream, so a narrowed frame in which no stroked strict member can show and the through and selection groups have no member in view walks no occluder and draws none of the task's passes (clears, depth copy, masks, compose): masks without identities compose to nothing. `verifySharedOutlineCulls` (perf route `HAPTIC_PERF_VERIFY_OUTLINES=1`) also culls the full way and counts disagreements (`rendererWork.outlineCull`). Readiness and a frame without view culling keep the full lists. The narrowed lists live in per-view arrays that Babylon observes: each cull rebuilds into a per-view scratch and rewrites its array only when the meshes or their order change, by index alone (`rewriteUnreadRenderList`), since Babylon answers an observed list that fills by marking every Scene mesh's lights dirty, and no light define reads a mask's list. While nothing is outlined (no CEL outlines, enabled Outline component or selection), `SceneOutlineHost` publishes an empty view once and then only records actor membership, in place: Play spawns and visual changes copy no actor map, rebuild no occluder list and leave the view's revision alone. Leaving idle always takes the validated publish. A Play snapshot apply publishes once, at its end (`SceneOutlineHost.batch`), however many visuals it builds, rebuilds or retires: the batch identifies its actors in call order (`SharedOutlineOwner.identify`), since the lower identity wins an outline tie, refuses a call over identity capacity where its own publish would, and replays one publish per call when the batched publish is refused. A single command (assignMesh, an Outline command, a despawn) publishes at once. A mask prepares every mesh it draws, outlined or an occluder: its coverage comes from the Material's authored variant, a built-in Babylon material, the CEL adapter or the built-in mirror surface material (opaque, so its plain plane); any other custom material fails preparation. A game's world view (Play, the player: `seedsFromSceneDepth`; never the editor viewport, whose depth also holds helper visuals, nor an auxiliary view) seeds the strict mask from the scene's depth where that is a texture of the mask's size (a settings or authored chain's, or the view's own pair's, resolved by sample under MSAA; the backbuffer's cannot be read, and such a view keeps its own occluder draws): one full-screen depth copy (`hapticSharedOutlineDepthSeed`) joins the mask's passes, and the mask draws the outlined meshes and only the occluders that depth lacks (`writesSceneDepth`: a blended surface without a depth pre-pass or forced depth write, or a Material that writes no depth), so an outlined actor no longer redraws the scene in front of it nor compiles its occluders' mask variants. An outlined mesh the colour pass drew is in that depth already, so every draw over it is offset nearer (`SCENE_DEPTH_BIAS`). Whatever else wrote the scene's depth occludes too (a debug collision visual, a line mesh), which the eligible-actor occluder list left out. Under FXAA the image is the same pixel for pixel; a multisampled depth is one sample's per pixel, so a corner pixel where outlined meshes abut can take the neighbour's stroke colour. |
| Particles | `particle-*.ts`, `node-material-particles.ts` | See [particles.md](particles.md). |
| Water | `water-*.ts` | See [Water](#water). |
| Mirrors | `mirrors.ts`, `mirror-selection.ts`, `mirror-reflection-block.ts`, `mirror-surface-state.ts`, `planar-mirror.ts` | One owner per scene draws from `onBeforeRender` through the planar mirror pass water uses (mirror camera and target outside scene membership, borrowed draw under the capture readiness exemption). At most `MIRROR_RENDERS_PER_FRAME` (2) reflections per scene render, from visible surfaces seen from their reflective side (local −Z): never-drawn first, then nearest by the closest point of the rectangle; the rest keep their last image (CPU budget: each is a scene draw). A surface whose reflection is current is no candidate: its view's mirror view-projection, target size and colour type are as drawn and nothing its pass draws changed (`ReflectedContent`, as the water planar reflection follows it), so a static view draws no reflection (`MirrorDiagnostics.reuses` counts the renders that kept one). Volatile content keeps its surface due on every render, within the same budget. A surface whose mirror view-projection moved since its draw (and whose last draw was not empty) is due whatever it shows, so that render does not ask `ReflectedContent` what changed (`MirrorDiagnostics.skippedPolls`): the next ask, at its draw or a later render, reports every change since the last one, and the draw's own ask is what the render the view stops compares with; a draw that does not happen keeps what that ask found due. `verifyMirrorContentPolls` (`HAPTIC_PERF_VERIFY_MIRRORS`) keeps a second record asked on every render and counts the renders it would have decided otherwise (`rendererWork.mirrorPolls.mismatches`). A target allocates at a surface's first draw (`sceneColor` and `depth` in the ledger), lives with the surface and resizes with Resolution Scale or the size the view's scene draws at (`upscaledRenderSize`: its output's, below it under FSR Upscaling; the water planar reflection's target follows it too, at Planar Resolution). Passes cull with the mirrored frustum cropped to the surface's screen footprint (`cropToFootprint`: the rectangle's projection clipped at the eye plane, widened by two target texels for bilinear sampling and by how far a Mirror Reflection node's Offset reaches), since the surface samples its target nowhere else. The reach is a conservative bound on what drives Offset ([`mirror-offset-bound.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/mirror-offset-bound.ts)): per-component interval rules over the compiled Babylon blocks, which hold the values the next draw binds (a parameter edit applies on the next render), for value inputs, add, subtract, multiply, scale, negate, one minus, abs, min, max, clamp, lerp, sin, cos, vector merge and split (with swizzles) and Simplex Perlin noise (|n| at most 1.21: N times the largest sum of the four corner kernels over every x0, per step outcome, recomputed by its test; the function stays near ±1), widened by 0.1 % and 1e-4. Attributes (directly or through the surface plumbing's morph targets), system values and time are unbounded values that only sin, cos and noise bound; any other block, a value that is not finite or an unbounded Offset gives no crop, as does a lattice-deformed surface. `verifyMirrorFootprintCrops` (`HAPTIC_PERF_VERIFY_MIRROR_CROP`) also culls each cropped pass with the whole frustum and counts the meshes only that lists whose bounds reach the widened footprint (`rendererWork.mirrorCrop.misses`, which must stay 0): it checks the crop's geometry for the reach it was given, not the bound itself. Culled-out meshes are neither drawn nor followed (`MirrorDiagnostics.drawnMeshes`). The culls of one Scene render share their candidates (`collectPlanarMirrorCandidates`: the tests no view or plane changes, enabled, visible, layer, geometry, opaque, exclusions, per Reflects kind) until a surface's sun maps or pass are about to run (`listEpoch` moves; a draw that stops before them leaves it), since nothing the owner runs between them writes what those test: the currency checks and the render's first draw walk the Scene once and each cull frustum-tests the candidates (`cullPlanarMirrorCandidates`); a cull after another surface's pass walks afresh, as a pass runs render hooks. `verifyMirrorSharedCulls` (`HAPTIC_PERF_VERIFY_MIRROR_WORK`) also walks the whole Scene for each such cull and counts a list that differs in content or order (`rendererWork.mirrorCulls.mismatches`, which must stay 0); `walkMirrorCullsUnshared` (`HAPTIC_PERF_MIRROR_UNSHARED`, the old path) walks for every cull. `rendererWork.mirrorCulls` also counts the culls, their candidate walks (`walks`: fewer than culls when shared, one per cull unshared) and the reflections drawn (`draws`); `redrawMirrorReflections` (test host) has every surface draw its reflection again on its next render, so `HAPTIC_PERF_MIRROR_PARITY` compares paused frames, each drawn by `requestPausedRedraw` (a paused Play view holds its picture), whose counters show the path each took. The whole update (placement, currency checks, culls, content asks, sun maps and passes) is one `mirrors` owner pass, so none of it counts to `observers`. Water's planar pass keeps the whole frustum: its wave-tilted lookup can reach any texel. Passes draw the opaque list without mirror surfaces or water, so nothing samples the target it draws into. No reflection for orthographic, rig, multi-camera or Scene Linear views without half-float targets, nor inside Render Target Captures. Mirror Reflection (`input.mirrorReflection`) compiles its sampler only under a per-mesh define set for Mirror surfaces, so any other mesh or preview gets black with no binding; before a surface's first draw it binds the scene's one white texel (`mirrorFallbackTexture`) at zero weight. Surfaces reset the scene's cached material before binding, since two surfaces sharing a Material and Effect would otherwise skip the second bind. Reflects (`MirrorProperties.reflects`, `MIRROR_REFLECTS`) chooses what a surface draws live, capped by the quality level's `geometry.mirrorReflections` (`capMirrorReflects`: whichever of the two draws less; `Mirror Reflections` in Project Settings, `quality geometry mirrors <value>`, Everything at every tier, live): a change of the effective value has the surface's Material prepare its defines again for its other variant (a frozen one too), the image is drawn again as after any render settings change, and `environment` gives the target back. `everything` is the pass above. `movingActors` lists only meshes whose actor can move: the list also leaves out the sky (`infiniteDistance`, `isSkyboxMesh`) and every mesh the host calls static (`isSceneStaticMesh` in `scene-static-meshes.ts`: core's `reflectionProbeStaticActors`, the rule a Reflection Probe captures by, answered by the editor through `isEditorStaticMesh` while a document has such a Mirror and by Play through its slots; with no answer every mesh may move). Its target clears to (0, 0, 0, 1) and its pass blends (one, zero) for colour and (zero, zero) for alpha through the Engine's alpha state, so a drawn fragment keeps its colour and writes alpha 0 whatever its Material outputs: the target holds the moving actors premultiplied by their coverage and alpha 1 where nothing drew, bilinear edges included. A draw that listed nothing leaves an image that is open everywhere, which stays current from any view until something that moves enters it (`drawnEmpty`), so such a Mirror costs a cull and no draw. `environment` draws no reflection and holds no target. For both, Mirror Reflection compiles a second per-mesh variant (`SLATE_MIRROR_ENVIRONMENT`) that fills the open part from the cube that lights the surface (`environmentCube`: the Reflection Probe the surface stands in through the per-mesh environment, else the Scene's own), looked up along the view ray mirrored across the surface's plane, projected onto a probe's box as the light block projects reflections and read at the cube's sharpest level; a Scene without one shows its clear colour. The built-in surface adds it in the reflection's own colour space (a display-encoded one clamps and encodes it, as a draw would); an authored Material gets linear colour, the drawn part decoded as its own colour and not as its coverage-weighted one. Live actors have no depth against that background: they draw in front of everything the cube shows. `e2e/mirror.spec.ts` checks the three values with a probe around the Mirror: the middle shows the moving box or, when nothing is drawn, the wall behind it, and the wall beside it comes from the pass or from the probe. The built-in surface is one unlit NodeMaterial per scene, ending in `GeometrySurfaceOutputBlock` like authored Materials, so the effects' geometry passes get its depth and normal and zero reflectivity (Real-Time Reflections add none to its image); a plain `FragmentOutputBlock` left those attachments unwritten, a WebGL error on every draw in view that failed the session's next scene presentation. A null material (a cleared override) restores it. Surfaces never cast shadows (`neverCastsShadows`) and Reflection Probes never capture them (their image belongs to the view). A pass draws the scene view's spot and point shadows, and the sun's only from its mirror camera's own cascades under Reflection Shadows (per-view sun maps, above: drawn just before its pass when due; a surface whose maps are due, or that drew under the other setting or with or without maps, is not current); without them the sun draws unshadowed. `MirrorDiagnostics.sunShadowViews` counts surfaces holding their own. Update Rate (`MirrorProperties.updateRate`, Play only; the gate above): after `place` and the Reflects check, a surface off its phase sits the render out before `current`, so it neither culls, asks `ReflectedContent` or its sun maps, nor draws (`MirrorDiagnostics.rateSkips`; `reuses` and `skippedPolls` do not move). It is a candidate whatever its phase until it has drawn, on the render it comes into view, when its target no longer fits (size or colour type), when the effective Reflects changed, while its last draw left a mesh out (`dirty`) and while it is owed (`due`: set by its phase's render and those causes, cleared by a draw or a `current` reuse). The rate decides who is a candidate and the budget picks among candidates as before, so a due surface the budget passes over stays a candidate on later renders until it draws; `mirror-selection.ts` is unchanged. Any Mirror property change rebuilds the surface (the editor's visual fingerprint holds every property; a Play write reassigns the slot's mesh), so a new rate or Material override draws at once. |
| Text | `text2d-*.ts`, `text3d-mesh.ts` | Not GUI `TextBlock` or `DynamicTexture`; 3D text is a flat TypeFace mesh with injected `earcut`. See [fonts.md](fonts.md). |
| Lattice deformer | `lattice-deformer*.ts` | Order morph → skin → WPO → lattice; `SlateLattice` plugin attaches only to materials an active cage draws, never globally. Fit To Mesh puts a mesh's outer vertices on the cage faces, where the stock inside test is decided by GPU rounding that changed with the camera (faces tore away as the view moved), so `latticeShaderFunctions` accepts a thousandth of a cell outside and clamps onto the cage; the lattice proof's `face-vertices-just-outside` case holds it. A cage reports `revision()` (bumped by every `refresh` and by its root's world matrix taking another value) and `widens(mesh)` (an active cage's part), which let shadow maps track a caged caster instead of redrawing it each frame. |
| Cable, spring arm | `cable-mesh.ts`, `spring-arm.ts`; core `cable-*.ts` | Play simulates cables in the worker; editor steps them on the main thread. A Spring Arm's Camera Probe sweeps in the worker after physics (`runtime/spring-arm-probe.ts`), which sends changed lengths as `springArmProbe`; `snapshot-spring-arms.ts` keeps them per `slotId|componentId` and `updateSpringArmRig` puts the socket there while lag still runs on the host clock. Worker poses read the same length through `springArmSocketOffset`. |
| Dynamic runtime mesh, fog volumes | `dynamic-runtime-mesh.ts`, `fog-volumes.ts` | Limits live in core (1M vertices / 6M indices; 8 admitted fog volumes). |
| Play debug draw | `play-debug-draw.ts` | Duration 0 is one sim tick, not one present. |
| Audio | `audio-service.ts`, `babylon-audio-backend.ts` | AudioV2 behind `AudioPlaybackBackend`; PCM cache separate from `ResourceCache`. |
