| Scene streaming | `scene-stream-*.ts`, `scene-streaming-readiness.ts` | Pending instances stay out of visible/readiness sets until their batch is ready; children use the parent's physics kind, navmesh and environment. |

## Instancing

- [`instance-pool.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/instance-pool.ts) (`sceneInstancePool(scene)`, disposed with the Scene) groups regular instances of one geometry, Material, receiving, casting, rendering group, explicit sort order and handedness (`InstanceGroupKey`; `mirrored` gives a group whose members' world matrices mirror, as every glTF model's mesh does under the loader's handedness root, a mirrored source, because Babylon draws an instance mirrored apart from its source as a mesh of its own (`_actAsRegularMesh`); the geometry's `key` names a cached model's leaf or a primitive template, its `build` clones a source sharing the geometry, its `retain` holds the owner, such as a container lease, while the group lives). A Scene turns it on with `setSceneInstancing`, which Play's world Scene does; it draws through the pool only where the Engine instances in hardware (`caps.instancedArrays`: otherwise Babylon draws each instance on its own; `sceneInstancing`). The readiness probe checks a source (`markInstanceSource`) for its instanced variants only, even while a member's move has left it empty for the rest of the frame.
- A group's source is hidden, unpickable, at identity with its world matrix frozen (the instance constructor copies the source's transform), carries `slateShadowParticipation` `{ castShadows: false, receiveShadows }` (members enter shadow maps one by one; its name never starts with `__`, which shadow participation rejects) and is marked to draw instanced only (`markInstanceSource`). Babylon reads Material, receiving, visibility, rendering group and skeleton from the source for every instance (the instance setters warn and do nothing), so those split groups. Casting splits them too (`castShadows`, default true): the geometry pass draws a group in one batch with one caster mark. The shadow controller moves a member with `setMeshShadowFlags`, one rekey for both flags; a warm-up's stand-in (`spawnStandIn`) casts into no map yet stays in the casting group a spawn joins.
- `join` makes a member at the origin, unparented; `rekey` moves one to another group of the same geometry in place (a Material, receive, cast or rendering group change), keeping its node and every record keyed by it; disposing a member leaves its group. An emptied group that drew is kept for a respawn, which then compiles nothing (`RETAINED_EMPTY_GROUPS`, least recently emptied disposed first, all at `releaseRetained`); one that never drew is disposed at once.
- With instancing on, a Play primitive (box, sphere, cylinder, plane or quad, ground) draws through the pool ([`pooled-primitive-visual.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/pooled-primitive-visual.ts), from `createPlayVisual`): the slot root or named part is a `Mesh` without geometry, which keeps every transform, flag and record keyed by it (picking resolves through its parents), and its drawn leaf, `<name>:drawn`, is a pool member at identity under it, built from the Scene's template of that kind. A slot with deformers stays unpooled. A visual sorted by an explicit layer or order (`applyComponentSorting`) draws in a group of that order (`sortPooledLeaf`, the key's `sortKey`): its leaf carries the root's `alphaIndex` and sorting group, which the sorted passes compare, and one batch draws at one place in the order. What is written for every mesh a visual draws goes through choke points ([`pooled-visuals.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/pooled-visuals.ts)): `setActorMeshMaterial` (from `applyMaterialToActorMeshes` and the construction material's restore and publish) moves the leaf to the Material's group, a private parameter instance included, or, for a Material no group draws (`materialIneligibility`: blended, not triangles, not the stock vertex stage), replaces it with a mesh of its own (`ejectPooledLeaf`); a runtime deformer ejects its targets first. Ejecting notifies `onPooledLeafEjected`, through which Play's visual bindings refresh the slot's outlines, deformers, fog volumes and probes. The shadow controller's receiving and `applyWorldVisualGroup`'s rendering group reach a member through `setMeshReceivesShadows` and `setMeshRenderingGroup`, which re-key it. A streamed scope admits the group sources of its slots' members (`admittedSceneMeshes`).
- The editor viewport's Scene turns instancing on too (`createEngine` with `editor`), and its primitives (`createActorMesh`, `createComponentMesh`) draw through the pool the same way, as `editorActor:<id>:drawn` or `editorActor:<id>|<component>:drawn`. What the editor writes to an actor's mesh reaches the leaf: `actorIdFromMeshName` names the actor from a leaf (taps, marquee and the debug overlay); `syncPooledLeafFlags` copies visibility and picking (`applyActorTransform`); construction, override and saved per-actor parameter Materials go through `setActorMeshMaterial`, and a mesh of its own inherits the visual's construction Material so clearing an override restores what draws; nav bakes read a leaf's source triangles through its world matrix; an editor deformer ejects its targets first; a probe's static predicate answers for a leaf as its root (`isEditorStaticMesh`). The viewport shading modes mutate the shared Material, which the group source draws with.
- A model's rigid meshes pool too ([`pooled-model-leaves.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/pooled-model-leaves.ts), from `beginSlotModelAnimLoad` once its slot Materials are assigned and before `prepareInstanceMaterials` compiles them): a model instance with no retargeted clip replaces each visible mesh with no skeleton, morphs or other `instancingIneligibility`, whose own clips animate only its transform, with a member in place (same parent, children, name, transform, flags, metadata and glTF primitive record, so `modelMaterialIndexOf` still names its slot). The instance's clip channels that moved the mesh move the member (`handOverNode`, [`node-handover.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/node-handover.ts)), so a node-animated rig such as the mannequin draws one batch per part; a retargeted clip binds through a skeleton linked to the instance's nodes, so such an instance keeps its meshes. `eject` hands a member's children and every animation targeting it, a playing one's included, to the mesh that replaces it. Its group's geometry is the container mesh's (`model:<geometry id>`): sources clone that mesh, and each group holds the cached GLB (`retain`, a container lease). Material writes reach such a leaf through `setDrawnMaterial` (`applyModelMaterialSlots`, `applyMaterialToActorMeshes`, the editor's construction and override writes, and a library rebind, which skips group sources): it moves to the group drawn with the Material, or is replaced in place by a mesh of its own for one no group draws (`eject` keeps the primitive record). When the model's levels arrive (`attachModelLods`), a pooled leaf's group source takes them, once, and so does every later source built from that container mesh (`attachTemplateLods`, from the geometry's `build`), so a member moving to another group keeps them; the Scene selector draws each member at the level of its own bounds, with its own hysteresis, by setting the member's `_currentLOD`, which Babylon registers it on (`InstancedMesh._activate`).
- Per-instance Material parameters ([`instance-parameters.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/instance-parameters.ts)): the compiler realizes a parameter marked Per Instance as an `InputBlock` attribute named `instanceParameterFloat_<letters>` or `instanceParameterColor_<letters>` (letters only: Babylon strips digits; by kind, because a mesh registers an attribute's width once and may draw several Materials), never giving it a value (the setter would make it a uniform), and records the Material's values with it (`attachInstanceParameters`; a Color in linear space, as a Color uniform converts it). The Material's `isReadyForSubMesh` first registers each attribute on the drawing mesh (an instance's source) with that value (`prepareInstanceParameters`; instances without one take it too), before the Effect is chosen: a mesh with user instanced buffers always binds through Babylon's instanced path, so a plain mesh reads its own value. `setParameter` on such a parameter (`setInstanceParameterValue`) rewrites the meshes it prepared, and their instances, that still hold its previous value, and leaves those carrying their own; no uniform, define or Effect changes. Surface variants compile their own blocks and prepare the same attributes. A mesh's own values (`setMeshInstanceParameters`, by parameter name) outlast a change of Material; a drawing mesh is rewritten the first time it is prepared for another Material (`preparedFor`), and a pool member that moves to another source is rewritten for the Material that source was prepared for (`refreshInstanceParameters`, from `rekey`), since attribute names repeat across Materials.
- Per-actor values of per-instance parameters only (`everyInstanceParameter`) keep the shared Material: Play's `resolveAssignedMaterial` returns it with the values, and `applyMaterialToActorMeshes` gives them to what draws (a pooled leaf, a mesh of its own, the mesh itself); a runtime edit prepares the shared Material and adopts no instance; the editor's saved instance (`applyAuthoredMaterialInstance`) writes them to the visual's drawn meshes and clears them with the instance. Any other per-actor value keeps a Material of its own, which carries per-instance values as its own Material values.
- Readiness of pooled visuals: the probe resets the frozen-readiness caches of, and reads hot swaps from, an instance's source's submeshes (`isMeshFrameReady`); a new instance is hidden until its actor's round reveals it with the actor's other meshes, though its batch draws (`hasDrawnItself`), while one that drew waits for a swap shown. Caster depth variants are asked of an instance's own submeshes (`ShadowCasterVariants`), whose defines read the instance's scaling. A library NodeMaterial whose first build is pending answers the vertex stage from its plan (`plannedStockVertexStage`, `hasStockOrPlannedVertexStage`), so assigning it does not eject. Play's load stages, for each spawnable surface Material, a member of the group a spawned box joins (`joinPooledPrimitiveGroup`, `spawnable-warm.ts`), so the instanced variants a spawn draws are the ones compiled and drawn; a plain staged box's variant would be one no spawn draws, which the load's preparation would wait on. `prewarmMeshMaterials` compiles an instance's source.
- Instance uploads ([`instance-uploads.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/instance-uploads.ts), the patch hook `Scene._instanceUploads`, installed by every `ForwardSceneFrameGraph`): on WebGL every pass draws a source's instances from one buffer, which Babylon refilled and uploaded at each draw, so the objects pass rewrote the geometry pass's bytes into a buffer the frame had already drawn from. A draw in the same Engine frame whose rows the buffer holds neither fills nor uploads them: same buffer, `renderSelf`, instances in the same order, each world matrix's `updateFlag` unchanged (the source's too) and the same floating-origin offset (Large World's, per camera). Previous-world rows, `Mesh.INSTANCEDMESH_SORT_TRANSPARENT`, manual and frozen batches always upload, and an upload the record did not fill drops it. Per-instance attributes have no producer to ask: the floats a draw wrote are compared bit for bit with what the buffer received this frame.
- `instancingIneligibility` says why a mesh draws unpooled in v1: deforming bounds, thin instances, levels of detail, instances of its own, draw hooks, partitioned shadow ranges, a sorting order, or a Material that blends, draws other than triangles or is not the stock vertex stage (World Position Offset: bounds and the CPU pick must stay exact). `diagnostics()` reports groups, retained groups, pooled members and re-keys; `RenderDiagnostics.instancePool` carries it for a view's Scene (`existingSceneInstancePool`, which never creates a pool), and the Feature Test Check's Profile prints it.

## Draw batching

- Scene Layer Scenes batch their draws ([`overlay-draw-batcher.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/overlay-draw-batcher.ts), `setSceneDrawBatching`, which the compositor turns on at `create`): HUD content is many small draws of the unlit Material and of authored Scene Layer Materials that the instance pool cannot take (blended, sorted, per-mesh bindings). The objects pass hands each sorted list to the Scene's batcher through the leaf [`draw-batching.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/draw-batching.ts): opaque and alpha-tested lists through `RenderingGroup._RenderSorted`, blended ones from `TransparentDepthPrePass`. The batcher draws a run as one draw on a batch mesh, with output identical to drawing its members one by one:
  - **Order.** The list keeps Babylon's sorted order, except that a draw joins the first batch of the same draw at or after the last batch holding a draw it overlaps (projected bounds widened by two pixels; meeting edges overlap). Draws that cover no common pixel blend and depth-test the same in either order. A draw whose bounds may not hold what it draws (skinning, morphs, a lattice, instances, `doNotSyncBoundingInfo`, a vertex stage that moves vertices) overlaps everything; a draw that joins no batch but whose Material keeps its vertices on its mesh (`isBoundedMaterial`) is ordered around by where it is, so it splits only the batches it overlaps.
  - **Values.** Members draw one Material and the same Effect, and the batch mesh, set up as its first member, an Effect whose defines are theirs and `HAPTIC_BATCH` (checked after the batch mesh's readiness); they bind the same values: the texture they sample (the unlit binding's own, or the image page below) and the binding's alpha rules (by value), visibility, side orientation and layout clip (one scissor per batch, `enableOverlayClipScissor`). A member's own colour with its alpha, its overlay tint (alpha times opacity) and its texel grid (below) are not among them: the batch carries them with each vertex (`batchColor`, `batchTint`, `batchTexel`), and the unlit graph reads them there in place of the bound values (`drawBatchValue`: gated attributes handed to the fragment stage as flat varyings, so a pixel reads exactly the member's value), so texts of different colours on one atlas page and tinted visuals share a draw.
  - **Vertices.** A batch holds its members' own vertices and, per vertex, the first three rows of its member's world matrix as the member's draw uploads them: `OffsetWorldToRef` (translation minus the floating origin's eye), then 32 bits (`DRAW_BATCH_WORLD_KINDS`, one interleaved buffer; three attributes, since an affine matrix's fourth row is (0, 0, 0, 1)). The unlit Material's vertex stage builds the world matrix from those for a batch's mesh ([`draw-batch-world-block.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/draw-batch-world-block.ts): `DrawBatchWorldBlock` after the `InstancesBlock`, `HAPTIC_BATCH` defined for a mesh `markDrawBatchMesh` marked) and transforms with the same expression on the same values as the member's own draw, so a turned member, or one whose scale rounds in 32 bits, batches too. A mirrored member (negative determinant), whose faces Babylon draws the other way round, and one whose matrix is not affine draw on their own. The attributes are declared under the define alone: only a batch's mesh has buffers for them.
  - **Retained lists.** A Scene Layer's objects pass draws from its retained draw list ([scene-layers.md](scene-layers.md#runtime-and-render)): the lists it puts in place are marked presorted (`setPresortedPass`) and handed over without sorting; every sorted list a batching Scene hands over is reported to `observeSortedPasses` for the list's checks, and `SceneDrawBatcher.outputs` names the meshes the last frame drew (Babylon's mesh loop and cull output while the list draws).
  - **Readiness.** A member joins only when its own draw would draw (`Mesh.render`'s gate: `_getInstancesRenderList`, then `isReadyForSubMesh`); a batch whose mesh is not ready with the batch variant of its members' program draws them one by one.
- Authored Materials batch when the compiler marked them (`markDrawBatchMaterial`, [`material-compiler.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/material-compiler.ts)): a Scene Layer Material (compiled with `overlay`) with the stock vertex stage (`markBoundedMaterial`: a surface or text with no custom blocks and an identity World Position Offset, so a mesh drawing it stays inside its bounds), or a bounded User Interface (`ui`) Material (no Custom GLSL), marked as an overlay Surface Material is (`markDrawBatchMaterial(material, false)`) whether or not it compiles with `overlay`; its Element Size comes from screen derivatives of each member's own UV, so a batch needs no per-mesh value for it. In either case every block that binds values is listed in `bindsAsABatchDoes`: blocks that bind the Scene's or the Material's own values (`ScreenSizeBlock` binds only the Engine's render size, the same for every member; `LayerMultiplyBlock` binds the Scene's layer kind), the overlay style (`OverlayStyleBlock` reads the batch's per-vertex tint), the lighting (its one per-mesh term, Receive Shadows, is a value a batch's members must share), and blocks whose per-mesh values belong to meshes that never batch. A block that binds and is not listed (a Mirror's reflection, water) keeps its Material out, so a new per-mesh block is safe until it is listed. The surface plumbing routes the world matrix through `DrawBatchWorldBlock`, and a batch of an authored Material carries its members' normals, tangents and second UVs beside their UVs (members of one batch have the same kinds).
- A text's Material (the Text domain, marked whether it compiles for a Scene Layer or not: `isDrawBatchTextMaterial`) draws each glyph as a mesh that binds values of its own (`TextMaterialBlock`, `textMaterialGlyphBinding`). A batch of glyphs carries them with each vertex (`DRAW_BATCH_TEXT_KINDS`: the glyph's rectangle on the atlas, its rectangle in the text's Material space, and its span colour with its visibility, which the block reads through `drawBatchValue` in place of the bound values), and binds what its glyphs share once, from its first member (`bindTextMaterialGlyph`): the atlas, the glyph mode, and the outline's width and colour. So the glyphs of one text, and of texts that draw one Text Material on one atlas with one outline, share a draw whatever their span colours and fades. A glyph batch binds at least nine vertex buffers (positions, UVs, the three world rows, the overlay tint and the three glyph values).
- Never batched: authored Materials the compiler did not mark, mirrored poses, draw hooks other than a layout clip, instances, thin instances, skinning, morphs, lattices, vertex colours, per-instance parameter buffers, depth pre-passes, occlusion queries, outlines, a pre-pass renderer. Bitmap Text2D glyphs of one Scene sit on shared atlas pages (below), so texts on one page bind the same values and share a draw wherever draw order and clips allow; MSDF texts share one by Font and style.
- Batch meshes are made under `_blockEntityCollection`, so they never join `scene.meshes` (never culled, picked, probed or counted). A batch takes the batch mesh it drew from last unless another draw took it this Engine frame (each draws once per Engine frame), else one that holds no live batch's fill, else any, else a new one. A batch rewrites its vertex, world matrix, style (colour and tint), UV and index buffers in place (`updateDirectly`, `updateIndices` on GPU memory only, which keep vertex array objects), each only when its own contents changed: a member that moves uploads the world matrices alone, a tint or fade the styles alone; a member's 32-bit vertices and world rows are cached by the same keys.
- **Retention** (`OverlayDrawBatcher.draw`): each pass keeps its members' readings (classification, binding, cell, clip, vertices, world rows, bounds), its readiness answers, its batches and the batch meshes they filled, by its place among the Engine frame's passes (a record the last frame drew no pass with lets its members go). What changed is heard, not compared: the batcher reads the Scene's render change journal (all kinds) and re-reads only the members it names; the rest cost nothing.
  - **Every pass**: the sorted list is compared (members and order, and whether it blends; another list keeps the readings of the members it still holds, reads its new ones and groups again); the pass-wide inputs are compared (the Scene's transform matrix and the margins from the camera viewport at the render size: every member bounded anew and the pass grouped again; the floating-origin eye, the image page's `revision`, `latticeMarksRevision`: every member read again; `sceneReadinessRevision`: every readiness answer asked again); members the journal cannot hear (`journalFollows` false) are read again; and each distinct Material and texture the kept members read is polled once an Engine frame (a Material's depth pre-pass, culling pass and batch marks; a texture's sampling, wrap, alpha, internal texture, readiness and matrix), a change re-reading its users. Per member per pass only the list compare, `sameProgram`'s Effect compare and the `_isActive` resets of members drawn in a batch remain; a member found not ready is asked again every pass.
  - **A re-read member** keeps its readiness answer when only a world matrix or an overlay style named it (`KEEPS_READINESS`: Babylon's gate reads no pose, a mirrored pose draws on its own through its own gate, and a style that crosses full opacity marks the Scene's readiness); any other kind, a poll or a pass-wide re-read asks it again. A HUD whose texts wave or pulse every frame would otherwise run a full `isReadyForSubMesh` for each such glyph each frame. It is classified again, its draw stamp (`stampDraw`: whether it may batch and what `sameDraw` and `drawKey` compare, at full precision) and projected bounds compared with its last reading's: a difference groups the pass again (`regroup`: bounds kept for every other member; a new batch of exactly a kept batch's members, in order, whose slot still holds their fill takes that slot over). Otherwise only its batch refills (its serial cleared), uploading only the buffers whose contents changed: a tint uploads the styles alone.
  - **Haptic's write points** note what no Babylon write reaches (`noteRenderChange`): `setUnlitBinding` (the mesh and the child meshes falling back to its binding), `setMaterialUnlitBinding` and `updateUnlitBinding` (every mesh drawing the binding: its holders, their falling-back children, and the meshes of Materials holding it; `UnlitBinding` and `TextMaterialBinding` are read-only types, so in-place writes go through these), `bindTextMaterialGlyph`, `applyOverlayVisualStyle` (its component's meshes), the layout clips' refresh (each mesh gaining or losing its scissor hooks, or whose clip's rectangle changed), and `setMeshShadowFlags` (Receive Shadows). Babylon writes with no hook and no Haptic writer on layer meshes (`applyFog`, `sideOrientation`, skeletons, morphs, instances, render observers, outlines, `ignoreCameraMaxZ`, fill mode, `doNotSyncBoundingInfo`, `Geometry.removeVerticesData`) reach the batcher only through the periodic full check: a writer that starts writing one on a layer mesh must note it.
  - **A fill is skipped** while the batch's slot still holds its fill (the slot's owner and fill serial are the batch's; a fill clears the owner until it is whole). A batch mesh draws once per Engine frame: a batch takes the one it drew from last unless another draw took it this frame, else one that holds no kept batch's fill, else a new one.
  - **Full path**: a new list for the record, another blend mode, a journal the reader fell behind on, or a heal reads every member and groups the pass from scratch.
  - **Checks** (`check`): every `FULL_CHECK_INTERVAL`th (32nd) retained pass, and every retained pass while verifying (`verifyRetainedOverlayBatches`, `__hapticPlayTest.verifyRetainedOverlayBatches`, `HAPTIC_PERF_VERIFY_BATCHES`), works the full path out before the pass draws, on the check's own entries and arrays: classification and readings (binding, cell, glyph binding, clip, vertex data, positions, world rows, draw values), bounds, batches in order, each cached "ready" answer (asked again; a changed answer or Effect counts), and, for each batch about to draw without refilling, its slot's counts and arrays. A difference takes the full path at once and counts in `heals` (in `mismatches` while verifying) with the kind of input missed (`missedKinds`) and the first differences described. `sceneDrawBatchWork` (each `layerWork` entry's `batching`) also counts members skipped outright, re-read, and on the full path, and passes retained, grouped again, on the full path and checked.
- Glyph atlas ([`text2d-glyph-atlas.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/text2d-glyph-atlas.ts), `sceneGlyphAtlas`): every bitmap glyph a Scene's 2D Texts draw sits on that Scene's pages, one set for pixel fonts (nearest sampling) and one for the rest (bilinear), disposed with the Scene. A page is a 512 × 512 RGBA texture (a larger cell gets a page of its own size) that keeps its pixels on the CPU; cells sit on shelves a texel apart and a texel from the edges, so a glyph samples the same texels wherever its cell is. A text holds each of its cells (`acquire`, by `glyphCellIdentity`: the glyph, how it is drawn and the fonts loaded) until it is redrawn or disposed; a cell no text holds stays findable and gives its room up only when a page has none left, a reused slot is cleared first, and a page left empty goes unless it is the last, which clears what its shelves covered and starts them again from its origin. Pages never resize, so a held glyph's UVs stay valid. A page uploads only the full-width band of rows written since its last upload (`updateTextureData` with a view of its pixels, placed for the flipped page as a whole-page upload would; a NullEngine updates the whole texture). Written pages upload once a frame, first of the Scene's `onBeforeRenderObservable` work (a render target capture draws the Scene from that observable), however many texts were built in it; a Play layer's are uploaded before the world draws (`uploadSceneGlyphPages`, from `SceneLayerCompositor.beforeWorld`) and the observer finds them uploaded unless a text was built since, and a text whose glyphs are all there uploads nothing. The allocation limits stay per text, as the atlas the text alone would need (`planBitmapGlyphAtlas`); `text2dBitmapBytes` is the texels its own glyphs hold.
- Texel grid (`unlitTexelGrid`, [`unlit-material-blocks.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/unlit-material-blocks.ts)): for a texture sampled nearest (`TEXTURE_NEAREST_NEAREST`) that repeats or clamps, the unlit graph finds the texel a UV falls in itself, `floor(uv · size)`, and samples that texel's centre, `(origin + texel + 0.5) / sampled`, in place of leaving a UV on the line between two texels to the sampler's rounding. A texel outside the image is taken as the sampler takes it: wrapped where the texture repeats, else clamped. The grid is a vec4 of the mesh's own: bound (`unlitTexel`) or, in a batch, flat with each vertex (`batchTexel`, the third attribute of the style buffer, through `drawBatchValue`). Its first two values say how each axis leaves the image (0: no grid, the UV is sampled as it is, as for a filtered texture or one that mirrors; 1 clamps; -1 repeats); its last two are negative (`UNLIT_TEXEL_WHOLE`) where the image is the whole of the sampled texture, and otherwise the origin of the image's cell on it, the first two then scaled by the cell's size. `sampled`, and the size of a whole image, are the GPU texture's own (`textureSize`), never the size a texture object records: Babylon leaves a mip-mapped KTX2 it decoded to plain colour at its last level's. A draw of an image's own texture and a batch that samples the image from the image page then read the same texel, by the same arithmetic on the same interpolated UV, also where the image is drawn at a size that puts pixel centres on texel lines. With the grid an unlit batch binds eight vertex buffers: positions, UVs, the three world rows, colour, tint and grid.
- Image page ([`layer-image-atlas.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/layer-image-atlas.ts), `LayerImageAtlas`, one per batcher and disposed with it): members whose small images sit on the Scene's shared page sample the page in place of their own textures, so visuals that differ in image alone share a draw. Only a batch reads the page: a visual drawn on its own samples its own texture.
  - **Per draw** (`cellOf`): the texture's matrix must leave UVs as they are (`keepsUvs`; a `Texture`'s unmoved matrix is the identity only as 3 × 2), the texture must have a texel grid, and the page must hold the image, drawn (`cell`). Batches are found and compared by the texture they sample (`sampled`); a batch binds its first member's values with the page for the texture (`pageBinding`, the slot's own object), and each vertex carries its member's grid scaled by its cell's size, with the cell's origin. UVs stay the member's own, so tiled, clamped and flipped lookups resolve inside the cell as on the image's texture.
  - **What the page takes**: a ready 2D texture of 8-bit RGBA or RGB, neither compressed nor an sRGB buffer, each side at most 256 texels, with a single level of storage (a cell is copied by the size the texture records, which Babylon does not always keep at a mip-mapped texture's first level).
  - **The page** is one 1024 × 1024 RGBA8 render target (the device's largest texture when that is smaller), sampled nearest, on the managed ledger (`sceneColor`; no page when the ledger has no room, or on WebGL1, which cannot read texels by index), made with the first image. Two `BaseTexture` wrappers of it, with and without alpha, stay out of the Scene's lists: a draw binds its texture's `hasAlpha` with the texture, so images that differ in it do not share a draw.
  - **Cells** sit on shelves, edge to edge: a lookup reads a texel's centre, never a neighbour. A cell is copied on the GPU, texel by texel (`texelFetch`, one draw whose viewport is the cell, between `saveEngineDrawingState` and `restoreEngineDrawingState`), first of the Scene's `onBeforeRenderObservable` work (a Play layer's before the world draws, `flushSceneImagePages` from `SceneLayerCompositor.beforeWorld`; the observer then copies only what was asked for since), and is given out only once drawn: the frame that first shows an image draws it from its own texture. A cell goes when its texture is disposed or reloaded (another `InternalTexture`); its slot takes the next image that fits. When an image fits no freed slot but freed room exists, the page is laid out again, tallest first, and every cell drawn where it is now. A restored context draws every cell again once its texture has reloaded. An image the page has no room for keeps its own texture.
  - `setSceneImagePages(scene, false)` draws batches from their members' own textures, which the browser proof compares; `sceneImagePages(scene)` counts the images on the page and those drawn.
- Batch statistics (`countOverlayBatches(stats, draw)`, `RenderDiagnostics.layerBatches`): the batcher adds the passes drawn while `draw` runs to `stats`: the draws asked for (`visuals`), the draws made, the batches and the visuals they drew, and the visuals that drew on their own by reason (`alone`): `single` (its pass's only draw), `material` (neither the unlit Material nor a marked authored one), `transform` (mirrored, or not affine), `vertexColor`, `other` (anything else a batch cannot reproduce) and `unmatched` (batchable, but no other draw of its pass draws the same, or its batch could not draw as one then). `visuals` is `batched` plus every `alone` count. A `createEngine` view counts its frame's Scene Layers (its compositor's `render`) and reports the last frame it drew, as `drawPasses` does; a frame it holds or skips changes nothing, and a frame without a layer reports zeros. An opaque or alpha-tested list of one goes through the batcher too, so it is counted; it draws as Babylon draws it. While a layer phase counts (`countLayerPhase`, [`layer-frame-stats.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/layer-frame-stats.ts)) each `draw` adds its time to `LayerFrameStats.batchMs` ([scene-layers.md](scene-layers.md#runtime-and-render)).
- [`scene-layer-hud-fixture.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/scene-layer-hud-fixture.ts) builds a HUD like the Feature Test's `FT_HUD` for `overlay-draw-batcher.test.ts` (draw counts) and the browser proof (`e2e/scene-layer-batching.spec.ts`, exact images, with filtered icons and with nearest ones, which share the image page beside sprites that repeat, clamp and flip their images).
- **World sprite batching** ([`world-sprite-batcher.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/world-sprite-batcher.ts), [`sprite-batch-block.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/sprite-batch-block.ts), [`sprite-batch-handle.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/sprite-batch-handle.ts)): Play world Scenes batch (`syncWorldSpriteBatching` in `create-engine.ts`, after `animVisibility.flush()`; Editor viewports and Scene Layers do not). An eligible `SpriteComponent` visual (not in a Scene Layer, no Material override, Deformer or Outline, and `castShadows` explicitly false: a mesh casts unless told not to, and the shadow depth pass draws a batch's raw unit quad) keeps a geometry-free, unpickable root `Mesh` (`isVisible = false`) in `binding.meshes`, so transforms, children, particles, 2D lights/occluders and commands are unchanged, and draws as a thin instance of a unit quad. `SpriteBatchVertexBlock` (`HAPTIC_SPRITE_BATCH`) applies per-instance `spriteSizePivot` and `spriteUvRect`, `UnlitSurfaceBlock` reads `spriteTexel` (`LayerImageAtlas` cells for `<= 256×256` nearest textures) and `UnlitFinishBlock` reads `spriteFinish` after the cutoff. Invariant: the picture equals the same sprites drawn one by one (`e2e/world-sprite-batching.spec.ts`).
  - *Order.* With the default `LEQUAL` depth test a later cutout draw wins at equal depth, so order is the picture. Cutout sprite layers are grouped per (rendering group, `alphaIndex`) bucket and sorted by `compareDrawIdentity` (`compareAlphaTestDraws` past `alphaIndex`: sorting group, layer key, ordinal, name, unique id; an overlay layer sorts as `actor-N-blend`). A bucket's other meshes with geometry (tilemap chunks, text, ejected sprites, found by a scan when `scene.meshes` changes or `applyComponentSorting` changes a mesh's order, `sceneSortingRevision`) split it into runs of consecutive sprites drawing alike (lighting, winding, shadow reception, sampled texture); a batch draws one run in order and sorts as its first sprite (`metadata.sortingName` / `sortingUniqueId`, read by `compareMeshDrawIdentity`). A crossfading layer (weight in (0, 1)) blends in the transparent queue, sorted by distance: each draws alone with its quad's own bounds, the primary's mesh older than the overlay's (Scene order breaks ties, as the unbatched root precedes its overlay).
  - *Updates.* Each layer owns a slot in its batch. A transform, frame or weight change writes its rows and grows per-buffer dirty ranges; a flush uploads each dirty range once per buffer (one row through a scratch copy, a longer range through Babylon's ranged form), never the clean buffers. A spawn, despawn, hide, show, texture or crossfade change re-places that sprite and re-partitions its bucket alone, writing only slots whose sprite changed. Bounds hold every instance (grown by each written row, recomputed on re-partition), so frustum and occlusion culling trust them (`markBoundsHoldInstances`); off-screen instances of a drawn batch still run the 4-vertex stage. Large World Rendering anchors batches near the eye and re-bases them (every row rewritten) only after the eye moves `REBASE_DISTANCE` on an axis; rows hold the placement minus the anchor in 64 bits, then 32. Unchanged frames upload and allocate nothing.
  - *Ejection.* `ejectBatchedSprite` / `ejectPlaySprite` populate the root in place (`populateSpriteQuadGeometry`, pickable) for Material overrides, Deformers, Outlines, sprites that start casting, Simulation Edit with the transform tools and `worlddebug spritebatching off`; the slot's own visual also gets its crossfade overlay then (showing the overlay layer it drew batched, hidden with its actor), as an unbatched sprite has one from construction. `RenderDiagnostics.worldSprites` reports sprites, batches, pool, atlas images and uploads.

## Water

- One `WaterDefinition` is shared by renderer, worker and editor (core `water.ts`). Physics, queries and buoyancy never read quality, the GPU FFT or GPU state: every look, Custom Material included, evaluates the CPU spectrum field of core [`water-spectrum.ts`](../../../packages/core/src/water-spectrum.ts) (JONSWAP wind sea plus swell, deterministic hashed Gaussians so every quality tier and the worker share one ocean; 64² and 32² grids at `WATER_SPECTRUM_PHYSICS_RATE`, frames blended, Catmull-Rom sampled). The GPU spectrum (`water-fft-spectrum.ts`) builds the same modes at its own size and drops bins the CPU grids cannot hold, so short render-only detail is the only difference.
- `WaterClock` (runtime driver) advances with the simulation step; the renderer pairs water time with the applied snapshot frame (`setSceneWaterTime` / `sampleSceneWaterFrame`). Editor and previews use real time. Play's clock starts at 0 and moves only on `waterTime` commands, which the main step publishes whenever the scene owns a water surface (`WaterWorld.hasSurfaces`), also from a 2D world, whose step evaluates no water.
- Rendering gates on the device-effective `sceneWaterQualityDeviceClamp(scene)`, never requested values; `sceneWaterQualityRevision` change re-plans each FrameGraph ([`render-settings.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/render-settings.ts)). `clampWaterQualityToDevice` is the single device gate.
- Water Shading Detail is the water nodes' `SLATE_WATER_DETAIL` define (0 Low to 3 Ultra; `WATER_DETAIL_DEFINE` in `water-shader-defines.ts`), set by every water node block from the device-clamped setting, so a Material with water nodes compiles one variant per level and an unchanged setting compiles nothing; a change compiles, as other water quality defines do (frozen Materials are marked dirty on the quality revision, `syncQuality`). It drops only costly per-pixel terms (texture taps and noise): Medium the four-tap shoreline, edge foam's two finer octaves and object reflections below a Fresnel of `SSR_FRESNEL_FADE` (Water Reflection skips the march there, as it does wherever its roughness, horizon, grazing-ripple and fog fades leave nothing), Low also contact foam; High and Ultra draw every term. Cheap terms (haze, aerial perspective, slope-variance roughness, transmittance, the silhouette fallback below) stay at every level. It never overrides Reflections, Refraction, Reflection Steps or FFT Cascades.
- Water shader sources: defines and tuning constants in `water-shader-defines.ts`, the source chunks the water nodes compile in `water-shader-chunks.ts`, the Water Waves node's per-language assembly and the Water Cut block's blend cull in `water-shader-source.ts`; per-Scene placeholders, lighting and binding data in `water-scene-state.ts`; GPU wave uniforms and `WaterVertexWaves` in `water-vertex-waves.ts`. [`water-material.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/water-material.ts) re-exports their public names beside `contactRange` and the removal-volume selection (`selectWaterRemovals`).
- Water receives every shadow through the light block's 3 × 3 kernel (four taps) whatever the view's filter quality, without Contact Hardening: `HapticLightingBlock` sets `HAPTIC_SHADOW_NARROW` on water surface meshes, which compiles the wider kernels and the PCSS search out of their shadow filter. On moving water a wider penumbra barely shows (frames under a near sun differ only at a near shadow's edge pixels), and at Medium's sixteen taps receiving shadows cost water about 3 ms of a sustained 42 ms iPad frame.
- Water writes a zero normal and zero F0 into the scene effects' geometry pass (`WATER_EFFECTS_GEOMETRY_DEFINE`, set by the Water Cut block only in pass ids [`spatial-geometry-passes.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/spatial-geometry-passes.ts) registers for `SpatialEffectsGraph`; the authored post-process geometry pass keeps its normal): the AO march treats a zero normal as no surface and SSR skips zero F0. Its per-pixel FFT normals do not match the displaced mesh in view depth, so AO would darken it in view-dependent blotches and bands behind far crests.
- Refraction/screen-space reflections read a per-view scene copy ([`water-scene-copy.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/water-scene-copy.ts)) registered by render pass id before the first readiness probe; copy-sampling water draws only in `Forward transparent`. Each copy texel holds the nearest depth its taps find (a one-texel occluder keeps it; open sky is `WATER_SCENE_COPY_SKY_DEPTH`) and a colour (its taps averaged at integer ratios, one filtered tap otherwise). Water shows nothing of open sky (Water Depth Fade's transmittance is 0 there), so open sky's colour never reaches a surface's: a surface texel whose colour would blend open sky in takes its surface taps' alone, Water Scene Color's filtered read falls back to the texel's own colour where the filter blends open sky in, and a bent lookup that lands on open sky where the unbent point has a surface keeps the unbent point. Water Depth Fade also lets nothing through one copy texel in from open sky: the multisampled resolve blends a silhouette's colour with the background while its depth is one sample's, which the copy cannot tell apart. Otherwise a submerged edge (an underwater landscape's border) showed the background as dark dashes along the waves. Beside the silhouette of something in front of the water, the copy texel holds that object (a downsampled texel's nearest depth, a multisampled pixel's one sample), so the water there would read nothing behind it (Water Scene Color's Available 0, Water Depth Fade the body's own depth: an outline one copy texel wide around every object in or over water, and stripes on far water at grazing angles, where a texel's nearest depth comes from the row below). Both nodes take the farthest of the four neighbouring copy texels that lies behind the water instead (`swCopyBehind`; the farthest, since an object's own edge can lie just past the water). The four depths are loaded once per fragment by whichever node asks first and kept in private shader globals (`swCopyNear`); Water Depth Fade loads them on every fragment anyway (its open-sky test), so the fallback adds no texture loads and runs at every Water Shading Detail and copy scale (a full-resolution copy still has multisampled silhouettes). Which variant built-in water draws depends on the pass (the water nodes' copy features compile only into the copy's pass, the depth pre-pass has its own), so the shader warm-up and the pre-graph strict probe skip it (`isBuiltInWaterMesh`) and only the graph's own probe compiles it, under the real pass id: a probe under any other pass compiles a variant nothing draws, and every one is a slow link. Planar mirror ([`water-planar-reflection.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/water-planar-reflection.ts)) is drawn capture-style, entity-free, for one flat body per view that mirrors objects (`waterMeshReflectsObjects`: a Material with a Water Reflection node, Object Reflections on); the node looks it up while it draws (the lookup is the demand, `planarLookupSource`) under `SLATE_WATER_NODE_PLANAR`, which follows `WaterNodeSurface.flat`; its mirror camera, oblique projection, target and pass are [`planar-mirror.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/planar-mirror.ts), shared with Mirror components, its sun shadow too: none, or its own cascades under Reflection Shadows (per-view sun maps), whose due maps make a static view dirty and go with its idle storage (`WaterPlanarReflectionDiagnostics.sunShadowViews`). It draws every render while its view moves; a static view reuses its last draw until something it reflects may have changed ([`reflected-content.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/reflected-content.ts), followed every render: the culled meshes and their placement, visibility, geometry revision, sub-meshes and materials, a compiled Material's parameter revision (`materialParameterRevision`), an unlit Material's per-mesh binding (its texture and placement, colour, alpha and outline, read every render because owners edit it in place) and each mesh's visual style, the Scene's lights, environment, fog, image processing and render settings, and shadow map passes that change a shadow it shows (below)), and a redraw follows a change at most every other render. A draw that skipped a mesh not yet ready stays due. Content that can change without any of these (skinning, morph targets, baked or unnotified vertex data (sprite quads and tilemap chunks write theirs only through notified updates, `markOwnerNotifiedGeometry`), render hooks, an active lattice cage on the mesh (the Lattice Deformer block every graph Material carries makes nothing volatile on its own), time inputs, Mirror, text or capture images, textures updated in place, materials other than compiled graphs, the unlit Material and stock Standard or PBR) is volatile and keeps the every-other-render cadence while it is reflected. A transform set between renders is current only once the Scene's render id moves on, so the reflection draws it one render after the view does. Shadow map passes: every real pass of a map in the Scene's atlas (one that clears; probes do not) is recorded in the Scene's shadow change journal ([`shadow-change-journal.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shadow-change-journal.ts), a ring of passes) with each tile's drawn view-projection, its filter reach in NDC (the PCF kernel, or Contact Hardening's search and penumbra) and where it changed: world boxes of the casters `ShadowMapRefresh.apply` saw change, enter, leave or change on their own (where they were and are; deforming ones at twice their bounding radius, as `casterWithinShadowRange`), or everywhere for a changed view, fit, light or setting, a map continuous for another reason, a caster with unbounded geometry, a pass (or the one after a pass) that skipped a caster not ready, and a pass no refresh described (a capture view's own maps). A reflection redraws when, in some tile, a shown receiver's rectangle (Receive Shadows on) widened by the reach overlaps a changed box's projection; depth is not compared, and a box partly behind a perspective tile's eye covers the whole tile. FFT ([`water-fft.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/water-fft.ts)) is fragment-shader passes, demand-driven (a Material with a Water Waves node): the whole surface (three cascades, displacement, derivatives with per-layer mipmaps, and a ping-pong turbulence foam target accumulated from the Jacobian, each cascade recovering over its spectrum `foamPeriods` entry). Crest foam is the cascades' turbulence below calm weighted by `WATER_FOAM_WEIGHTS` (long waves first) past `waterFoamThreshold` (core: Crest Foam's share of the sum's Gaussian model, calibrated on GPU readback), bound per asset by `waterFoamThresholdOf` to the Water Waves node's foam uniform `.z`. Devices without float render targets draw water flat (`WaterQualityDeviceClamp.spectrum`). All reserve in the ledger's `water` category. A Scene's simulations (all of the Water quality's size) share one pair of RGBA32F work atlases (`WaterFftWork`, its own lease, released with the last simulation): a dispatch rewrites the first in full before reading either, and dispatches never nest.
- [`water-mesh.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/water-mesh.ts) creates water surfaces and runs their per-frame update (placement, culling bounds, CPU waves, copy intent, the GPU or CPU path the Material's graph chooses, picking). Beside it: the per-surface model in `water-mesh-surface.ts`, the scene water clock in `water-scene-clock.ts`, the rest-grid layout and cell budgets in `water-mesh-grid.ts`, per-vertex rest data and its uploads in `water-mesh-rest-data.ts`, the scene blend registry, blend kernel, Global blend rows and normals' fit in `water-mesh-blend-data.ts`, and the blend index sync and budgeted blend refresh in `water-mesh-blend.ts`. `water-mesh.ts` re-exports their public names, and code outside these modules imports only it.
- `settleSceneWater` ([`water-mesh.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/water-mesh.ts)) runs the water update as an early run (it advances neither the clock nor the drawn flags the frame's own run consumes), so blend, placement (`flat`) and the sky reflection view are final before a surface's variants are first probed. The before-render update fires after the strict probes; left to it, those values change the defines of variants already compiled, and every one compiles again. Copy intent is counted when the surface is created (`createWaterMesh`, once its Material's water nodes are known) and re-synced by the update only when enablement changes through an ancestor or `setEnabled`, when the mesh's Material changes, and by `updateWaterMeshDefinition` when an asset edit moves Object Reflections. Call sites: `prewarmSceneMaterials` (always, before the load's warm-up and graph probes), the render loop on frames that draw the world (only when a surface was created or its enablement changed since the last update: editor edits and drops, Play spawns; load frames leave it to the load's settle), and a published scene stream (its roots are held disabled until then, and the update skips disabled surfaces). The syncs are idempotent with the before-render update, and a Scene without water does nothing. `prepareSceneStream` skips built-in water in its warm-up like the load does.
- Bodies within Water Blend Distance blend via core `water-blend.ts`; only the owning body draws and answers queries. A body takes part out to the full distance (`waterBlendReach`) and fades out of the union shoreline over its outer half, where the union's smooth maximum (radius R) no longer pulls: a fade inside the rounding cuts corners into stepped tabs. A grid's outer vertices past its own reach still evaluate the kernel (`outside`), owning nothing, so shoreline and ownership interpolate toward true values. Colours fade over a wider coverage than shares; each surface keeps up to `WATER_BLEND_COLOR_PARTNERS` differently coloured neighbours (alike-coloured ones share a slot) with per-vertex shares (`slateWaterBlend.w`, `slateWaterBlendColors`), read by the Water Colors node (`WaterNodeSurface.partners`). Water hides what it does not own through the Water Cut block (below).
- The ownership seam is exact per pixel where a surface's participants allow ([`water-blend-ownership.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/water-blend-ownership.ts)): Global, Ocean, Lake and Puddle bodies, all level, at most `WATER_OWNERSHIP_PARTICIPANTS` (4) in all. The Water Cut block then evaluates the kernel's shares from per-participant uniforms (`WaterNodeSurface.ownership`, under `SLATE_WATER_OWNERSHIP`), keeping a pixel past the seam on each side because two meshes interpolate the same world point slightly apart. Whether a surface qualifies depends only on the blend index (bodies added, removed, moved or edited), never on the camera, so regrading and Global recentres only write uniforms and compile nothing. Such a finite body's grid extends past its bank by half the distance (`SurfaceBlend.reach`) even toward Global Water, since the seam is its own bank. Any other surface (a River among the participants, a tilted body, more participants) cuts on the per-vertex margin (`slateWaterBlend.z`), which `blendFit` refits on a coarse cell the seam crosses to the signed distance to the seam: along the margin gradient when a 4-neighbour lies across it, along the cell's diagonal when only the triangle-sharing diagonal neighbour does (a convex corner, where the margin around the vertex is flat), so a graded grid's large cells still meet the seam and a corner vertex is never mistaken for deep water by the blend vertex cull.
- Water Waves' fragment samples the first FFT cascade everywhere; each finer cascade fades out (`swnSr`) where one pixel spans its longest wave (its patch over `WATER_SPECTRUM_LOW_BIN`), from half of it, and skips its two spectrum taps once gone: they sit in a branch with explicit-gradient taps (`swSpecTapGrad`, the uv's own screen gradients, so filtering is unchanged and the taps are legal in non-uniform control flow). Its foam tap stays unconditional, since turbulence does not average to zero. `swnContactAt` skips its tap off the contact field, where the result is unused.
- The reflection march (`marchSource` in `water-shader-chunks.ts`) takes one sample per scene-copy texel its ray crosses on screen, at most `SLATE_WATER_SSR_STEPS` (Reflection Steps). Its depth reads are nearest-texel and the hit is solved where the bracketing samples' depth gaps interpolate to zero, so samples closer together than a texel find nothing more and the output is unchanged. With a 500 m march most rays still cross more texels than the steps: at Medium's 8 steps it saves a few percent of samples, at 32 about a tenth.
- Water displaces on the GPU from a static rest grid only through a **Water Waves** node, which the starter has (`evaluateWaterVertex` in core is the CPU reference); a graph without one draws CPU-displaced vertices. CPU picking hits the rest grid. The other water material nodes ([`water-node-blocks.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/water-node-blocks.ts): Water Colors, Foam, Depth Fade, Scene Color, Reflection) are fragment blocks that read the mesh's `WaterNodeSurface` (asset, and the terrain and contact fields every water mesh builds) and the pass's scene copy (`SLATE_WATER_NODE_COPY`, compiled only into the copy's pass; an alpha-blended Material whose graph reads the copy counts in the copy intent). Their per-pixel wave inputs come from the graph's Water Waves block. Their shared shader sources: the bank, depth and edge fades (`waterEdgeSource`, Water Depth Fade's Edge Fade) with the four-tap shoreline under the waves (`shorelineSource`; the nodes read whether the asset has waves from `slateWaterFieldStep.w`), the refraction bend and silhouette fallback (`silhouetteFallbackSource`, Water Scene Color, with Water Depth Fade's own through the shared copy neighbourhood (`emitCopyHelpers`), whose Color is pre-divided by the Material's own fog, the Scene Fog block's factor and linear fog colour (`sceneFogKeep`, `sceneFogColor`), so any linear share of it keeps the background's fog once in every channel), the planar lookup, and the aerial perspective and horizon haze (`aerialPerspectiveSource`, `horizonHazeSource`, Water Reflection), the horizon lift of the reflected ray (`horizonLiftSource`), and the slope-variance roughness (`slopeVarianceSource`, Water Reflection's Roughness output, which blurs its reflection and goes to the Material Output's Roughness in the starter); Water Reflection's Normal output is the half vector toward the lifted ray, which the starter mixes back to Water Waves' Normal under foam, lighting its foam along the facet. A vertex-and-fragment block gets no automatic varyings for its inputs, so `WaterWavesBlock` passes its rest point and normal itself. The water blocks live only in a Surface Material's water variant (`acquireWaterVariant` in [`material-compiler.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/material-compiler.ts), `CompileMaterialOptions.water`), which is what a water mesh draws: the base Material compiles none of them, so a mesh that is not water neither prepares nor binds them. The variant is the same graph, built once per Material generation, reference-counted by the water meshes holding it and disposed with its base; parameter edits reach it through the base, and its own outline and capture variants keep the cut. `createWaterMesh` puts it on the mesh (`holdWaterVariant`, also when the mesh is handed another Material), the Material library moves water meshes to the next generation's variant on a rebind (`rebindWaterMeshMaterial`), warming it for them first as it warms the base for its other meshes, and `waterVariantBase` names the base wherever a mesh's Material asset matters (`isBuiltInWaterMesh`). The compiler adds a **Water Cut** block (`WaterCutBlock`) to the water variant of every Surface Material, whatever its nodes. It removes (`cutSource`) the nearest `WATER_REMOVAL_SLOTS` removal volumes reaching the mesh (`selectWaterRemovals`), known terrain above the geometry's waterline (the Water Waves node's vertex height through a hidden Water Surface block on the GPU path, the displaced vertices' own otherwise), and a blending neighbour's water (bank distance and ownership margin). Its draw with colour writes off, the blended depth pre-pass under its own render pass id, sets `DEPTHPREPASS` (`WATER_DEPTH_PRE_PASS_DEFINE`): removed water is discarded there and the program returns before the look, the Water Waves node's per-pixel spectrum included. The colour pass discards at the very end (`_injectAtEnd`), after every derivative of the look was taken with the whole quad running. With Water Waves, a blending surface also culls vertices deep in water it never draws (`waterBlendCullSource`). In the scene effects' geometry pass it sets `WATER_EFFECTS_GEOMETRY_DEFINE` and, after the output's geometry writes, writes the encoded zero normal and zero reflectivity (`geometryOutputWrite`), so AO and SSR skip the water whatever its graph; the Material's geometry writes are opaque, so its surface fills the buffers at every angle. A translucent Surface Material's water variant also gets a hidden **Water Coverage** block (`WaterCoverageBlock`) between its colour and its fog: outside Scene Linear (`IMAGEPROCESSINGPOSTPROCESS`), it raises the coverage by the brightest channel (to at most 1) and lowers the colour as far, so light brighter than an 8-bit target shows over the background instead of clipping. The water starter is two-sided. Built-in water has one look; stylized water is a Custom Material. Built-in water draws with the water starter itself: `builtInWaterMaterial` ([`water-template.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/water-template.ts)) compiles it once per Scene and every water mesh without a Custom Material shares it (the callers of `createWaterMesh` pass it), and `isBuiltInWaterMesh` counts it as built-in water, which the view's graph warms under the passes that draw it. [`e2e/water-template.spec.ts`](../../../e2e/water-template.spec.ts) checks its look scenario by scenario: it draws over the lake and nowhere else, deep water is darker and bluer than shallows, the environment cube warms it, glints saturate toward the sun, and fog mixes the unfogged lake toward the fog colour by Babylon PBR's rule. The starter graph `createWaterMaterialDocument` (shader-graph) draws the built-in look from the nodes (`subsurfaceSource`, `fresnelSource`, `reflectionKeepSource`, `transmittanceSource` with `waterAbsorption`, the body light `sceneWaterBodyLight`, and the shore and contact foam `edgeFoamHelpers`, which the Water Foam block reads from the Water Waves block's hidden `edgeTurbulence` output; their foam noise flows by the current plus `waterFoamDrift`, the downwind drift along Wave Direction, bound in `slateWaterClock.yz`). Water meshes never cast shadows (`neverCastsShadows`).
- Grid geometry (except Global Water Volume and a spectrum Ocean) is world-anchored and never depends on the camera. A spectrum Ocean grades its grid around the camera (`oceanBudget`, capped by `WATER_OCEAN_CELL_BUDGET`) and rebuilds it when the camera moves `OCEAN_RECENTRE_SHARE` of the dense region, along an axis its budget cannot cover at whole cells only (`uniformFits`: such an axis is uniform wherever the camera is, so following the camera would rebuild an identical grid). Global Water's dense columns run its grid's whole length, so it draws thinned indices (`thinnedGridIndices` in `water-mesh-grid.ts`): each grid line keeps only columns about as far apart as the shorter row beside it, and the strip between two lines zips their kept vertices together, so no edge has a T-junction. Far rows hundreds of metres long draw no sub-pixel slivers along Z (they converge on the vanishing point and cost a triangle each), near rows draw the full grid, and the vertex buffer is unchanged. A blending Global grid keeps every column of the lines within a neighbour's reach (`blendRows`) and one line either side (`syncGlobalIndices`, which rebuilds the indices only when those lines change): its ownership margin, union shoreline and the GPU path's blend vertex cull are per-vertex and assume a triangle spans about one grid cell, so a thinned far triangle with a vertex inside a neighbour would lose open water with it. Rows stay full width, so the outer columns' cells remain wide and shallow beside the camera. The worker's `WaterWorld` keeps step and script-query evaluations in separate states.
- Object contacts ([`water-contact-field.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/water-contact-field.ts)): `WaterContactField` is a per-surface, CPU-built, height-aware signed-distance texture (four layers across the wave envelope). Waves never rebake it; objects, the surface and the envelope do. Contact Resolution caps its cells per side (Low 256, Medium 256, High and Ultra 1024).
- Contact texture: RGBA8, no mips, bilinear, clamped; one channel per `WATER_CONTACT_LAYER_OFFSETS` layer, signed distance over the contact range mapped linearly to [0, 1] with 0.5 at the waterline, negative inside closed sections. It is an Engine raw texture wrapped in a `BaseTexture` on the Engine, never in `scene.textures`: `ForwardSceneFrameGraph` counts that list (`membershipCounts`), and a changed count marks readiness dirty for every mesh, so each reallocation (objects spreading, Contact Resolution, `releaseTexture` / `restore`) would make every view re-probe the whole Scene strictly before it draws. The Engine keeps `data` as its contents for a lost context, as for any raw texture. Like the terrain field's texture it is not in the managed-allocation ledger.
- Rasteriser ([`water-contact-raster.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/water-contact-raster.ts)): the single implementation of sliced objects to texels (`rasterContactWindow`), Babylon-free, with the distance transform in [`distance-transform.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/distance-transform.ts) (re-exported by `water-field.ts`). A job holds only numbers and typed arrays (allocation, range, envelope, smoothing radius, the computed and written windows, the pieces within reach), and a window's texels depend only on its job: built over `contactRasterTarget` (the write window grown by twice the smoothing radius plus one, clamped to the allocation), any window size gives the bytes a build of the whole allocation does. A change of objects queues their bounds grown by the range plus that rounding reach; an allocation that carries its cells over also queues the bands along the old edges that now lie inside.
- Contact cost cap: `WATER_CONTACT_FRAME_BUDGET_MS` bounds main-thread contact work per scene and frame, across surfaces (`WaterContactField.drain`). A moved object is sliced again only once some point of it moved `MOVE_TOLERANCE_CELLS` (a quarter) of a cell since its last slice (`movedBeyond`: translation plus the frame's change times its bounding reach), so bobbing floats do not rebuild every `MOVE_MS`, and then queues only the window its movement can change; windows that overlap merge and keep their age, and a cell keeps its last result until its window is built. Slicing stops once the frame's budget is spent, after at least one stale placement per surface update; the rest stay stale (least recently sliced first, so constant movers cannot starve the others) and the next update continues without waiting for `MOVE_MS`. Slicing reuses grow-on-demand scratch, and on a level body transforms X/Z only for vertices of triangles that reach the envelope (same arithmetic as `Vector3.TransformCoordinatesFromFloatsToRef`, so slices are bit-identical). Order: seen before unseen, nearer before farther, any window waiting past `OVERDUE_MS` first (oldest first), and always at least one window per frame, so none starves. Main-thread windows past `MAX_WINDOW` (64) cells a side are cut into blocks, so a frame overshoots the cap by at most one block. Cadence falls with distance and view: a seen window waits 0 to `FAR_MAX_MS` before it runs (ramping from `FAR_START` to `FAR_END` metres from the nearest camera), an unseen one `OFFSCREEN_MS`, which coalesces bursts of moves; surfaces no camera sees skip their contact update altogether (`updateSceneWater`).
- Synchronous and progressive rebuilds: `update(now, true)` and `settle(now)` slice every stale placement (parked too) and build every cell before they return, dropping work at the worker; a forced update rebuilds everything, a settle finishes what is pending (all of it after a changed surface or envelope or a first allocation). Every other full rebuild (a first allocation, a changed envelope, range or Contact Resolution, a changed cell size, `invalidate` for a reshaped body) is progressive: the texture is allocated (or kept) and bound at once, cells keep what they show (carried over, or far for new cells), and every cell is queued as one window built within the budget. A moved surface re-cuts every cell at most every `SURFACE_MOVE_MS` the same way. Callers: `createWaterMesh` and `updateWaterMeshBody` (editor drags) and `updateWaterMeshDefinition` (asset edits) are progressive; `settleSceneWater(scene, true)` (a load, from `prewarmSceneMaterials`) settles every enabled surface's contacts, seen or not, and restores released ones settled; the render loop's and a scene stream's `settleSceneWater(scene)` and the before-render `updateSceneWater` are progressive, and so is `restore` after a released texture outside a load.
- Contact worker ([`water-contact-worker-client.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/water-contact-worker-client.ts), [`water-contact.worker.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/water-contact.worker.ts)): one lazily started module worker per realm (`sharedContactRaster`), used unless the Engine is a `NullEngine` or workers do not exist. `drain` keeps its priority order but sends the chosen window to the worker instead of building it: blocks up to `WORKER_WINDOW` (256) cells a side, at most `WORKER_FLIGHT` (2) windows per scene out at once, the job structured-cloned (placements keep their arrays) and the texels transferred back. Sending and applying (copy into `data`, partial upload) count against the budget; results apply at the next `drain`, in the order sent. Each field's `generation` drops results sent before an allocation change, a forced update or settle, `release` or `releaseTexture`; a carried allocation and `releaseTexture` queue those windows again (translated). Every write carries a sequence number, and a result older than a write that overlapped it is dropped and its window queued again, so an older result never lands over newer cells. `queued` counts windows at the worker until applied. A worker error, unreadable reply, failed job or `WATER_CONTACT_WORKER_TIMEOUT_MS` disables it for the session with one warning; pending windows return to their queues and the main thread builds them.
- No duplicate contact work between bodies: a window in which no body draws the surface is not built (`WaterFieldSurface.draws`: a blending neighbour owns the water there, as under an Ocean over Global Water Volume) and stays queued until `drawStamp` (the blend index revision) changes; a stale object whose last slice and current neighbourhood the surface draws nowhere is not even sliced (`parked`, asked again at each rebuild). It samples each window's centre and corners and treats any point the blend cannot place as drawn. Forced builds and settles still cover every cell; a progressive full rebuild queues every cell under the same rule.

## Babylon modules and registrations

- Production code imports Babylon by module, without an extension (`@babylonjs/core/Meshes/mesh`). A value import from the `@babylonjs/core` root is an ESLint error, in tests too (`BABYLON_ROOT_VALUE_IMPORT` in `eslint.config.js`; `import type` is erased and allowed): Babylon marks every `index.js` barrel and every shader side-effectful, so one root value import bundles and registers all of Babylon.
- Babylon 9 splits a module into `X.pure` (no side effects; exports `RegisterX()`) and `X` (re-exports it and registers). Import `X` when anything it registers is used (class-name lookups, scene components, prototype augmentations); `X.pure` only when nothing is.
- Side effects that no imported class module registers are imported once. Engine-wide ones live in [`babylon-registrations.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/babylon-registrations.ts) (views and `registerView`, WebGL2 `setAlphaEquation`, timer queries, multiple render targets, `createEffectForParticles`, …), which `create-engine.ts` loads before it constructs an Engine. A feature's own sit in the module that uses it: the WebGL2 GPU particle simulation (looked up by class name) in `particle-system-factory.ts`, `shadowGeneratorSceneComponent` in `scene-illumination.ts`, `edgesRenderer` in `nav-debug-overlay.ts`, `boundingBoxRenderer` in `play-console-viz.ts`, `ShaderStore` entries beside the effect that names them, and the glTF loader in `gltf-loader.ts`.
- No shipped code imports `@babylonjs/core/ShadersWGSL/*`, `Engines/WebGPU/*` or `Engines/webgpuEngine`. Babylon imports them lazily for an engine Haptic never creates; the editor and player builds leave those lazy imports out (`apps/editor/vite-unused-babylon-modules.ts`, [exporter.md](exporter.md#files-and-catalog)).
- A missing registration fails only at runtime: `TypeError: … is not a function`, a stub that does nothing (`_MissingSideEffect` placeholders on `Scene`, `AbstractMesh` and camera input managers), a class lookup that throws ("… class is not available"), or an unknown shader. Unit tests import by module too, with `babylon-registrations.ts` loaded by the `babylon` project's setup, so a registration that only another module supplies fails or stubs there as it would in production ([testing.md](../testing.md)); browser tests catch the rest, and `SetMissingSideEffectWarningsEnabled(true)` (`Misc/devTools`) logs each stub call.
- Not registered because Haptic does not use them: Babylon occlusion queries (culling uses Haptic's occlusion raster), the outline, depth, geometry-buffer, pre-pass and depth-peeling scene components (`getOutlineRenderer?.()` reads the stub as absent), sprites, collisions, the loading screen (only `AppendSceneAsync` shows it), multiview, video textures, texture selection and debug groups.

## Babylon pin and workarounds

`@babylonjs/core` is pinned at **9.29.0** with one pnpm patch (`pnpm-workspace.yaml` → [`patches/@babylonjs__core@9.29.0.patch`](../../../patches/@babylonjs__core@9.29.0.patch)). Review every item below when moving the pin; drop a patch hunk only when upstream fixed it.

| Patched file | Why |
| --- | --- |
| `Materials/Node/Blocks/Input/inputBlock.pure.js` | The GLSL branch define-gates the optional vertex streams (vertex colour, the instance world rows, bone indices and weights): a program declares each only under its define and reads a zeroed variable otherwise |
| `Materials/effect.pure.js`, `Misc/rgbdTextureTools.js` | Disposal guards; owned, cancellable RGBD decode with caller-state restore |
| `Materials/drawWrapper.js` | `dispose()` (the deferred form, at the next frame's end: a submesh's wrapper of a mesh that is disposed or rebuilt) waits for a program that still links before releasing its last reference, 15 s at most. Upstream deletes it, and the browser goes on asking the deleted program for its link status (see below). `dispose(true)` is unchanged: an owner that disposes at once keeps a linking Effect itself (`disposeKeepingLinkingEffects`) |
| `Materials/material.pure.js` | `_checkScenePerformancePriority` keeps one priority observer and one dispose observer per material. Upstream adds both at the end of every full readiness pass, and a Play scene (Intermediate priority) runs that pass on every probe (`isMeshFrameReady` sets `checkReadyOnEveryCall`), so the observers grew with each probed frame until the material was disposed. `forceCompilation`'s poll (every 16 ms until the mesh's variant is ready) stops for a NodeMaterial disposed meanwhile: upstream asks on, and a disposed NodeMaterial's readiness throws on the state its disposal cleared (a model thumbnail disposes its Material library as soon as its Scene is ready, with a warm-up's poll still running) |
| `Materials/shadowDepthWrapper.js` | Depth Effects keyed by source and defines instead of a random token per (subMesh, generator), so identical caster depth shaders share one Effect and program. Only safe with a wrapper that disposes each entry's reference before a define change and when its generator is disposed: upstream `_makeEffect` overwrites `mainDrawWrapper.effect` without disposing it and `ShadowGenerator.dispose` never visits the wrapper (`AuthoredShadowDepthWrapper` retires the entry in both cases) |
| `assetContainer.js` | `dispose()` removes its Scene dispose observer (otherwise every replaced generation leaks) |
| `Materials/Node/nodeMaterial.pure.js` | Pass the material's `shaderLanguage` to `createEffectForParticles` |
| `Engines/thinEngine.pure.*` | `_setTexture` and `_bindTexture` bind the WebGL2 sampler object an optional `_samplerObjectFor(name)` gives the uniform, or none, per texture unit, forgotten with the texture cache: the light block's plain read of the shadow atlas's depth beside its compare sampler. Without the hook nothing changes. `bindFramebuffer` attaches no colour layer of a layered target without a colour texture (the shadow atlas's pages) instead of throwing |
| `Engines/renderTargetWrapper.js` | `_cloneRenderTargetWrapper` (a context restore) keeps the layers of a layered target without a colour texture (the shadow atlas), which upstream reads from the colour texture and so makes flat |
| `Engines/thinEngine.pure.js` (`ExceptionList`) | No `uniformBuffer` exception for user agents matching `Mac OS.+Chrome`: every Chrome on a Mac and the macOS desktop app (Electron) match it, and without uniform buffers the light block's own buffer (`haptic-lights.ts`) is written before any Effect is bound, so no Scene opens. `e2e/macos-chrome-engine.spec.ts` opens a Scene under that user agent |
| `Engines/Extensions/engine.multiRender.pure.js`, `Engines/thinEngine.pure.js`, `Materials/materialHelper.geometryrendering.js` | A mesh draw of a pass with a geometry configuration binds its effect's attachments and restores the pass's colour attachments after it, which on a pass of multi target effects set the framebuffer's draw buffers twice per draw. The restore waits (`_deferAttachments`) and is bound before the next draw, clear, framebuffer change or resolve (`_flushAttachments`); `bindAttachments` skips a list the bound framebuffer already holds (`_drawBuffersHeld`, per framebuffer, keyed by `_currentFramebuffer`, dropped wherever Babylon sets draw buffers directly, at a single target's resolve, which binds a draw framebuffer without `_currentFramebuffer`, and at brute-force `wipeCaches`, a context restore) |
| `Lights/Shadows/cascadedShadowGenerator.pure.js` | Stabilised cascades snap each cascade's fit to its own tile size (`_cascadeMapSizes`, set by `AtlasCascadedShadowGenerator`) instead of the one `_mapSize`: the sun's far cascades draw into smaller tiles |
| `Materials/Node/Blocks/transformBlock.pure.js` | `NONUNIFORMSCALING` is always set for a NodeMaterial: a direction transforms by the inverse transpose whatever its mesh's scaling (a uniformly scaled matrix's turns it the same way, at another length), so one program draws a Material's meshes however they are scaled and a scale animation compiles nothing. What reads the length is given a unit normal (the lighting, and the Fresnel and Normal Map nodes); the World Normal node stays as transformed |
| `Meshes/mesh.pure.js` | `_registerInstanceForRenderId` starts a source's record of visible instances anew with each Engine frame (the mono storage, unless frozen), keeping its intermediate default. Upstream starts it anew only in `_preActivate`, which evaluating the Scene's active meshes calls and a frame graph never does, so the record kept the list of every render id that registered an instance. `visible-instance-lists.test.ts` draws a source through an object renderer over several frames. `_updateInstancedBuffers` asks the Scene's optional `_instanceUploads` (`kept`) before it fills and uploads a batch's rows, and reports each upload (`uploaded`); unset, every draw uploads |
| `Meshes/instancedMesh.pure.js`, `scene.pure.d.ts` | `_processInstancedBuffers` skips an attribute upload `_instanceUploads.attributesKept` answers for; the Scene declares the hook. Unset, every draw uploads |
| `Meshes/abstractMesh.pure.js`, `scene.pure.d.ts` | `_markSubMeshesAsLightDirty` marks nothing for a mesh the Scene's optional `_lightFreeMesh` hook names. Haptic's scene lighting installs it ([`light-free-meshes.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/light-free-meshes.ts)) for meshes whose every Material (its own or the Scene's default, and each render pass's) is a NodeMaterial without a light-reading Babylon block (`LightBlock`, `PBRMetallicRoughnessBlock`, `LightInformationBlock`): upstream marks every draw's defines of each mesh a light joins or leaves, and each then prepares its defines and asks for its Effect again, which the light budget caused every few frames as the camera moved. `lightSources` is still kept. Scene lighting's own `sceneLighting` readiness mark names only the meshes the hook does not (`scene-lighting.ts`): a light-free mesh's readiness stands. Without the hook nothing changes |
| `Meshes/subMesh.pure.js` | `_removeDrawWrapper` leaves a sub-mesh with no draw wrapper at the pass as it is, and drops the trailing empty slots after a removal. Upstream writes an empty slot at the id into every sub-mesh of every Scene on each `releaseRenderPassId`; ids only count up, so each pass a frame graph, preview or thumbnail creates and releases grew every sub-mesh's array, and every walk of it (`_markSubMeshesAsDirty`), for the session |
| `Misc/khronosTextureContainer2.js` | A texture disposed while its data decodes is not uploaded (its bind binds nothing, so WebGL logged `texImage2D: no texture bound`), as the engine's own `_prepareWebGLTexture` skips it |
| `Particles/gpuParticleSystem.pure.*` | `paused`: draw last buffers without simulating |
| `Shaders/particles.vertex.js` | Animation sheet UVs flip V after Babylon's cell maths, so a Texture uploaded with invertY (Haptic's default) shows cell 0 at the top left and each cell upright |
| `scene.pure.*` | `render(…, ignoreActions)` for render-only frames |
