- Replacing a drawn mesh's vertices (`setVerticesData`, `VertexData.applyToMesh`, `updateVerticesData` with `updateExtends`) makes Babylon build its bounds anew at the origin; only the next world matrix it computes moves them to the mesh, and it computes none for a frozen matrix (a Play slot's root between the snapshots that move it) or, after `updateVerticesData`, for an unchanged one. Until then culling and picking read bounds at the origin. Whatever replaces vertices in place calls `refreshBoundingInfo` after it: `setSpriteQuadSize` ([`sprite-quad.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/sprite-quad.ts)), `updatePainter2DMesh` ([`painter2d-mesh.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/painter2d-mesh.ts)), `updateLandscapeMesh` ([`landscape-mesh.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/landscape-mesh.ts), whose editor caller also re-applies the actor's transform) and a 2D Text's redraw (`replaceVertexData`, [`text2d-mesh.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/text2d-mesh.ts), for a frozen mesh: `applyToMesh` recomputes the matrix of any other).
- The unlit Material replaces `NodeMaterial._prepareDefinesForAttributes` on its instances: it pins NORMAL, TANGENT and UV2 to UV6 off and UV1 to texturing, so meshes of different vertex layouts share its program, and repeats Babylon's geometry-pass define preparation. Re-read the original when the pin moves.
- `full-target-clear` replaces each full-screen pass's execute function after each build (reading `FrameGraphPass._executeFunc`) and calls `FrameGraphRenderContext._applyRenderTarget`. It trusts that `FrameGraphPostProcessTask` (the bloom blur's stages and Display Color included) and the copy tasks draw through `applyFullScreenEffect` at the full viewport, honouring `alphaMode`, `viewport`, `stencilState`, `disableColorWrite` and `depthTest` as 9.29 does, and that `Effect._fragmentSourceCode` holds the processed fragment source; re-read them on a pin move.
- Runtime member replacements (restore the original only while their wrapper is installed): `shadow-atlas` (`CascadedShadowGenerator.IsSupported`, scoped to building the sun's atlas cascades, restored in a `finally`: Babylon's constructor asks `EngineStore.LastCreatedEngine`, which a model conversion's NullEngine (OBJ, FBX, STL) is while it runs, and a rejected constructor throws, failing the sun's shadow until a settings change; the replacement asks the light's Engine), `framegraph-forward-scene` (cull-task target, depth retain on borrowed targets, record abort, camera-update pin), `framegraph-managed-shadows` (`ObjectRenderer.render` intermediate flag), `registered-view-admission` (`_renderViewStep` / `_renderViews` / `setSize`), `node-material-particles` (`_createEffectForParticles`), `owned-post-process` (`Effect._isReadyInternal`), `shared-outline` (`_processInstancedBuffers`), `overlay-visual-style`, `particle-graph-realize`, `particle-preview`, `environment-irradiance`, `variant-first-draws` (`createEffect`, a cold Effect's own `isReady`), and floating-origin `getScene` swaps for manual draws.
- Babylon observes a list an object renderer draws (`ObjectRenderer.renderList` wraps its `push`, `splice`, `pop`, `shift` and `unshift`; `_renderListHasChanged`): a call that empties the list, or adds to an empty one, marks every Scene mesh's lights dirty, once per renderer drawing it, because shadow defines read whether a shadow map's list is empty. Lists rewritten per frame (the cull task's world and reveal lists, `ViewOcclusion`'s own list, the outline occluders) therefore go through [`observed-render-list.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/observed-render-list.ts): written by index and length, which Babylon does not observe, with only an empty list's first entry pushed. Never clear and refill such a list; re-read `_renderListHasChanged` and `Misc/arrayTools.js` on a pin move.
- Draw batching ([Draw batching](#draw-batching)) replaces the static `RenderingGroup._RenderSorted` once a Scene batches (every other Scene, a blended list and frozen active meshes take the original) and repeats its sort, in place when the list fills its array (a list of one is handed over unsorted and uncopied). The batcher repeats `Mesh.render`'s gate for a regular mesh and its `_isActiveIntermediate` / `_isActive` reset for members it draws, draws a batch with `Mesh.render(subMesh, alpha, effectiveMeshReplacement)` so the mesh draws itself, and writes each member's world matrix as `OffsetWorldToRef` and the 32-bit upload give it (mirrored as `Mesh.render` decides it, a negative `determinant()`); it reads `_userInstancedBuffersStorage`, `_occlusionDataStorage` and `_instanceDataStorage.isFrozen`. A batch's mesh, frozen at the identity, takes its members' non-uniform scaling state (`_updateNonUniformScalingState`) so that it compiles the program they do, and starts its submesh's defines anew when the state flips (`SubMesh.resetDrawCache`), as `TransformBlock` keeps `NONUNIFORMSCALING` once set. Re-read those on a pin move.
- A Scene Layer's retained draw list (`layer-draw-list.ts`) replaces its objects pass's `ObjectRenderer._prepareRenderingManager` on the instance (a retained frame: `RenderingManager.reset`, the active mesh and bounding box lists reset, then the retained submeshes pushed into the groups' `_opaqueSubMeshes`, `_alphaTestSubMeshes` and `_transparentSubMeshes` with `_empty` cleared), and repeats what Babylon 9.29's mesh loop, `FrameGraphCullObjectsTask` and `_prepareRenderingManager` with `RenderingGroup.dispatch` answer for a plain mesh: `isReady`, `isBlocked`, `isEnabled`, zero scaling, `isVisible`, `visibility`, layer masks, `alwaysSelectAsActiveMesh`, `isInFrustum(camera._frustumPlanes)`, `needAlphaBlendingForMesh` / `needAlphaTestingForMesh` / `needDepthPrePass`, and the groups' `_opaqueSortCompareFn`, `_alphaTestSortCompareFn` and `_transparentSortCompareFn`. It relies on preparation doing nothing else a plain mesh's draw needs (`_activate` sets `_renderId` alone; `_preActivateForIntermediateRendering`, `_postActivate` and `_prepareSkeleton` touch instances, edges and skeletons, which fall back). Re-read those on a pin move.
- `instance-pool` moves a member between group sources by assigning `InstancedMesh._sourceMesh` around `Mesh.removeInstance` and `addInstance`, then `_syncSubMeshes`, and forces a frozen source's `_updateNonUniformScalingState(true)`; `instance-pool.test.ts` checks those members exist.
- `apps/editor/vite-unused-babylon-modules.ts` names the Babylon module paths the editor and player builds leave out (`UNUSED_BABYLON_MODULE`); re-derive them on a pin move.
- Re-check [registrations](#babylon-modules-and-registrations) on a pin move: compare what a build registers (prototype members, `ShaderStore` keys, registered class names) with a build that imports the root. A class module that stops registering a side effect, or a new stub, fails silently.
- Re-inventory internals first: `rg '\._[a-zA-Z]' packages/render/src apps/editor/src` plus member replacements. Access through casts or string names is invisible to `tsc`; read the new Babylon source.
- `ForwardSceneFrameGraph.graphReady` re-implements `FrameGraph.isReady` (the private render context's `_isReady()`, then each task in order) (it throws `Unsupported Babylon frame graph readiness layout` without it), and `requestCompilesAhead` and `readinessScanMeshes` repeat `ObjectRenderer._checkReadiness`'s mesh filter and default list; re-read them on a pin move.
- The render change journal (`render-change-journal.ts`) wraps `TransformNode` and `AbstractMesh` `_afterComputeWorldMatrix`, `Node._syncParentEnabledState`, the `Node` `isVisible` and `inheritVisibility` setters, the `AbstractMesh` `visibility` setter and `_setMaterial`, and `Geometry` `_notifyUpdate`, `applyToMesh`, `releaseForMesh`, `setVerticesBuffer`, `removeVerticesData`, `setIndices` and `setIndexBuffer` (the last four count `geometryBufferLayout`: they must stay the only writers of `_vertexBuffers` and `_indexBufferIsUpdatable`). `canCacheShadowMaterialFor` trusts that PBR, Standard and Node Materials keep Material's own `needAlphaBlendingForMesh` and `needAlphaTestingForMesh`, which read only a mesh's `visibility` and `hasVertexAlpha`. It trusts that every overriding `_afterComputeWorldMatrix` either is one of those two or calls super, that `setEnabled` and the `parent` setter run `_syncParentEnabledState` and that it recurses into children, that `isVisible` reads `_nodeDataStorage._isVisible` and its inheriting parent, and that an instance's `material` and `visibility` read its source. A pin move re-reads those methods and the classes that redefine them (`journalFollows`); the shadow walk's verify mode (`HAPTIC_PERF_VERIFY_JOURNAL`) reports what still slips past. It also wraps `AbstractMesh.setMaterialForRenderPass` (`Material`), the `AbstractMesh` `renderingGroupId`, `layerMask` and `hasVertexAlpha` setters and `Mesh.addLODLevel` and `removeLODLevel` (`Dispatch`), trusting that those setters are their fields' only writers after construction and that an instance's `renderingGroupId` reads its source's.
- The lean preparation (`lean-render-preparation.ts`) re-implements `ObjectRenderer._prepareRenderingManager`, `RenderingManager.dispatch` and `RenderingGroup.dispatch` (with the patched `_registerInstanceForRenderId` reached through `_activate`). It trusts that `Mesh.isReady(false)` reads only `delayLoadState`, `Node.isReady` (`_nodeDataStorage._isReady`) and the sub-mesh list, and `InstancedMesh.isReady` its source's; that `InstancedMesh.getLOD` without source levels stores and returns the source; that `SubMesh.getMaterial` resolves `_renderingMesh.getMaterialForRenderPass(pass) ?? material`, a MultiMaterial slot or the default material; that Material's own `needAlphaBlendingForMesh` and `needAlphaTestingForMesh` read only a mesh's `visibility` and `hasVertexAlpha`; and that a rendering manager keeps its groups. A pin move re-diffs those functions against the pinned source and runs a Play lap with `HAPTIC_PERF_VERIFY_PREPARATION`.
- Held atlas pages (`shadow-atlas.ts`, `AtlasTileTarget`) trust that `RenderTargetTexture._render` binds and unbinds a pass only through `_bindFrameBuffer` and `_unbindFrameBuffer` (the unbind's callback notifying `onAfterRenderObservable`), that `ThinEngine.bindFramebuffer` and `unBindFramebuffer` reach GL only through `_bindUnboundFramebuffer`, which compares `_currentFramebuffer`, and that an unbind does nothing else for a depth-only, mip-less, non-MSAA target than clear `_currentRenderTarget` and `_integerMRTAttachmentsMask`.
- Layout guards that throw `Unsupported Babylon … layout` (`node-material-particles`, `particle-system-factory`) must be re-derived, never relaxed.
- `styleEditorGizmos` trusts Babylon's gizmos to keep their idle, hover and disabled materials in `_coloredMaterial`, `_hoverMaterial` and `_disableMaterial`, to read them from there (and from the parent's `_gizmoAxisCache` entries) at each swap, and to copy `material.diffuseColor` to line meshes; re-read the gizmo sources on a pin move.
- Shadow handoffs (`shadow-receiver-handoff`) trust that `PrepareDefinesForLights` and `BindLights` index lights by `mesh.lightSources` position, that `_resyncLightSource` only splices or appends when membership disagrees with `isEnabled() && canAffectMesh`, and that `receiverSignature` lists every per-light input of `PrepareDefinesForLight` besides the shadow generator; re-read those on a pin move.
- The starting-state draw (`starting-state-draw`) trusts that an Effect keeps its cache entry and program while its `_refCount` is above zero (`Effect.dispose` decrements it, and `DrawWrapper.dispose`, which a released render pass id triggers for every submesh, disposes its Effect at the end of the frame), and that `SubMesh._drawWrappers` holds every pass's wrapper.
- The spawnable warm-up (`spawnable-warm`) trusts that `Material.forceCompilation` keeps its temporary submesh's Effect referenced, so the Effect outlives the staged mesh; a pin that disposes it would leave warmed variants to recompile at spawn. Re-check on a pin move.
- Variant swaps (`scene-perf`) trust that Standard, PBR and NodeMaterial `isReadyForSubMesh` keep the previous Effect when `allowShaderHotSwapping` is set and the new one is not ready, call `markAsUnprocessed` (so `materialDefines.isDirty` marks a swap) and return the previous Effect's readiness, except after `_areLightsDisposed`; and that NodeMaterial sets `_wasPreviouslyReady` after a swap (the per-frame re-probe exists for that). Re-read them on a pin move.
- `SlateTextureQuality` registers through `registerPersistentMaterialPlugin` ([`material-plugin-registration.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/material-plugin-registration.ts)) because Babylon unregisters global plugins when the last Engine disposes.
- Rectangular area lights register their textured-emission define when a slot changes light type ([`area-rect-light.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/area-rect-light.ts)); app-owned light blocks refresh dynamic samplers likewise. Authored forward is +Z, native emission −Z.
- `LogicalGeometryTask` clears view depth with its own target rebound (Babylon's base clear ends on the backbuffer, an MRT `INVALID_OPERATION`). Borrowed shadow/cluster draws restore caller state through [`framegraph-borrowed-draw.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/framegraph-borrowed-draw.ts) / [`engine-drawing-state.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/engine-drawing-state.ts) because 9.29 draws lack `finally`. [`allocation-checkpoint.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/allocation-checkpoint.ts) is the only reader of the private render-target wrapper cache.
- `FrameGraphBloomTask.record` resolves its merge output from the source's creation options without resetting `samples` (`FrameGraphPostProcessTask` resets them), so `SceneEffectsGraph` hands it a single-sample `targetTexture` over the multisampled scene colour; re-check on a pin move whether that is still needed.
- Never delete a WebGL program that still links: the browser goes on asking it for its link status, which leaves an `INVALID_VALUE` on the context for each ask (`glGetProgramiv: Program object expected`), read back as the pre-existing errors of the next presentation. An owner that can be released right after it was built keeps such an Effect until its compile settles ([`owned-effect-retirement.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/owned-effect-retirement.ts): `retireOwnedEffect` for one Effect, `disposeKeepingLinkingEffects` for an owner Babylon disposes as a whole). `SceneEffectsGraph.disposeTasks` does so for its chain's tasks (`chainTaskEffects`, which reads the bloom blur's three stages from `FrameGraphBloomBlurTask.effects`): a Play load's first graphs are released within a second when the game's first commands (the active camera's post-process stack, a quality change) invalidate their preparation.
- NullEngine test hosts need the `clearAttachments` adapter in `framegraph-test-fixtures.ts`; NullEngine never proves GPU output.
- `.hdr` environment import ([`hdr-environment.ts`](../../../packages/assets/src/hdr-environment.ts)) reproduces, on the CPU, `HDRCubeTexture`/`PanoramaToCubeMapTools` orientation, `HDRFiltering` roughness per mip (lod scale 0.8, `hdrFilteringFunctions` GGX and `K = 4`) and the DDS loader's face-major mip layout. Re-read those and `CubeTexture`'s `DefaultLodScale` on a pin move; already imported cubes keep their filtering.
- Draw calls come from `engine._drawCalls` reset at the start of each view's frame (Babylon 9 has no public count, and NullEngine's empty draw methods never report one); the per-pass split reads the same counter, so re-check `_reportDrawCall`'s call sites on a pin move.
- **Projected decals** ([`decal-pass.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/decal-pass.ts), [`decal-projection-block.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/decal-projection-block.ts)): every decal draws a Decal-domain Material ([shader-graph.md](shader-graph.md#compilation)). `SceneDecalHost` tracks authored `DecalComponent` entries and pooled dynamic decals and resolves their Materials through a `DecalMaterialSource` (`setDecalMaterialSource`; `create-engine.ts` hands it `binding.resolveMaterial`, so the `MaterialLibrary` owns compiles, generations and refcounts). A component's own values (`DecalBinding.materialInstance`) resolve a private copy under the instance key `decal:<actor>:<component>:<n>`, kept while the values stay the same and released (`release`) when they change or the decal goes; it shares its root's program. Only a `NodeMaterial` whose `metadata.materialDomain` is `decal` draws; any other Material, or none, leaves the decal undrawn. Batches key on `(sortOrder, Material, mirrored)`, so decals of one Material draw in one thin-instanced draw whatever their Tint and Opacity, which travel as instance rows (`decalTintOpacity`, `decalParams.y` = cos Fade Angle) beside the world and inverse-world rows.
  - **Draw slots**: one unit cube per Material (outside `scene.meshes`, `receiveShadows` on) plus the pass's own instanced `VertexBuffer`s (`DECAL_INSTANCE_ATTRIBUTES`: world rows `decalWorld0..3`, inverse rows, Tint/Opacity, params), updated in place and bound with the cube's vertices through `engine.bindBuffers` (no cached vertex array object), divisors cleared after the draw (`unbindInstanceAttributes`). Babylon's thin-instance `matrix` streams read zero in this program. The pass calls `context._applyRenderTarget()` first: a frame graph pass binds its target lazily for its own helpers, and raw engine draws otherwise land on the default framebuffer. Its own submesh keeps that Material's Effect, so defines never thrash between batches. A batch draws only when `isReadyForSubMesh(cube, subMesh, true)` and its Effect are ready: the pass enables the Effect, culls front faces (`reverseSide` for a mirrored world), binds the cube's buffers, `resetCachedMaterial` then `bindForSubMesh` (a frozen Material would skip its bindings when the previous draw shared its Effect), sets the pass uniforms (`DECAL_PASS_UNIFORMS`) and the resolved depth (`decalSceneDepth`), and blends by the Material's `alphaMode` with depth test and write off. A batch whose program is not ready is skipped (`SceneDecalStats.pending`, `sceneDecalsPending`): a compiling Effect calls the pending listener once it compiles, one not yet created calls it every frame; `create-engine.ts` turns it into `scheduler.invalidate("asset")`. Previews and thumbnails wait for no pending decal before they present.
  - **Materials change**: the host asks its source again on `refreshDecalMaterials` (Material documents or texture bytes installed, a generation published through `onMaterialReady`); a disposed generation re-resolves once per refresh, never per frame (`resolve` serializes the document).
  - **Warm-up** (`warmDecalMaterials`): Play's load (`warmPlaySpawnables`) compiles every Decal-domain Material document on its own draw slot with instancing on, as the pass draws it, within the warm-up bound, then the next pass draws each once with a zero world (no pixel covered) for drivers that link at a program's first draw. A warm-up also sets the sticky dynamic demand, so a later Spawn Decal never rebuilds the forward graph.
  - **Graph demand** (`sceneHasDecals`): authored decals, or any Spawn Decal (or warm-up) since the host's last `clear` (Play `changescene`, Scene dispose). Dynamic demand is sticky so isolated spawns never rebuild the forward graph on spawn and again on expiry; with none visible the split leaves the decal and transparent tasks disabled and draws nothing extra. While true, `ForwardSceneFrameGraph` allocates `ownScene` if needed for sampleable depth, enables `resolveMSAADepth` under MSAA, and executes `Forward objects` (opaque + alpha-test) → `FrameGraphDecalTask` → (`WaterSceneCopyTask`) → `Forward transparent`; while false, no split or extra target exists.
  - **Dynamic pool**: live decals only, oldest first; expiry removes an entry at once, a spawn into a full pool evicts the front (oldest live, whatever its lifetime), and a capacity drop keeps the newest. Capacity (`maxDynamicDecalsForScene`) keys on the resolved Geometry tier (`profile`, else the tier its values match, else Medium, as Auto local lights do): 64 on Low/Medium, 128 on High/Ultra.
  - **Lifetime clock**: `prepare` ages the pool at most once per `engine.frameId` (a Render Target Capture or second view prepares in the same frame) by `engine.getDeltaTime()` × the simulation time scale, and not while paused: Play's `applyPause` calls `setDecalTimePaused(paused || held)` and `setSimulationTimeScale` calls `setDecalTimeScale`, exactly as for `ParticleService`.
  - **Large World Rendering**: instance world rows lose the eye (`scene.floatingOriginOffset`) from their translation, inverse rows take it back (`eye · inv rows 0..2 + inv row 3`, in 64 bits), and the view is `T(eye) · view`, so the depth reconstruction, OBB test, `HapticLights`/clusters/shadow views and fog all work eye-relative. The pass's uniforms (`DECAL_PASS_UNIFORMS`: `decalView`, `decalViewProjection`, `decalInvViewProjection`, `decalCameraPosition`) are its own because Babylon's floating-origin `setMatrix` override rewrites `view`/`viewProjection` by name from the Scene's camera. With the mode off every upload is the camera's own, unchanged.
  - **Projection** (`DecalProjectionBlock`, added by the compiler to every Decal Material): the vertex stage draws the box from the thin-instance world rows; the fragment reads the resolved depth, rebuilds the surface point (eye-relative), discards outside the box, and gives the graph World Position, the geometric normal oriented towards the near-plane point of its pixel (perspective or orthographic), the projected UV (local X and Z, 0 to 1), a tangent along local X, Camera Position, View Direction and Object Position (the decal's centre). `facing = dot(N, local +Y)` and Fade Angle (1–180°, 180 never fades) fade it, with the box's projection ends. Front-face culling draws the OBB's far faces so entering the volume never near-clips it; a mirrored world (negative determinant) flips the winding, so its batch passes `reverseSide`. Textures sample through the Material's own Texture blocks, which apply each texture's matrix (`ResourceCache` flips KTX2 V there). Lighting is `HapticLightingBlock` with the cube as receiver. The projection is written inline in `main` with no helper function, and the fog helpers it shares with surfaces (`sceneFogKeep`, `CalcFogFactor`) keep one exit (`decal-projection-block.test.ts`): an early `return` in a decal helper made linking the lit program crash Chromium's GPU process under ANGLE Direct3D 11, which loses the WebGL context; software GL and Metal do not take that path.
  - Not drawn in mirrors, reflection probes or planar reflections.
- **Foliage distance culling, shadow cutoff, dither fade and per-cell LODs** ([`foliage-mesh.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/foliage-mesh.ts), [`foliage-fade.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/foliage-fade.ts)): each foliage root's batches split into 32 m spatial cells, one thin-instance mesh each, whose batch distances (`cullDistance`, `fadeRange`, `shadowDistance`, scaled by `lodDistanceScale`) `updateFoliageDistances` evaluates from the frame's main camera in `onBeforeRender`, only when that camera moved more than 0.5 m, quality changed or a cell or distance changed. A cell beyond its cull distance is disabled (`setEnabled`; actor `isVisible` stays authored); one beyond its shadow distance leaves every shadow map through `setShadowDistanceCulled` ([`shadow-caster-list.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shadow-caster-list.ts)), which notes the caster in the render change journal (`RenderChange.Other`): the caster walk reads it into the caster's snapshot (`shadowDistance` in `CASTER_VALUES`), so exactly the tiles it reaches redraw that frame, without marking readiness. A cell in the fade band binds `hapticFoliageFade` per draw: a 4 × 4 Bayer screen-door discard on PBR and Standard Materials (`FoliageFadePlugin.hardBindForSubMesh`, allowlisted in `shadow-material-policy.ts`), measured from `hapticFoliageCamera`, Babylon's bound eye less `floatingOriginOffset` (`foliageFadeEye`), the space of `vPositionW` with Large World Rendering on or off; and on compiled Surface Materials (`GeometrySurfaceOutputBlock`), measured in view space. Both fade in colour and geometry passes and in neither shadow depth (Babylon's depth shader, the authored `shadowDepth` variant), outline masks nor depth/normal captures. LOD levels attach asynchronously from `acquireGlbContainer().lods()`, sharing the cell's vertex and `world0..3` buffers; an attach failure warns once per Scene and Model. Each cell has its own Geometry (thin-instance attributes live there), but every cell's Geometry for one primitive or level draws one GPU index buffer (`applyWithSharedIndices`, [`shared-index-buffer.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/shared-index-buffer.ts)), found per Engine by the CPU index array and counted by the Engine's buffer references, so it goes with the last Geometry holding it; a level's buffer is made at the first swap to it. A restored context rebuilds only Geometries that meshes draw, each with its own buffer, so the foliage state forgets the lost buffer of every level a cell does not draw. Editor views (`EditorSceneSync` calls `showEveryFoliageCell`) never cull, shadow-cull or fade, so every cell draws and picks; LOD swaps stay. The distances are excluded from `foliagePropertiesFingerprint`: a distance edit (`isFoliageDistanceOnlySceneEdit`, an editor fast path) applies in place (`applyFoliageDistances`) without rebuilding. Decided: cells are evaluated once per frame for the main camera; Mirrors, Reflection Probes and Render Target Captures draw that camera's result (no per-view preparation hook exists, and `setEnabled` is global to the mesh).
- **Foliage instance storage** ([`foliage.ts`](../../../packages/core/src/foliage.ts), [`foliage-chunks.ts`](../../../packages/assets/src/foliage-chunks.ts)): a Foliage component's JSON holds its group and batch infos (Model, Material Override, distances); its instances are one binary `HPFG` chunk: magic, `u16 version` (1), `u16 batchCount`, `u32` instance count per batch, then little-endian floats, `FOLIAGE_FLOATS_PER_INSTANCE` (10: position, rotation quaternion, scale) per instance. Editor documents carry it as base64 `data` (undo, journal, copy and paste); a saved Scene `.hasset` stores the raw chunk `foliage:<actorId>/<componentId>` named by `chunkId` (actor-qualified because every instance of a Prefab carries its component ids; `extractFoliageChunks` on save, orphans dropped with the landscape chunks; `hydrateFoliageChunksToEditor` on load). Prefab and Class documents keep their Foliage inline (`data`), so synced instances and runtime spawns never need another asset's chunk. Play and Export carry a Scene's raw chunks beside it (`collectFoliageChunksForPlay`; the `sceneComponentChunks` map keyed `<sceneGuid>:<chunkId>` is shared with landscape; export sidecars of type `Foliage` with guid `foliage:<sceneGuid>:<chunkId>` and `ownerGuid` the Scene), and the runtime decodes Landscape and Foliage data once per actor at realization (`hydrateActorComponentChunks`, every creation path), looking only under the Scene document the actor came from (a streamed instance's own Scene before the played one). Instances that do not decode or do not match the batch list parse as `unreadable` with no batches: loading reports a project diagnostic once (`unreadableComponentChunks`, shared with landscape) and keeps the `chunkId` (the save carries the stored chunk unchanged), saving keeps unreadable `data` inline, the erase brush skips the component, and the renderer and runtime warn. `parseFoliageProperties` returns `Float32Array` views (no object per instance), cached per properties object, base64 string and chunk; `createFoliageMesh` composes cell matrices straight from those floats. Visual fingerprints name instances by `foliageInstancesFingerprint` (a hash of `data`, the `chunkId`, or the floats), never by serializing them.
- **Landscape per-chunk LODs, seam skirts, exact picks and in-place sculpting** ([`landscape-mesh.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/landscape-mesh.ts), [`scene-brush-input.ts`](../../../packages/render/src/scene-brush-input.ts)): each landscape splits into chunks of up to 32 × 32 cells (`CHUNK_CELLS`) backed by pre-allocated `Float32Array` vertex buffers (`position`, `normal`, `uv`, `color`) mutated in place via `updateVerticesData` + `refreshBoundingInfo` (zero per-vertex allocation during sculpt drags). One `Uint16Array` index array concatenating `[LOD0 | LOD1 | LOD2 | LOD3]` (cell steps 1, 2, 4, 8) is built on the CPU per chunk shape and skirt set; each chunk's geometry uploads its own GPU copy, and LOD switches only mutate `subMesh.indexStart` and `subMesh.indexCount` in place (no index or vertex uploads, no `geometryBufferLayout` change, no readiness invalidation). Every LOD, LOD0 included, draws an outward-wound downward skirt on each edge shared with another chunk and none on the landscape's outer border; a chunk's skirt depth is twice the largest gap between its skirted edges drawn at a coarser LOD and at full resolution, plus half a cell, because a neighbour shares the edge's samples and the higher of two meeting edges must reach the lower one (a valley or a ridge along a seam). The depth is the same at every LOD, so it changes only when the heights do. `updateLandscapeLods(scene, camera)` selects per-chunk LODs from the camera's distance to each chunk's world bounding sphere against `LANDSCAPE_LOD_DISTANCES` (`[45, 110, 240]` × `lodDistanceScale`, 5% hysteresis), re-evaluating only after the camera moves 0.5 m or the settings or heights change; when `autoLod` is `false` (or no camera exists), chunks pin to LOD0. Each chunk mesh's `intersects` is replaced by `intersectLandscapeChunk`, an exact trace of the full-resolution cells under the ray's segment (drawn Float32 vertex values, the mesh's `(1,0)–(0,1)` split, LOD0 face ids so `PickingInfo.getNormal` works), so scene picks, the brush and its footprint ring, foliage painting, surface placement and foot placement meet the LOD0 surface at any drawn LOD and never a skirt. During a sculpt drag, `scene-brush-input.ts` dabs a `LandscapeSculptSession` and once per pointer event calls `updateLandscapeMesh(root, session.liveProperties(), undefined, { dirtyRegion: session.takeDirtyRegion() })`: only chunks within one sample of the changed samples (their normals read them) refill and upload, skipping `sync.apply` and base64 encoding; pointer-up commits one `HPLD`-encoded property update.
